# KB: Bug-fix invariants (incident essays)

Moved VERBATIM out of CLAUDE.md (tier-2 pass).

## THE PLAN WEEK READ THE MODEL BUCKET (inc-mubu23bd-5vxi, 2.369.138, owner 2026-09-21 15:44 PDT: "刚才7d用量红了，刷新之后变成绿的了")

**What the owner saw.** The taskbar's 7d donut went red; a ⟳ turned it green; the next few seconds turned it red again. Two producers were writing two different numbers into the same field.

**The measurement (already established, not repeated).** claude 2.1.274's `rate_limit_event` schema, verbatim from the binary: `rate_limit_info { status: allowed|allowed_warning|rejected, resetsAt, rateLimitType: five_hour|seven_day|seven_day_opus|seven_day_sonnet|seven_day_overage_included|overage, utilization, unifiedWindows: { five_hour, seven_day, seven_day_overage_included } }`, described by the binary itself as "Per-window usage for the session (5-hour), weekly (7-day), and overage-included weekly (per-model bucket; present only for accounts whose responses carry that window) subscription rate-limit windows, as read from the anthropic-ratelimit-unified-* response headers … Unlike the top-level status/utilization fields, which describe the currently limiting window, both windows are tracked on every observation, and events are emitted when a window's rounded percentage or reset time moves, not only on status transitions." The top-level type/utilization is the REPRESENTATIVE claim (the `anthropic-ratelimit-unified-representative-claim` header). The corpus on this box — 12 live session buffers, the latest event of each, both weekly windows sharing one reset — reads `seven_day` vs `seven_day_overage_included` = 0.15/0.29, 0.11/0.22, 0.13/0.25, 0.26/0.51, 0.42/0.85, 0.37/0.74, 0.43/0.87, 0.29/0.59: always TWO numbers, the bucket about twice the plan. The owner's own session read `rateLimitType seven_day_overage_included, utilization 0.87, unifiedWindows.seven_day 0.43, unifiedWindows.seven_day_overage_included 0.87`, and the isolated, identity-verified /usage panel of the same account at 22:44:28Z said plan 7d 43 %, 5h 6 %, scoped weekly Fable 86 %. So `seven_day_overage_included` IS the account's per-MODEL weekly bucket (Fable on these Max accounts), not the plan week.

**The defect.** `parseRateLimitEvent` mapped `seven_day_overage_included` to kind `sevenDay` (2.361.2, "the accounting the CLI enforces") and never read `unifiedWindows`, so the plan 7d of the owner's account was written 86 % (source rate-limit-event, 22:44:55Z) right after the panel had written 43 %; the taskbar, the pool's plan-7d verdicts, the 15 % reserve floor and the estimator's anchors were all fed the bucket's number on every account with a model cap, wherever the representative claim was the bucket, since 2.361.2. `src/record-shape.js` had declared `unifiedWindows` since 2.369.126; nothing read it.

**The rule (the lane is decided by what NAMES it — B-ccaa's invariant, now with a producer that names every lane).** One `rate_limit_event` carrying `unifiedWindows` yields up to THREE readings for the credential slot: `five_hour` → the plan 5h lane, `seven_day` → the plan 7d lane, `seven_day_overage_included` → the account's model-cap weekly lane (scope 'model'). The representative status (allowed_warning / rejected) and top-level utilization attach to the lane its `rateLimitType` names and to no other (`seven_day_overage_included` = the model-cap lane, never the plan; `seven_day` = plan; `five_hour` = 5h; `seven_day_<model>` = that model); a non-representative window carries the status its own number implies. Without `unifiedWindows` (older CLIs) an overage-included event still goes to the model-cap lane — the 2.361.2 rejection stays marked dead on the CAP, the plan untouched. Naming the cap: the account's `.window-<key>` sidecar `scoped` map (api-phase verified) names it by family when its reset equals the window's; an existing scope:'model' limit with the same reset is the same lane; the requesting session's family breaks a tie; when nothing names it, it is a scoped limit with family null / name 'Model cap' / limitId `model:cap` that the next panel or banner MERGES into the named one (same reset, `foldPlaceholderCap`, the one permitted collapse) — never dropped, never written into the plan. The engine names the lane BEFORE any signal reads the kind (and re-asks on the key the guard lands on), hands the window guard every named lane the record states, feeds the witness ring the plan 7d reset, and no longer defers the type (it marks the cap on arrival; the same turn's banner marks the same lane). The statusline twin: 2.1.274's statusline never carries the type — it ships the bucket NAMED as `rate_limits.model_scoped[{display_name, utilization (the server's percent), resets_at (ISO)}]` — so `data/bin/vibespace-usage` writes each entry as the scoped bucket of that name and drops the model limits it measured as it drops the plan one; `liftCacheObject` restores a model cap from a legacy entry whose limit is absent. A one-shot repair (`2026-09-weekly-lanes-unfold`) re-derives every plan week last written by an event from the newest windowed event in the buffers linked to that slot, never touching a panel-sourced value.

**The repair (bounded, one-shot — `2026-09-weekly-lanes-unfold`, src/weekly-lanes-unfold.js).** Only a plan limit whose `source` is `rate-limit-event` can still hold the bucket (every other producer states both plan windows and corrects the week itself), so those are the candidates and a panel-sourced week is never touched; the evidence is the newest non-rejected windowed event in the live buffers linked to that credential slot (per-session pool link → pool default → own subscription), accepted only when its plan reset is the account's own established window (±120 s), archived before the rewrite, written through `captureRateLimitEvent` (the plan 7d, the cap named by the sidecar, the fold), the 5-hour lane left out, the clock never regressing `fetchedAt`; a key with no evidence is left, with the reason, for the next panel or event. Measured on the incident fixture: plan 7d 86 → 43, Fable 87 named, the first `/api/usage` of the upgrade boot draws 0.43.

**Invariant.** A stream reading names its lane through the producer's per-window fields; the representative claim's TYPE alone never chooses the plan lane. A lane nothing names is kept as a scoped placeholder that folds into the named lane, never promoted to the plan and never dropped.

**Gates.** test-rate-limit-capture (the owner's record verbatim, the no-unifiedWindows and seven_day_opus controls, the tree census over every file that spells the type), test-quota-model ⑲ (the naming rungs, the one permitted fold, the lift rung, the REAL statusline tool with a `model_scoped` payload), test-fable-cap-pool-storm §2b/§15 (the type marks the cap on arrival, never deferred, never the plan; the PRE-FIX control re-maps it to the plan lane and reproduces two demotions for one wall), test-migrations (the repair: the incident fixture on disk with its pool links and buffer tail, the panel/orphan/foreign controls, the archive, the notice, idempotency, the in-process `/api/usage` payload), test-restore-smoke (the same fixture seeded into a worktree server's own data/ — the first `/api/usage` of that boot reads 0.43 and the ledger records the run).

**r2 (2026-09-21, the adversarial verify — five findings, each reproduced before the fix).** ① A weekly window is the same lane by PHASE (`weeklyPhaseNear` in quota-model, reading-lag's rule), never by the instant: after every weekly roll, until the next verified panel restamped the sidecar, the cap was un-nameable, a placeholder was written beside the stale named cap, and an un-named cap gates every family — an opus session on that member read 0 % and was moved (test-fable-cap-pool-storm §16 reproduces the parking with the absolute compare). ② The scoped dead mark has the same reset ladder as the plan lanes (stated > stored > the record's weekly > a bounded 24 h): a model-cap rejection stating no reset was marked dead with NO deadline and `bucketRemaining` never rolled it. ③ `captureRateLimitEvent` stamps `scopedFetchedAt` with the reading's clock, and the anchors/estimator take the newer of the entry's and the file's stamp — the stream-written cap was invisible to rate learning while its estimate fed the pool. ④ The placeholder's lane is spelled one way on the event (`scopedName` = the placeholder key) so the wall signal and the fresh-window edge agree; the witness ring refuses that key. ⑤ A refused repair fails the migration (retry next boot) instead of being recorded as applied; the fixtures pin the production 60 s panel-vs-API delta. 不变量: a weekly lane's identity is its phase; every dead mark carries a deadline; a reading's clock is the reading's, never the last panel's; a wall is spelled the same by the signal that records it and the reading that opens it; a repair that did not happen is never recorded as done.

## A TERMINAL SELECTION TOOK THE WRONG LINES UNDER THE UI SCALE (2.369.118, userW inc-mu92zsgw-6c9y: "the login terminal cannot copy text — the lines cross")

**What the bundle said.** The reporter opened Manage Agents, clicked an account's re-login chip (the `claude auth login` terminal), and 5 min later dragged the pointer over the terminal twice (00:30:35–00:30:54, five `ptr` entries on `canvas.xterm-link-layer`, no Cmd+C ever recorded), then filed the report. The wrapper sidecar was frozen; the raw PTY buffer was not (the session was swept before anyone looked). The snapshot could not say whether a UI scale was on.

**The mechanism, measured.** A standalone page with the exact xterm build VibeSpace bundles, driven by REAL mouse events (CDP `Input.dispatchMouseEvent`, not synthetic DOM events): at `document.body.style.zoom = 1.25` — what the "UI scale (DPI)" setting does (`applyUiPrefs`) — a drag at the visual row 20 selects row 25; at 0.8, row 16; at 1, row 20. xterm's `getCoordsRelativeToElement` computes `clientX - rect.left`, which under CSS zoom is in ZOOMED px, and divides by a cell size its `TextMetricsMeasureStrategy` measured in LAYOUT px (`canvas.measureText`). The same defect class as inc-mtdrm922 (the VNC pointer offset, 2.369.5): a canvas-mapped surface under the body zoom.

**The fix** is that incident's rule applied to every xterm container: `.terminal-container` gets `zoom: calc(1 / var(--ui-scale, 1))` (`COUNTER_ZOOM`, now the ONE definition in utils.js, re-exported by vnc-view.js so the calc() is spelled once), and the xterm font size is the VISUAL px × the UI scale (`_xtermPx`) so nothing visible changes — every writer of `options.fontSize` goes through it (the constructor, `applyOverride`, `rescale()` for a scale change, app.js's font-size row through `applyOverride`). Verified on the same page: row 20 at every zoom, screen size within 3 %, and FitAddon's grid unchanged (69 cols; rows ±1 from line-height rounding at fractional font sizes).

**Forensics closed with it:** the incident snapshot carries `uiScale`/`dpr`/`bodyZoom`; the freeze copies the last 64 KB of every open terminal's raw PTY buffer (`frozen/buffers/<id>.tail`) — the screen the user was looking at survives the sweep.

**Invariants.** ① A surface that maps the pointer onto its own pixels (xterm, noVNC) sits at NET zoom 1 under the body DPI zoom; the literal lives in ONE place. ② Every font-size write in terminal.js scales by `uiScale()` — a raw `options.fontSize = n` is 1/scale too small (test-terminal-ui-scale enumerates the writers). ③ A UI-scale change calls `rescale()` (a bare `fit()` keeps the old glyph size). ④ An incident about pointer geometry can only be judged when the snapshot names the scale. Gates: test-terminal-ui-scale (fast) + test-terminal-zoom-select (heavy chrome, pre-fix page vs fixed page).

## "DESKTOP DISCONNECTED" WITH NOTHING IN ANY LOG (2.369.118, userW inc-mu3lmd4s-dmwf)

**What the bundle said.** 2.369.96, server up 32 h, RSS 2 GB, the reporter active in chats until 04:19Z and reporting at 04:26Z; the Desktop window (`openDesktop`) was open. The server console held only large-JSONL warnings between 03:35Z and the capture; the pod log for the window had 9 lines, none about VNC; the ws ring cannot see the picture socket (`/api/vnc` is its own WebSocket); the bundle had no desktop section. Nothing recorded the drop or its cause.

**Why nothing could have.** `bridgeRfb` (src/server/desktop-stream.js) was a pipe: ws ↔ TCP, no ping, no log on open or close. An RFB stream over a static screen carries NO bytes for minutes, and a proxy or load balancer on the way drops a silent WebSocket without telling either end. And the singleton Desktop window was the one caller that did NOT opt into the reconnect ladder ("behaviour byte-identical to the pre-extraction window"), so any single drop stayed "Disconnected" until a click.

**The fix.** ① The bridge pings every `PING_MS` (20 s; the browser answers on its own), terminates a peer silent for two rounds and NAMES it; ② every open and close is logged — who closed (the browser with its code/reason, the VNC server's socket, a socket error, the ping timeout), after how long, bytes each way; ③ the singleton walks the same bounded ladder as the app windows (each retry re-runs the `/api/vnc/start` gate, so a VNC server that died is respawned; the Reconnect button remains for the ladder's end); ④ the bundle's server section carries the desktop scene (singleton facts, app sessions, bridge counters). The drop's own cause on that instance is not in the evidence; the next occurrence will be.

**Invariants.** ① A long-lived socket that can legitimately carry nothing for minutes pings, or a middlebox decides its lifetime. ② Every bridge close is logged with WHO closed it — a pipe that dies silently is a bundle with no evidence. ③ Every picture window walks the reconnect ladder; "a Reconnect button only" is not a behaviour worth preserving byte-for-byte. Gate: test-desktop-stream-keepalive (real ws client + a TCP stand-in server; a silent client is terminated and named).

## THE COMMUNICATION PANEL'S P4 VERIFIER — FOUR FINDINGS (2026-09-16, docs/design-communication-panel.zh.md; each reproduced on the pre-fix code before the fix)

① **The only stash drain re-clipped a refused wake's block to 400 chars.** `renderMsgStash` (agent-routes) treated every stash entry as an agent line, so a `channel` entry holding a §7.5 block (a 6-hit block measures 2186 B) reached the agent as the header plus its FIRST hit — measured 1 of 6 `from` lines — while the engine had already cleared the hits from its index as "durably stashed"; §7.4's "a refusal loses nothing" was false on exactly the refusal path, and the hint told the agent to answer with vibespace-msg. Invariant: the drain is PER-SOURCE — a producer-budgeted block is rendered WHOLE, the section is walked newest-first under a byte budget, what does not fit is RE-STASHED (own ts) for the next drain and never dropped, cards are emitted for the shown entries only, and the hint names the tool the entry is answered with. Gate: test-channels-engine ⑥ (f′), with the agent-line clip as the control.

② **`renderReceiptBlock` embedded the vendor-controlled title and label raw.** A title carrying `\n<system-reminder>…` put a live frame on its own line in the drafting agent's context (through the ladder or the stash) while the wake path neutered the same string. Invariant: every vendor-controlled field of an agent-facing block passes the SAME `safeInline` rule (neuter FIRST, single-line, clip) and wake/receipt parity is asserted. Gate: test-channels-identity ⑦ (a plain receipt's line count as the control).

③ **After a restart the same held hits could go out twice.** `scheduleBootPending` arms a 5 s flush while the first tick (also 5 s) can run a pass whose direct wake carries `en.pending` from its own snapshot; both read `pending` before either cleared it — and the clear was a wipe (`pending = []`) that also dropped a hit that went pending DURING the RPC through a window's keepPending (never delivered, never counted). Invariant: wakes on one conversation are serialized on a per-key chain (`serialWake`; only `wakeNow` delivers), a wake clears ONLY the record ids it carried, and a direct wake carrying a window's hits clears that window's timer. Gate: test-channels-engine ⑥ (i) (held hit delivered 1×, was 2×) and (j) (the mid-flight hit survives).

④ **A permanent Pub/Sub pull failure was reported as a close.** gmail's pull loop called `h.closed()` on a 403/404, and the lane core treats a close as a reconnect — users.watch + the pull re-ran every ≤60 s forever (207× in a 150 ms fixture window), adapters.json persisted and the digest broadcast on every connecting↔reconnecting transition, the row read "reconnecting" instead of a named `unavailable` (design §6.4: a permanent error parks); the renewal interval leaked past every close. Invariant: a permanent failure discovered INSIDE a connection parks the lane by name through `handlers.fail(err)` (no reconnect; the connection closed; epoch bumped), exactly like a permanent connect() throw; `closed` closes the connection it ends; a REFUSED watch renewal parks at once and `WATCH_RENEW_MAX_FAILS` transient misses park; a 5xx pull backs off and retries. Gate: test-channels-push ⑦ (the 503 control: retried, never parked).
## THE HEAVY GATE WAITED 40 MINUTES FOR A SHA NOBODY COULD PUSH, THEN RAN 108 SUITES ONE AT A TIME (2.369.105, owner 2026-09-15 "heavy gate太占用时间了吧 不能优化一下")

Measured on data/ci-heavy/d0e7a8d4: wall 4472 s = 40 min waiting on the machine lock behind a run for d5e3e587 — a sha that had been amended away and could never be pushed — plus 34.5 min of 108 suites strictly sequential (writer-sweep 6.9 min alone, every one of its ~19 whole-box /proc and `lsof +D` walks looking for a holder it had spawned itself). FIX (feat-gate-speed, 13 commits, one adversarial verify + fix): parallel lanes with a longest-first schedule from the previous marker's timings and ONE serial lane for the suites `machineGlobalFixtures` flags or the `SERIAL` table names; the launcher supersedes a lock holder whose sha is neither an ancestor nor reachable from any local or remote-tracking ref; the pre-push hook hands the launcher the pushed RANGE (resolved before the push — after the pack transfer a lone sha's `--not --remotes` is empty) and the affected tier runs only the suites whose inputs that range touches, computed from the tree at the pushed sha, with a full run forced when the newest qualifying full green is older than 24 h; test-writer-sweep substitutes exactly one literal per run copy to walk only its own pids. Measured after: 817 s for 110 suites (4 lanes 564 s + serial 251 s; 2403 s summed). The verifier's six findings each got a control: nine heavy suites load the product through `require(REPO + '/src/…')` and were invisible to the import graph (a census now reddens any heavy row with no product input); the graph was read from the checked-out tree, not the pushed commit; master's 2.369.104 reaper never ran inside the lanes (`reapScratchOrphansAsync`, grace awaited); `fullTierDue` accepted an all-absent or off-line full green; the new-branch range raced the ref update; the abandoned-sha rule ignored `refs/remotes/origin/<x>`. 不变量=**a gate's wall is a measurement, and the lock holder must be provably wanted** (an unreachable sha is abandoned, a remote-tracking ref is still wanted); **an impact graph is read from the commit it judges, never from whatever tree happens to be checked out**; **a range is resolved before the operation that changes the refs it is spelled against**; **a "full green" that judged two suites is not a full green**; **a suite that walks the whole box for its own child is paying for every other lane**. test-ci-gate 224 (§8 lanes + selection census, §9 the reaper wiring) + test-ci-heavy-launch 109 (real detached launches, parked runs, lanes with an orphan stub, `--affected` from another checkout) + test-writer-sweep 239

## GATE SUITES LEFT A DETACHED DEVICE DAEMON PER WORKTREE SERVER — 504 OF THEM (2.369.104, owner 2026-09-16 "我现在系统变得异常缓慢，然后进程树里有大量vibespace相关进程")

Every worktree server a gate suite boots spawns the local device daemon DETACHED (setsid — by design, production's daemon must outlive a server restart), usually with wrapper/child node processes, and every server restart a suite drives leaves `dtach -a` bridge clients behind (KillMode=process keeps the masters). A suite's teardown kills the server it spawned and nothing else; a suite killed by a signal runs no teardown at all. Measured on the dev box: 504 `vibespace-device` processes (34.8 GB RSS, the oldest 76 h), 552 node processes under /tmp/vs-* scratch dirs, 2,137 orphaned `dtach -a` clients, 48 test `claude` CLIs — 86 GB used, load 15, the owner's KVM display blank; the first sweep missed the daemons because `/proc/<pid>/comm` truncates to 15 chars (`vibespace-devic`) — argv[0]'s basename is the name. FIX = `scratchOrphans` in scripts/ci.mjs (PURE over a proc root): a known executable whose cwd / HOME / device root lies under a `/tmp/vs-<name>-<random>` scratch dir is grouped by that root and reaped only when the dir is GONE or every member is UNOWNED (parents walked through fellow members reach systemd/init or a vanished pid) and older than the fixture stale floor; a ci.mjs runner and this process's ancestors are never candidates; production is rooted in a checkout and never matches. Runs after every suite (both tiers), at every heavy launch, and as `node scripts/ci.mjs --reap`. 不变量=**a process a suite starts is the suite's to end, and when the suite structurally cannot (a detached daemon), the gate owns a standing sweep**; **the sweep is keyed on evidence — root + ownership + age — never on a name heuristic** (the first cleanup here was hand-evidenced pid by pid; the reaper encodes exactly that evidence); **a process name read from /proc/comm is a 15-char prefix**. test-ci-gate §8 (fake proc root: gone dir / owned suite tree / stale orphan tree at depth 2 / young orphan / production shape / unlisted name / self-ancestry / the three wiring pins)

## THE ARM CARD NAMED 12PM AND NOT WHAT 12PM WAS (2.369.108, owner 2026-09-17 "明明当前hit limit的账号7am就会reset 5h，但你却提示12pm才能等到自动恢复")

02:30:43 PDT: the owner's conversation on PandyMax hit the 5h wall (the CLI: resets 7am) and the demotion recorded exactly that (`[wall] demoted PandyMax 5h until 2026-09-17T14:00:00.000Z`); the pool verdict then armed for 19:00Z (`walled turn … → blocked until 2026-09-17T19:00:00.000Z`) and the card said `用量已达上限。已安排在 9/17/2026, 12:00:00 PM 重置后自动继续`. The WAIT was right: a member unblocks when the LAST of its dead buckets resets (c1206711 #2), PandyMax's Fable sat at 98 % (2 % left < the 5 % hot floor → dead until its Fable week rolls 9/20), so its own 7am was not a candidate, and the pool's wait is the MIN over members = Member L's Fable reset (12pm). The SENTENCE was wrong: it named an instant and neither whose reset it was nor why the rejector's earlier one did not count — and B-Stack's 5h reset at 03:50:57 hot-switched the conversation after 1 h 20 m, so even the promise was pessimistic. FIX = STRUCTURE FIRST, WORDS SECOND: `quotaVerdict` (PURE) returns `deadBuckets` + `until` (the bucket that sets blockedUntil); `quotaVerdictFor`'s blocked pool verdict returns `soonest` {member, bucket} (invariant: its reset IS blockedUntil) + `rejector` (the walled member with every dead bucket it holds); the engine's PURE `armCauseFor(v, demoted, armWall, evReset)` folds them into an arm `cause` (the rejector's OWN wall from the demotion's buckets, its `floor` = the other dead buckets) that `armIfEnabled` persists and a cause-less re-arm INHERITS like lane/bucket; `armNoticeFor(reason, resets, now, cause)` says `PandyMax 的 5h 将在 07:00 重置，但它的 Fable 仅剩 2%（低于 5% 门槛，视为用尽，9/20 重置）。最早可用的成员是 Member L（Fable 12:00 重置），已安排到时自动继续；任一成员提前可用会立即继续` (cause-less arms keep the old sentence byte for byte; rejector === soonest reads as one member's own reset; an unpooled wall names its bucket); `statusFor().cause` rides the auto-resume broadcast so the status-bar chip's tooltip says the same in the device's language; the journal's `armed for …` line ends `via <member>/<bucket>`. The FLOOR POLICY is unchanged (owner question open: may the rejector's own earlier reset be the target when no member is above the floor?). 不变量=**a wait computed from several members' windows must name the member and bucket it waits for, and a discarded nearer reset must be explained where it is discarded** (the reader's own account resetting at 7am is the fact they hold; a sentence that contradicts it without saying why reads as a bug). Gate: test-auto-resume-loop §B-73fe (the real engine + the real producer on the 02:30:43 shape: the arm, its cause, the sentence, the journal, the cause-less control, the single-member and unpooled forms), test-pool-auto (the verdict shape, empty shapes, passed-reset and no-reset), test-auto-resume §5 (the cause survives a restart; inherit vs replace).

## THE ARM CARD CALLED A HOT SWITCH A RESET (2.369.104, owner 2026-09-16 "你这套到达上限的监测似乎出问题了，明明没到也没被中断你却提示到达上限了？")

A real 429 on the current member (its 5h window resets at 07:50) made the pool switch the conversation at once and arm the +45 s near-nudge (`switched to a usable account (<member>)`), and the DELAYED arm announcement — one sentence for every arm — said `用量已达上限。已安排在 9/16/2026, 3:24:53 AM 重置后自动继续`: a reset instant that was no reset, about a limit the conversation was no longer on. The continue card had been fixed the same way in 2.369.97 (`continueNoticeFor`); the arm card had not. FIX = PURE `armNoticeFor(armReason, resetsAtMs, nowMs)`: a switch arm names the member and the seconds, only an arm anchored on a real reset states the reset instant. 不变量=**a sentence that names an instant must say what that instant IS** (a nudge is not a reset); **two cards about one arm owe the same cause rule** (the continue card's fix did not reach the arm card). test-auto-resume-loop (the switch wording + the reset control + the wiring pin)

## A CHROME LEG MATCHED ONE SPELLING OF A NAME THE PRODUCT COMPUTES (2.369.102, test-opencode-s9 'the session card of an OpenCode conversation OFFERS the serve terminal')

The leg created a serve session titled `browser leg`, posted a first message `roll me back`, then looked for a `.session-item-card` whose text contains `browser leg`. The sidebar names an OpenCode conversation by the SHARED first-user-message rule (src/opencode-serve.js NAMING: `nameFromText` over the first user message, cached per id, at most NAME_BATCH lookups per discovery tick; OpenCode's own title is only the FALLBACK), so the card reads `roll me back` as soon as the naming read has landed before the card renders — which it now does reliably, so the .101 heavy tier and a standalone run were both red on this one assert with 335 passing, while every earlier green run had simply rendered the card before the read. Fix = match the card on the sidebar's OWN name for the row (`s.name` / `s.webuiName` from `app.sidebar._allSessions`, both literal spellings kept as fallbacks). 不变量=**a UI leg matches the row's own computed name, never one literal the product may replace**; **read the failing assert's `sample` before calling a heavy red load** — `{err:'no card', cards:1, sample:['roll me back']}` named the mechanism.

## A UNIT GUESSED FROM THE VALUE'S MAGNITUDE READ ONE PERCENT AS A HUNDRED (2.369.99, inc-mu3giy8t-7k36 "刚才刷新刚刚添加的账号不知道为啥突然提示fable用量100%但实际上不是")

A subscription added at 01:56Z got its first ⟳ read at 02:00:27Z (Fable 0 %), the pool moved nine conversations onto it, and at 02:02:11Z a `control`-source reading stored `Fable u:1` beside a correct 5h 3 % / 7d 0 % — the pool declared it `exhausted, from 0% left`, moved everything back, and the owner's next ⟳ at 02:03:24Z read Fable **0.01** and moved everything forward again. MECHANISM: `parseOAuthUsage`'s NAMED scoped-bucket loop (`seven_day_<model>` top-level fields, added in 2.305.0 for inc-msof8i22) computed `pctRaw > 1 ? pctRaw / 100 : pctRaw` — a GUESS at the unit — while the `five_hour`/`seven_day` and `limits[]` branches of the very same payload divide by 100 unconditionally; the endpoint reports percent integers, so the one value the guess cannot classify is exactly 1, and a brand-new account after its first turn sits exactly there. Corpus proof (this instance's anchor streams): 139 control-source scoped readings, every one an exact hundredth, none at 0.01, ten at 1.0 (three with a genuine 100 % on the neighbouring panel read, the rest the same shape as this incident). The overage twin `pct <= 1 ? pct * 100 : pct` sat 130 lines lower. 不变量=**a unit is a fact about the PRODUCER, never an inference from the value** (when a sibling branch already states the unit, the guess is a second, weaker parser of the same payload); **a property over the whole input range is the test** (every integer 0..100 → n/100; the incident shape 1 → 0.01; the retired rule as the control answers 1.0 at exactly that point); **a bucket written by a guess is a wall the pool acts on within the same tick**. test-quota-source (d).

## A SETTING READ BUT NEVER DECLARED HID THE DESKTOP BUTTON (2.369.97, userW's inc-mu1qa5gj-9qe9 "找不到右上角的desktop了，Custom UI 也点不开")

userW's pod ran 2.369.96 with a real `Xtigervnc` on PATH and the shared desktop live on `:7`, yet the toolbar Desktop button was gone and customize mode could not bring it back (his action ring: Customize UI → two clicks on `btn-desktop` → Done → still nothing → report). MECHANISM, reproduced A/B in headless chrome against worktree servers with a fake `Xvnc` on PATH (2.369.95: `btn-desktop` computed `flex`; 2.369.96: `none`, `app._vncAvailable === true`, `app.settings.get('toolbar.showDesktopButton') === undefined`): `applyChromeSettings` draws the button as `show('btn-desktop', s.get('toolbar.showDesktopButton') && this._vncAvailable)` and customize mode lists it under `hideKey: 'toolbar.showDesktopButton'` — since 2.111.4 — but **that key was never a row in `SETTINGS_SCHEMA`**, so `settings.get` answered `undefined` and the `&&` hid the button whenever chrome settings were re-applied AFTER the VNC probe had answered. The probe's own resolve (`b.style.display = … !== false ? '' : 'none'`) tolerated the missing row, which is why the button appeared on load for 250 releases and only vanished on customize-mode Done (the one re-apply after the probe); 2.369.96's desktop-app launcher added a second probe that calls `app._applyChromeSettings()` when it resolves — on EVERY page load, after the VNC probe — and the latent defect became the default state. FIX = the row (`toolbar.showDesktopButton`, boolean, default true, liveApply, "Toolbar & Layout"), so `get` answers true and the customize toggle also persists; **test-architecture §44b** derives every `s.get('…')` inside `applyChromeSettings` and every `hideKey:` in customize-mode's registry from the two consumers' own text and fails any key the schema does not declare; **test-desktop-app-window** now states the probe outcome (`app._vncAvailable = true; app._applyChromeSettings()`) and asserts the button stays, with the user-off control — because the existing singleton leg only ever spoke the "no VNC server installed" text on a dev box without Xvnc, i.e. it could not see the box the feature is for. 不变量=**a key a consumer reads is a key the schema declares, and the census reads the consumers**; **a probe result that is tolerated at one site and required at another is two rules** (`!== false` vs `&&`) — the schema row makes them one; **a suite leg that passes only on the box WITHOUT the binary proves nothing about the box with it** (state the outcome directly, or put the binary on PATH).

## THE DESKTOP-APP KEEPER'S r2 — EIGHT VERIFIER FINDINGS ON A ONE-DAY-OLD FEATURE (2026-09-14, docs/design-desktop-apps.zh.md P8-1)

An adversarial verifier read round 1 (the desktop-app keeper, one day old) and found eight real defects. Every one was REPRODUCED on the real keeper (real Xvfb + x11vnc) before a line was changed, and every fix ships with a control that fails alone — a patched copy of the real keeper beside it (`src/server/vs-dak-mut-*.js`, gitignored, swept by PID), a Node-semantics control, or the pre-fix reader driven over the same processes.

- THE ACTION-TRACE ROW SAID "SERVER UNREACHABLE" ON EVERY CARD (2.369.145, owner "为啥都是没有记录到操作"): EasyPrivacy's `/trace?sessionid=` (filters are case-insensitive) blocked `/api/browser/trace?sessionId=…` in every content blocker; `fetch` threw, `fetchJson` returned null, the client's fallback word was "server unreachable" while the server never saw the request — the housekeeping window on another path worked, which was the tell. FIX = the path is `/api/browser/actions`; and a call whose browser the keeper never started (an agent's own profile, an unregistered directory) says "not traced" by name instead of "no recorded actions". 不变量 = a fetch failure the server never saw is diagnosed by the ONE request that differs (path/query shape) against the public block lists before anything else; an API path never pairs a tracker-shaped word with an id-shaped query; "no data" and "not collected" are different sentences.

- THE OWNER'S WINDOWS FREEZE WAS CHROME'S ACCESSIBILITY TREE (2.369.144, after .137/.141/.143's probes): a chrome://tracing over a refresh showed the BROWSER process UI thread in `HandleAXEvents` (content/browser/accessibility/render_accessibility_host.cc:77) for 39.5 s + 4.8 s + 0.8 s + 0.6 s; GPU main ≤ 31 ms, renderer main ≤ 91 ms, renderer `LocalFrameView::RunAccessibilitySteps` ×3526 = 218 ms. chrome://accessibility: Web accessibility ON, no screen reader, UIA client `typeless.exe`, 50,150 accessible nodes on the VibeSpace tab. Every in-page probe (self-gap, rAF-dead, jank storm, long tasks) was blind because the renderer was fine — the whole browser was blocked one process up. FIX = `accessibility.exposeChat` (off ⇒ chat lists aria-hidden, live) + hidden-desktop windows aria-hidden; the box-side remedy is Chrome's "Suppress automatic accessibility enablement" switch or quitting the UIA client. OWNER-CONFIRMED the same day: the Suppress switch removed the stall entirely; quitting the UIA client alone left a few seconds — Chrome keeps the mode on for the browser process's lifetime once enabled. Census (incident bundles, 2026-09-22): ≈ 60 elements per rendered card ≈ 130 AX nodes; 82 % of a visible window's rendered cards were OFF-SCREEN at capture and every one is in the tree (content-visibility:auto keeps them; code-block cards are content-visibility:visible); the minimap draws one marker per user turn of the WHOLE conversation; chat windows on hidden desktops were already out (content-visibility:hidden) so the desktop lever removed nothing in any captured state. The root fix (pending owner go): off-screen cards out of the tree, code blocks as ONE text node, paint-only surfaces aria-hidden, and a CDP AX-node census leg with a per-card budget. 不变量 = a whole-browser stall with a healthy renderer is diagnosed one process up (chrome://tracing → perfetto: longest slice per thread with its src_func), never by adding more in-page probes; a 50k-node DOM is an accessibility-tree cost the page must be able to opt out of.

- A WORKFLOW CARD LOST ITS PHASES ON EVERY SERVER RESTART (inc-muc2fmtt-5jat, 2.369.140): the task lifecycle records are LIVE-ONLY (transcript never; the wrapper's ring drops them minutes after a big tool output — measured: the owner's ring held 79 records / 498 KB, zero task_*), so a rebuild after the Update emptied every card's tree and the CLI emits `task_progress` only on transitions (throttled `Gr` while a workflow_agent is in progress — none in 30 min of one quiet agent). FIX = the stdout consumer persists the latest record per task in session-meta `taskRecords` (progress keeps the last tree field-wise), boot-restore carries it, rebuildHistory replays it silently via `MessageManager.replay` after the history and before the live queue. 不变量 = a live-only record a card depends on is persisted by the server the moment it passes, and replayed on every rebuild; a rebuild never emits ops (no client is listening yet); the wrapper's sidecar is the wrapper's, the server writes its own meta.

- A WORKFLOW CARD'S LIVE DETAILS APPEARED ONLY AFTER A RELOAD (inc-muc1hfeg-0qn2, 2.369.139): the CLI's `system/task_started` spells a Workflow `local_workflow` and lands BEFORE the launch ack on the live stream; `taskInfo.type` kept that word (the .122 fix registered the ack's runId but not its TYPE), and chat-view's live re-render gate `fields.taskInfo.type === 'workflow'` dropped every task_progress edit — a reload rebuilt the card from the server's normalizer, so the details "appeared after refresh". FIX = `normalizeTaskType` (`local_*` → ours) at every task_type read, the ack's type wins, the gate also admits a card carrying the tree. 不变量 = a producer's vocabulary is normalized ONCE where it enters; a client gate compares our word, never the producer's; when two records name one thing, the later-arriving one may not silently change the type the first one stamped.

- THE DESKTOP'S PASTE BUTTON WAS A SILENT NO-OP TWICE OVER (userW inc-mubu8xdg-pvwa, 2.369.136; the pod ran 2.369.116): the bundle's action ring shows Paste pressed three times on `.desktop-bar .file-tool-btn` and a `typing` burst on the BUTTON, the console ring shows the RFB closing 1006 on every connect (the desktop-app Chromium had exited with code 0 at once). `pasteBtn.onclick` returned silently when the RFB was gone, toasted one generic line on every clipboard failure, and never moved the focus off the button. FIX = a "not connected" refusal, a PASTE BOX (textarea → `paste` event → `clipboardPasteFrom`, works on plain http and without the permission) for refused / empty / API-less clipboards, focus handed back after every send; the retired window's exact toast kept on the unclassified path (test-vnc-view's byte-for-byte control). 不变量 = a user action on a disconnected surface says so; a failure of the browser's clipboard API offers the route that does not need it; a button that sends input hands the focus back to the input target. Recorded, not fixed: a desktop app that exits at once (Chromium code 0) shows "disconnected", not the exit — the keeper's exit reason must reach the window (desktop-apps lane).
- A WORKFLOW CHIP SAID "Workflow" (2.369.136, owner "这个workflow没有展示正确的名称"): `parseBackgroundLaunch` named a run only from `Workflow "<name>"` in the ack or `input.name`; a `scriptPath` launch has neither and its ack carries the description on a "Summary:" line; the live `/api/workflow` skeleton then overwrote whatever the client had with its own placeholder. FIX = Summary line → inline `meta.name` → script basename; the placeholder never overwrites. 不变量 = a name shown for a run comes from the run's own words (ack / meta / file), never a type label; a server placeholder is not a value.

- CODE APPENDED AFTER A MID-LINE `//` WAS A COMMENT, AND THE PIN THAT GUARDED IT WAS SATISFIED BY THE COMMENT (2.369.134, the agent-browser merge): the mounts-plugins wiring call in server.js carried three chunk notes mid-line; a build chunk appended `getTelemetry: () => …` after them (never wired; the wiring's `getTelemetry = null` default made it silent) and the verifier's fix round moved `activeSessions,` after them to satisfy test-plugin-loader's `/, activeSessions,$/m` — the pin matched, the key was comment text, `sessionFacts()` saw no session and every `/api/browser/*` route answered 404 "no such live session". Found only by the MERGED-TREE heavy run (test-window-binding / test-browser-live ③), reproduced on the lane's own head in a control worktree. FIX = the notes on their own line above the code, the keys as code; test-architecture §48 = the census over server.js + src/server/*-wiring.js (no object key / `, activeSessions,` after the first `//` outside a string) with the shipped line as its negative control. 不变量 = a textual pin anchored at `$` says nothing about a line whose `//` comes first — census the comment boundary, not the suffix; a dependency defaulted to null in the wiring is a silent-failure shape (the default hid a never-wired key for five chunks).

- INSTALLING xpra BROKE EVERY DESKTOP-APP LAUNCH (2.369.131, 2026-09-21): the P8-1 ladder's rule was "first rung whose needs are present" while the xpra row is `wired: false` — the binary's arrival made it the winner and the keeper's `backend-not-wired` refusal answered every launch. FIX = the PURE ladder judges `wired` with the needs (present + unwired ⇒ passed over, reason named, recipe still reported); the suite's presence oracle learned the rule and a wired-table control keeps DA1. 不变量=a capability table's `wired` flag is a SELECTION input, not a post-selection refusal — a rung that cannot run must lose the ladder, so that installing a binary never turns a working rung into a refusal. ⇒ gate: test-desktop-apps §3

- THE ACTIONS MIRROR WAS RED FOR 50 VERSIONS ON GATES THAT DEPEND ON THE MACHINE (2.369.125 r6–r8, 2026-09-21; three suites red on every mirror run since 2.369.75, a fourth from .124): each one was green on every developer box BY CONSTRUCTION. ① 40 of 41 chrome suites never pre-set `vs-onboarded`; `_checkOnboarding` ALSO skips the first-run wizard on a machine WITH sessions, so only the runner (empty ~/.claude) showed the modal under the chrome — its capture-phase Escape ate test-gear-menu's first Esc and test-toolbar-resize dragged the overlay. FIX = ONE shared `ONBOARDED_SOURCE` (scripts/scratch.mjs) before every navigate + test-architecture §47 census. ② The ghost-host heal lived in the workbench zone render; an instance with no sessions and no hosts takes the "No sessions" early return first, so a stale host id was never healed there. FIX = `_wbHealHostSelections()` ahead of the empty branch; the suite boots under an EMPTY HOME. ③ The roster identity line wrapped under DejaVu Sans (the runner's `system-ui`), dropping the credits tag off the name's line. FIX = `.acct-key-line` never wraps (name/ident ellipsize, the tag is a non-shrinking item); the suite runs a wide-font CONTROL. ⑥ (2.369.129, owner within hours: "完全看不到账号名称了") r8's `.acct-key-line` gave the NAME the largest shrink weight — every name in the Agents modal collapsed to one letter beside a full email; the ident now shrinks first (weight 1000) and the suite pins it under a 70-char ident with a negative control. 不变量=when two flex items compete for one line, the shrink weights ARE the product's priority — write them for the field the user reads first, and pin the order under pressure. ⑤ (2.369.128) The ephemeral-install warning row quotes the install PATH as one unbreakable token — on the runner (npm-global claude) 357 px inside a 352 px phone section; the roster suite's rightmost-element diagnostic named it from the mirror log. FIX = `overflow-wrap: anywhere` on the warning + the phone modal's status row wraps; the suite injects the product's own row shape with a negative control. ④ The mirror uploads every chrome suite's `/tmp/vs-*-shots-*` as an artifact on failure and the chrome asserts print their live state — the r6 artifact is what named ①. 不变量=a chrome leg red only on the mirror names the MACHINE-dependent product gate first (sessions, logins, fonts, a display); the gate becomes a census or a control the local run also exercises, never a per-suite patch; and a mirror red is read from its artifact, not guessed at. ⇒ gates: test-architecture §47 (41 chrome suites) · test-ghost-host-heal (empty HOME) · test-roster-reset-eta §3 wide pass + §4

- **A spawn failure killed the server.** `binOnPath` remembers a YES for good; a shim `x11vnc` was probed, deleted, and `launch()` threw `UNCAUGHT EXCEPTION … ENOENT spawn …/x11vnc` inside `bringUp` — the disk record read `launching` with every pid null, and the Xvfb started a moment earlier was alive with nothing recording it. `startX11vnc`/`startWindowManager` spawned with no `error` listener (only `startApp` had one — the asymmetry). FIX: `desktop-display.spawnDetached` is THE spawn (resolves on `spawn`, rejects `spawn-failed`, keeps the `error` listener for life), every start function returns a promise, `assertExecutable` re-stats the memoised path at spawn and forgets a refuted YES (`exec-vanished`), and each recipe hands every part back through `onPart`, which stamps pid+starttime and COMMITS at once — a failure one step later leaves a record the teardown and the next boot can reap. Control: a bare `spawn` of a missing binary with only an `exit` listener exits non-zero with ENOENT.
- **The picture port was an assumption, and the banner accepted anyone.** Measured: x11vnc 0.9.17 with a taken `-rfbport` does NOT exit — it prints `PORT=<the requested port>` on stdout, binds `[::1]:<the same number>` and leaves `127.0.0.1:<port>` to whoever holds it; `waitForRfb` read the stranger's banner and the record turned `ready` on a display the bridge would relay from someone else. FIX: `listenerInode(port)` (/proc/net/tcp, the 127.0.0.1 LISTEN first, else 0.0.0.0 — an `[::1]`-only bind is not a 127.0.0.1 listener) + `pidHoldsInode` + `listenerHeldBy(port, sessionCensus([server]))` before `ready`; else `failed` naming port and pid, and the stranger is never signalled. Control: a patched keeper whose holder is assumed turns READY on the impostor's port.
- **Stop leaked the application's children.** `sh -c 'sleep 3600 & exec sleep 3600'` ⇒ after stop the record said `exited`, `lastError null`, and the forked `sleep` lived on (a `yes` at 100 % CPU in the second shape). FIX: the SESSION model — every leader is spawned detached (sid == pid) and stamped `VIBESPACE_DESKTOP_APP=<id>` (a `KEEPER_ENV` name a row may not set); `killSessionVerified` signals the group and every member of `cli-identity.sessionMembers(pid, {fresh:true})`, verified empty; a leader that is gone or recycled contributes only members carrying the marker (a recycled pid is never signalled). Control: the pre-fix teardown (no census, no group signal) on the same shape reports "exited, clean" with the child still running.
- **The runaway guard sampled one pid.** `sh -c 'yes > /dev/null'` ⇒ `live.cpuPct 0` while `yes` burned 100 %. FIX: `sessionSample(sessionPids(rec))` over app + X + picture server + WM sessions; the verdict function is unchanged. Control: over one second the recorded pid burned 0 ticks while its session burned 100.
- **The bridge counted every frame as input.** noVNC sends a FramebufferUpdateRequest whenever the picture changes; measured on a real worktree server + headless chrome with no input dispatched, a once-a-second repaint advanced `lastInputAt` at exactly the 2 s throttle, so the DA3 idle stop could never fire for a session any tab showed. FIX: `rfbInputSieve()` walks the client→server stream (version → security → None/VNCAuth → ClientInit → typed messages with their lengths, several per frame) and counts only KeyEvent / PointerEvent / ClientCutText / QEMU extended key; a stream it cannot follow flips to `opaque` and every later frame counts — the direction that never reaps a live user.
- **The bring-up was a string switch on the rung.** `if (rec.via === 'Xvnc') … else if (rec.via === 'Xvfb+x11vnc') … else throw 'unknown bring-up'` — a fourth `DISPLAY_BACKENDS` row resolved, was chosen, and died at bring-up; the §2 rule "one row + one probe + one relay" was prose. FIX: rows carry `recipes[via]`, `M.recipeFor` names, `desktop-display.RECIPES[name]` runs, an unnameable pair is refused `no-recipe` before anything spawns, `resolveBackend`/`recipeFor` take an optional table and the keeper takes `backends`, and §8f brings a FOURTH rung up end to end through a recipe name the keeper has never seen. A grep census pins that the keeper spells no rung.
- **A cached fact judged a destructive verdict.** vnc.js's `singletonFacts().running` is its last `status()` answer and starts false; `adoptAll` reaped a desktop-singleton session as `X display gone` at every boot. FIX: `singletonLive()` calls `refresh()` before adoption, the tick and bring-up judge. Control: a patched keeper reading the cached boolean reaps the live app.
- **The gate suite killed itself.** Its §3 fixture recorded `process.pid` as a recycled app pid, and its exit sweep SIGKILLed every live recorded pid by bare pid — `ALL PASS (75)` then exit 137, twice, red in the heavy tier, a `/tmp/vs-desktop-keeper-*` dir left per run because `fs.rmSync` never ran. FIX: `sweepTargets` = pid AND starttime (`sameProcess`), never this process, whole sessions; the fixture is a throwaway `sleep`; the rule is pinned by a leg naming this process with the right AND the wrong starttime; exit 0 measured.

不变量: **a promise nobody awaits and an event nobody listens to are the same failure**; **a memoised probe is not a guarantee at the moment of use — re-measure at the act**; **record a pid the instant it exists, not when the sequence succeeds**; **a banner proves somebody listens, the socket's OWNER proves who — on the ADDRESS you connect to**; **a process a keeper starts is a SESSION, and its identity once the leader is gone is what it INHERITED**; **a guard samples what the thing it guards can spend**; **a cached fact is re-asked before a destructive verdict**; **a table the design promised is proven with a row the code has never seen**; **a test's exit code is part of its verdict, and a fixture must never name the process that will be asked to kill it**. Gates: test-desktop-app-keeper 115 (heavy, exit 0) · test-desktop-display 63 · test-desktop-apps 98.

## THE DESKTOP-APP KEEPER'S r3 — FOUR MORE FROM THE ROUND-2 VERIFIER (2026-09-14, docs/design-desktop-apps.zh.md P8-1)

Each reproduced on the real keeper (real Xvfb + x11vnc 0.9.17) before a line changed; each fix ships with a control that fails alone.

- **A stop during the bring-up orphaned a live X display and a passwordless picture server under an `exited` record.** `launch()` then `stop()` in the same tick: stop found no recorded pids, answered `exited pids={x:null…}`, and the recipe went on to spawn Xvfb and `x11vnc -nopw -forever` — 4 s later the SAME record read `exited` with `pids={x, server}` and a `/proc` environ census found both carrying `VIBESPACE_DESKTOP_APP=<id>`; nothing reaped them (`onPartExit` returns early on a non-live record, the bring-up's three early returns had no teardown, `adoptAll` skipped every non-live record, `pruneHistory` later forgot the record). Reachable from the window's Stop (shown while `launching`) and from `POST /api/desktop/apps/:id/stop`. FIX = three layers: `stop()` marks the record STOPPING first (the `stopping` set, which the bring-up reads at every await and then adds no more parts), tears down what is recorded (which also ends the recipe's RFB wait), WAITS for the in-flight bring-up (`inflight` + `settleBringUp`, bounded by the recipe's own deadline), tears down again and only then writes the verdict — so `stop()` 0 ms after `launch()` now ANSWERS with the pids it reaped, and a terminal state keeps meaning 'torn down, verified' (writing the verdict first made §5 observe `failed` while the burner was still dying); `onPart` on a record that DIED under the bring-up kills the part the moment it is known; `bringUp`'s `finally` reaps whenever the record died; and the BOOT belt `reapTerminalLeftovers` (ONE marker census over this store's ids) reaps whatever still carries a terminal record's marker — the only layer that survives a SIGKILL of the server between a stop's verdict and its teardown. Measured after: 0 marker-carrying processes at 5 s, both windows (stop before Xvfb / stop inside the RFB wait); the pre-fix mutant (`gone` neutered + no settle) leaves ≥ 2, and a fresh keeper on its store reaps exactly those and names them.
- **The runaway guard was blind to work in short-lived children.** `sessionSample` summed utime+stime of the pids ALIVE at sample time; a child that exits between samples takes its ticks with it. Measured, limits shrunk to 50 %/3 s: `sh -c 'exec yes >/dev/null'` tripped at 4.5 s; `while :; do yes | head -c 30000000 >/dev/null; done` read `live.cpuPct 0` for 20 s while Δ(own ticks over live pids) = 0-1 per 500 ms and Δ(own+cutime+cstime) = 53-55 (≈110 %). FIX = `procSample` returns `reapedTicks` (cutime+cstime — the kernel's own odometer of reaped children) and `sessionSample` sums own+reaped: a live child's ticks count directly and move into its parent's cutime when reaped, never twice. The churn burner now trips at 4.1 s; the live-only sample as the control stays `ready`. HONEST BOUNDARY (re-measured 2026-09-21, 2.369.125 r5): a burner whose parent is DEAD before the burner is reaped is reaped by init and NO reader over the session's own /proc entries can see that work (it is in no member's utime, cutime or cstime). The first spelling of the leg, `while :; do timeout 0.7 sh -c "yes >/dev/null"; done`, asserted `reapedTicks === 0` on a RACE: coreutils `timeout` signals the whole group, but in 4 of 6 standalone runs the intermediate `sh` reaped `yes` before its own signal landed and one iteration's 70 ticks rode cutime up into the loop — the heavy tier went red on it twice while nothing near the keeper had changed. The leg now CONSTRUCTS the ordering (`sh -c 'yes >/dev/null & echo $! > pidfile; kill -9 $'`, the orphan killed 0.7 s later) and reads 0-1 ticks against a `< 20` margin (≈ 210 burned). 不变量=a boundary leg constructs the ordering it claims; a leg that samples a kernel race is a coin the gate flips every run. The only reader that would is a per-session cgroup odometer (`cpu.stat` of a cgroup the session is spawned into — membership is inherited and outlives every reparenting); deferred, named here and in the suite's printed boundary leg. opencode-serve's single-pid guard deliberately keeps `cpuTicks`: a lone parent's cutime arrives as a lifetime LUMP when a long-lived child exits, a spike the sustain window correctly ignores, not a rate.
- **The bridge's message table misread one client message and then everything after it.** `RFB_FIXED_LEN[150]` (EnableContinuousUpdates) was 4; noVNC's encoder pushes 10 bytes (type, enable, x, y, w, h) the moment a server accepts the ContinuousUpdates pseudo-encoding. A wrong FIXED length does not flip the sieve to `opaque` — it stays "followable" and permanently misaligned: measured through the exported sieve, handshake + SetPixelFormat + SetEncodings + ECU + FramebufferUpdateRequest + KeyEvent ⇒ `inputs 0, pending 4`; ECU + three KeyEvents ⇒ 0. x11vnc 0.9.17 never advertises ContinuousUpdates (its binary carries no such string), so this box's rung was unaffected; TigerVNC's Xvnc — the fleet image's rung, driven here through the shim — does, and there the idle timeout would have reaped a session the user was typing in, the exact direction the bridge's header forbids. FIX = the table is `{0:20, 3:10, 4:8, 150:10, 250:4}` and is DERIVED, not typed: test-desktop-apps §7 parses every `RFB.messages.*` encoder out of the shipped noVNC, checks each fixed entry against that encoder's pushes, and drives every encoder's shape followed by a KeyEvent that must count with nothing pending; along the way the census showed a SECOND thing a fixed table cannot say — the shipped client sends type 5 in two lengths (6, or 7 with the ExtendedMouseButtons marker bit), so PointerEvent is read from its own marker bit, and ClientFence (248, sent to TigerVNC) is walked as `9 + payloadLen`. The round-2 table as a patched copy of the bridge is the control (0 inputs, not opaque, 4 pending).
- **A record with no starttimes made boot adoption SIGKILL strangers' process groups.** `sameProcess(pid, null)` degraded to bare liveness "because liveness is all we have", so a `ready` record naming three unrelated pids with `starts:{null…}` made `adoptAll` treat each as a VERIFIED leader (`failed at boot: picture server not answering — reaping what is left`) and `process.kill(-pid, SIGKILL)` all three — measured `[false,false,false]` after adoption; with WRONG starttimes the same record left them alive (correct). Latent today (the keeper only writes a null starttime where `procStart` returns null — a machine with no readable /proc — or from an older/hand-written record), and the shape the starttime rule exists to prevent. FIX = no evidence is not a yes: `sameProcess` is false for a null/undefined starttime and for a pid whose /proc cannot be read now; the keeper asks ONE ladder `partIsOurs` everywhere (a live ChildProcess handle this process spawned → pid+starttime → unproven), and an unproven leader is never signalled — only members carrying the session marker are. The round-2 `sameProcess` injected into the fixed keeper is the control (all three strangers dead). BOUNDARY stated in the keeper header: on a machine with no readable /proc a session cannot be proven ours after a restart, so it is recorded as ended and LEFT RUNNING — nothing unproven is ever signalled — while this process's own children still stop through their handles.

不变量: **a verdict that turns a record terminal owes every process that can still arrive under it** — the bring-up's exits, the parts spawned after the verdict, and the next boot each need their own reaper, and a `finally` is the one place every exit passes; **a stop must OUT-WAIT the launch it cancels, bounded by that launch's own deadline**; **a boot that only re-judges LIVE records cannot see the leftovers of TERMINAL ones — the marker the processes INHERITED is the census, scoped to this store's ids**; **a guard over a SET must count the work the kernel has already folded into a parent (cutime), and must SAY which reaping topology it cannot see** (init-reaped children — a cgroup is the only odometer that survives reparenting); **a message table typed from a spec is wrong at exactly the entry nobody tested — derive it from the peer you ship and drive every shape through the parser**; **a fixed length in a framing parser fails SILENTLY (misalignment, never a refusal), so the control must show 'followable and wrong'**; **an identity nobody recorded is not proven, and a process a keeper cannot prove is its own is never signalled** — the safe direction for a stranger is "left alone", for our own straggler it is the marker. test-desktop-app-keeper §9 + test-desktop-display (d′) + test-desktop-apps §7.

## THE DESKTOP-APP KEEPER'S r4 — TWO MORE FROM THE ROUND-3 VERIFIER (2026-09-14, docs/design-desktop-apps.zh.md P8-1)

Each reproduced on the real keeper (real Xvfb + x11vnc 0.9.17) before a line changed; each fix ships with a control that fails alone.

- **A stop between two parts of the bring-up still orphaned the part recorded during it.** r3's `stop()` tore down what was recorded, then asked `inflight.has(id)` and settled on the bring-up only when the answer was yes. But the first teardown waits ≥ 100 ms per part it signals (x11vnc, then Xvfb), and that is long enough for the bring-up's next `await` to resolve, `onPart('app')` to record the app, `gone()` to return, and launch()'s `finally` to delete the `inflight` entry — so `has(id)` answered false, the settle and the second teardown were skipped, and the verdict was written with `pids.app` set and alive. Reproduced by injecting `keeper.stop(id)` at the moment `display.startApp` is called: 3/3 `exited`, `/bin/sleep 3600` carrying `VIBESPACE_DESKTOP_APP=<id>` at 1.5 s and 5.5 s, no reap / still-running line; the same injection at `ctx.freePort` records x11vnc under `exited` without ever signalling it (it died on its own when its Xvfb closed — on that rung the leak is the app, not the listener). FIX: `stop()` captures `inflight.get(id)` BEFORE the first teardown and always settles + tears down on THAT promise (`settleBringUp(id, bound, p0)`). Control: the round-3 keeper (capture removed AND belt removed) leaves the app alive under `exited` with no reap line; and because the r4 marker belt (next finding) would ALSO catch this app — it spawns within ms of the stop, before teardown #1's census — the capture is proved ALONE with the belt removed.
- **A member that `setsid()`s itself left the session and survived every stop.** Rule 4 identified a session's members by their session id; a daemonising launcher, or `setsid sleep 3600 &` typed into the app's own xterm, leaves every leader's session and the sid-based teardown never sees it — while the env it INHERITED still names the session, and the keeper already owned that reader (`display.markerCensus`) but asked it only at boot (`reapTerminalLeftovers`). Reproduced: `sh -c 'setsid sleep 3600 </dev/null >/dev/null 2>&1 & exec sleep 3600'` ⇒ 4 marker-carrying processes while ready; stop() ⇒ `exited`, lastError null, the setsid'd `sleep` alive 1.5 s later; a fresh keeper's adoptAll on the same store ⇒ census 0 (only the next boot ever found it). The same worker survives the app-EXIT path (`sh -c 'setsid sleep 3600 … & sleep 1; exit 0'` ⇒ `exited (code 0)`, worker alive) — so the belt belongs in `teardown()`, the ONE place every terminal path passes (stop, app exit, failed bring-up, boot adoption), not at the end of `stop()` as first suggested. The runaway guard was blind to the same shape for the same reason (a setsid'd `yes` read cpuPct 0 / pids 3 for 12 s while `ready`). FIX: `teardown()` ends with `markerLeftovers(rec)` (ONE `/proc` environ walk for this record's id, never this process) + `signalVerified` on whatever still carries the marker, saying so with the pids; the tick runs ONE census over the records due a sample and unions it into `sessionPids`. Cost measured: 47-50 ms over 3,847-3,856 pids, per teardown and per GUARD_SAMPLE_MS while a session is live. Controls: the belt removed ⇒ the worker survives stop (and the next boot reaps it, as the verifier measured); the guard's union removed ⇒ the burner stays `ready` at 0 % over 3 pids for 6 s while the fixed guard trips it in 2.2-2.6 s. The §8(b) "round-1 keeper" and §9(a) "round-2 keeper" controls lose the belt too: the day it landed, §8(b) stopped reproducing the orphan it names.

不变量: **a fact you are about to depend on is taken BEFORE the wait that can change it** (`has()` after the teardown answers a different question than `get()` before it, and the teardown's own grace waits are the window); **a session id is a property of processes that never called `setsid` — the inherited env is the identity, and a reader that exists for boot exists for every teardown**; **the belt goes where every terminal path passes, not where the finding was observed** (stop was the symptom; app exit and a failed bring-up carry the same worker); **a belt that also catches the previous finding's shape is not that finding's fix — prove each layer with the other removed**; **an older pre-fix control must lose the newer belts too, or it stops reproducing the defect it names**; **a /proc walk on a hot path is stated in numbers and where it is paid**. test-desktop-app-keeper 155 (§10).

## THE FABLE-CAP POOL STORM — PLACEMENT FOLLOWED A FALLBACK, ONE REJECTION MARKED TWO BUCKETS, AND A HEALTHY MEMBER WAS REPORTED DEAD (2026-09-13 04:58-05:01Z, production forensics)

**THE REPORT.** "有账号有 Fable 额度但总是提示没有了" — and, in the journal, the owner's main conversation being thrown around a five-member pool for three minutes.

**THE TIMELINE**, from `journalctl --user -u vibespace`, `data/usage-anchors`, `data/usage-cache` and the frozen transcript in `data/incidents/inc-mtzcj5sl-lzmw` (all read-only):

- 04:38Z the server is OOM-restarted.
- 04:42:45Z the CLI's safety classifier reroutes the owner's session: an assistant record carrying `{"type":"fallback","from":{"model":"claude-fable-5-1"},"to":{"model":"claude-opus-4-8"}}`, and at 04:43:36Z a `system/model_refusal_fallback` with `originalModel: claude-fable-5-1`, `fallbackModel: claude-opus-4-8`, `trigger: refusal`, **`scope: "session"`**. After that record the session's **next 18 assistant records carry `claude-opus-4-8` and NO marker at all** — no `fallback` block, not even the `usage.iterations[].type: "fallback_message"` the announcing record had.
- 04:58:03Z `[pool] per-session switch …: sub-000000000001 → sub-000000000002 (fam=opus, from 66.95%)` + `Pool "全部": conversation "VibeSpace 主开发" moved to Member F (its opus quota was at 67%)`. The session's spawn model is `fable[1m]`.
- 04:58:33Z the CLI answers `You've reached your Fable limit.` — it had asked for **Fable**, and Member F's Fable cap read **100 % used** in its own 04:31Z panel reading.
- 04:58:33Z `[wall] demoted Member F 7d …` **and** `[wall] demoted Member F fable …` — two buckets, one rejection.
- 04:58:33 → 05:00:42Z the same three lines repeat for **Member W** and **Member Q**, auto-resume continuing the conversation onto each one (`pool switched to Member W — continued automatically` → rejected → armed → …), and the two OPUS conversations linked to the same members (`米哈游监控`, `Proxmox和Docker管理`) are dragged along on every hop, each notice reading "its opus quota was at 0%".
- 04:39:40Z, 04:58:00Z and 05:00:11Z, in between: `[notice] Pool "全部": no member can serve it — out of quota (still available: 5h 100%, 7d 69%, Fable 45%).`
- 05:01Z the owner kills and resumes the session with `model=fable[1m]` explicitly, `spawnModel` is persisted, the family reads `fable`, it lands back on Member P and has run fine since.

**THREE INDEPENDENT DEFECTS.**

**① PLACEMENT FOLLOWED A FALLBACK-SERVED MODEL.** `sessionModelFor` is `picked (newest) > served > spawn`, and the CLI's safety reroute is announced ONCE and then silent (`scope:"session"`), so the eighteen unmarked opus records made the answer `claude-opus-4-8` while the session kept REQUESTING Fable. `familyOfModel` said `opus`, `projectCacheForFamily` dropped the Fable scoped bucket — the binding constraint — and `decidePoolSwitch`'s proactive EDF tier then picked Member F on its soonest weekly deadline (11.8 h out), a member whose plan week read 83 % and whose **Fable cap read 100 %**, over Member P (plan 34 %, Fable 60 %), **the only member with Fable left**.

**② A MODEL-CAP REJECTION MARKED THE PLAN LANE.** The wire form of that rejection on the installed claude 2.1.267 is `rate_limit_event {rateLimitType:'seven_day', status:'rejected', resetsAt}`. The binary's whole type vocabulary is `{five_hour, seven_day, seven_day_overage_included, seven_day_sonnet, seven_day_opus, seven_day_oauth_apps}` — **there is no `seven_day_fable`**, so a Fable model-cap rejection is emitted on the UNSCOPED weekly lane and the only thing that names the model is the banner text, which `parseLimitBanner` reads as `{kind:'scoped', name:'Fable'}`. The wall machine got two signals for ONE rejection and wrote both: the event marked the PLAN week `utilization: 1, status: 'limited'`, the banner marked the Fable cap. Three members' plan lanes were falsely marked exhausted with resets 11.8 / 44.8 / 59.8 h out, and the OPUS conversations — for which the Fable cap is invisible but the plan lane is not — were evicted on every hop.

**③ A HEALTHY CURRENT MEMBER WAS REPORTED AS "NO MEMBER CAN SERVE IT".** On a HOT pool `decidePoolSwitch` runs the candidate ranking PROACTIVELY, and the `!pool.length` branch claimed `no-members` **before anything asked whether the current member was exhausted**. Member P was healthy on every bucket (THRESH weekly hard=3/hot=5 against 45 % Fable remaining) — the "still available" numbers in that notice are ITS OWN. The same verdict fed `noteNoPoolTarget`, i.e. auto-resume's "no usable member left". The owner read the sentence exactly as written.

**THE FIXES.**

**① PLACEMENT FOLLOWS THE REQUEST MODEL, NEVER A FALLBACK-SERVED ONE.** New session fact `_servedViaFallback {from,to,at}`, stamped from a `fallback` content block or a `model_refusal_fallback` system record **on the MAIN thread** (a sidechain's reroute says nothing about what the main thread requests), and retired the moment any other model answers. `sessionModelFor`'s ladder becomes **picked (newest) > spawn > served-IF-NOT-A-FALLBACK**: a served model that IS the standing fallback target cannot define the session. NULL-persisted like `_servedModel` itself — it is a statement about a RUNNING CLI's current reroute, and boot-restore's ladder falls to the picked/spawn model, which is the REQUEST model this field exists to protect. **Unknown stays `null` and `null` is the conservative answer**: no projection, so every bucket of every member counts — a member whose Fable cap is spent is a wall for an unknown session too. Both stdout feeds (the parse and `claudeSideEffects`) write it through ONE engine consumer pair (`noteServedModel` / `noteModelFallback`) rather than assigning `_servedModel` inline, because an inline twin is the drift the CS rules ban.

**② A MODEL-CAP REJECTION MARKS THE MODEL'S CAP.** A REJECTED, UNSCOPED weekly `rate_limit_event` on a claude session is **PROVISIONAL**: the wall SIGNAL is raised at once (the session is blocked whoever owns the bucket, the turn still classifies as walled, `maybePoolAutoSwitch` still runs in the same tick) but the BUCKET MARK waits for the lane, stashed on `session._turnLaneDefer` the way `_turnWallRefile` already stashes a per-turn fact. The lane is decided by, in order: the same turn's banner naming a MODEL (`kind:'scoped'` ⇒ fold the event into `scoped:<model>`, its `resetsAt` riding along — the banner deliberately states no time); the banner saying "weekly usage limit" (`kind:'sevenDay'` ⇒ the plan lane, confirmed); or, with no banner by turn end, EVIDENCE — if the slot's own cache shows the plan week clearly alive (remaining > 10, i.e. under 90 % used) **and** a scoped cap for the session's REQUEST-model family at ≥ 99 %, it is the cap; otherwise the plan, which is today's behaviour. Every outcome journals which rule decided, with the numbers it read. A 5-hour banner decides nothing about WHICH weekly lane and leaves the deferral pending. **The rule never mentions a model NAME**: a future `seven_day_fable` parses to `kind:'scoped'` through rate-limit-capture's existing regex and is never deferred, and `seven_day_overage_included` is the weekly lane's own accounting (the 2.361.2 monthly-cap incident) and is likewise never deferred. THE COST, STATED: the immediate write is what makes the pool act in the same tick, so a turn KILLED between the rejection and its `result` now leaves no bucket mark at all (today it would leave a wrong one) — the window is milliseconds, the incident's own rejection and its turn end share a second, and the pool still moves on the provisional signal.

**③ A HEALTHY CURRENT MEMBER IS NEVER "NO MEMBER CAN SERVE IT".** The `!pool.length` branch may claim `no-members` / `all-rejected` / `all-logins-expired` **only when the current member is exhausted (soft or hard)**; otherwise it returns the new named reason `no-better` with `noBetter: true`, which no surface renders and which `noteNoPoolTarget` is not fed. The three refusals are otherwise UNCHANGED — this narrows WHEN they may be claimed, never WHAT they say. And the sentence itself: `poolBlockedNotice` now prints the live list only BESIDE a dead one, because "out of quota (still available: 5h 100%, 7d 69%, Fable 45%)" is a contradiction about one member and it is verbatim what the storm printed.

**THE DATA — MEASURED, NO MIGRATION OWED.** The three false plan-7d marks carried resets 12-60 h out, but a wall mark does NOT outrank a fresh enumerating reading: `carryUnmeasuredLimits` hands back a previous limit only when the CLAIM is unchanged, and a re-measured 7-day window is a different claim. Measured on this instance's own store (read-only, 05:50Z): **Member F 1.00 @ 04:58:33 (wall) → 0.84 @ 05:35:58 (on-demand); Member W 1.00 @ 04:59:48 → 0.66 @ 05:39:58; Member Q 1.00 @ 05:00:42 → 0.70 @ 05:41:58** — i.e. 37 / 40 / 41 minutes of false exhaustion each, healed by the auto-cli loop's ordinary idle-rung panel read, not 12-60 h. That is now a pinned PROPERTY (§4) rather than a note, driven end to end: the false mark is written by the REAL engine (the pre-fix copy, because only it produces one) and cleared by the REAL `refreshViaCliPanel` with a fake `claude` on `CLAUDE_CMD`.

**WHAT COULD NOT BE REPRODUCED, STATED.** The incident's own `rate_limit_event` records are not recoverable — stdout is a head-dropped ring and that session was killed at 05:01Z — so the rejection's payload in the regression is the CLI's documented shape plus the `unifiedWindows` block measured on this instance's surviving buffers, not the incident's own bytes. Separately: those `unifiedWindows` state the SIBLING windows' utilization on every event (the one rejected sample here reads `five_hour 1.01` beside `seven_day 0.13`), which is strictly better evidence than the cache the turn-end rule consults — `parseRateLimitEvent` ignores it today, and wiring it is an OPEN follow-up, deliberately not taken in this change.

**INVARIANTS.**
- **A served model that the vendor SUBSTITUTED for ours is not this session's model.** The reroute is announced once and `scope:"session"`, so the fact must live on the SESSION and be retired by evidence, never re-derived per record.
- **`null` is the conservative answer to "which model".** No projection means every bucket counts; guessing a family hides exactly the bucket that refuses the turn.
- **A rejection whose LANE the vendor cannot spell must not be written on the lane it arrived on.** Defer the WRITE, never the SIGNAL: the session is blocked whoever owns the bucket.
- **Decide the lane by evidence that exists, and say which rule decided.** The banner outranks the cache; the cache outranks a guess; no model ⇒ the plan lane, named.
- **A rule about a model cap may not mention a model NAME** — it must fall out of the type vocabulary and the parse.
- **An empty candidate list on a PROACTIVE scan is not a wall.** A refusal that feeds a user notice and a spend breaker may only be claimed once the member the conversation is actually on has been asked.
- **A notice may not contradict itself about one member**: "out of quota" and "still available: …" are the same member's buckets, and the live list only means something beside a dead one.
- **A suite fixture that pins a CALENDAR DATE is a time bomb.** The codex prose-reset leg in test-auto-resume pinned `"try again at Sep 13th, 2026 8:36 PM"`, which was six days out when written; on 2026-09-13 it became ~14 h out, the arm stopped being a WATCH, and two asserts went red in the MANDATORY pre-push tier with nothing about the product changed (measured identically on master at d2065aa2). The fixture now reproduces the prose SHAPE and derives the instant six days out.
- **A hand-written stub of a live module drifts, and it fails far from the cause.** The three suites that model the engine for `session-stdout` destructure by name from a plain object, so a new dep is `undefined` and the first record that reaches it throws mid-consumer — surfacing four sections later as "the message never rendered". test-stdout-registry now DERIVES the required dep set from the consumers' own `const {…} = engine;` statements.

**ROUND 2 — TWO DEFECTS THIS BRANCH ITSELF SHIPPED, both reproduced against the round-1 commit before anything was changed.**

**r2① THE PROJECTION FAMILY IS NOT THE STATED MODEL — fix ① traded the incident's blind spot for its MIRROR.** `projectCacheForFamily(cache, fam)` DROPS every model-scoped cap of another known family, so whatever family reaches it decides which walls the pool and the verdict may SEE. Fix ① made `sessionModelFor` answer the REQUEST model while a classifier reroute stands, and then handed THAT straight to the projection — so the cap of the model that is actually ANSWERING is thrown away. The spend really is the substituted model's: this instance's own anchor for the incident (`data/usage-anchors/anchors-org_1567dcbc-….ndjson`, 04:58:47Z) reads `costSince.byFamily {opus: 21.3957, fable: 0}` over 24 requests. MEASURED through the REAL engine + REAL pool + real credential symlinks, one variable — current member healthy on everything, the EDF-preferred member with Fable 10 % used but **Opus 100 %** and a sooner weekly deadline: round 1 logs `[pool] per-session switch …: … (fam=fable, from 80%)`, links the conversation to that member and tells the owner "moved to Member F (its fable quota was at 80%)", and `quotaVerdictFor` answers `usable: true` about it — which is the thing that AUTHORISES an unattended continue; master moves nothing and answers `Member P: Opus 0% < 5%`. **Reachability, stated honestly:** an Opus scoped bucket is producible today by BOTH shipped producers — `parseRateLimitEvent({rateLimitType:'seven_day_opus'})` → `{kind:'scoped', scopedName:'opus'}` (that type IS in ②'s measured vocabulary) and `markLimitBanner` on "You've reached your Opus limit." → a `{name:'Opus', utilization:1, status:'limited'}` row — and `familyOfScopedBucket('Opus') === 'opus'`, which `projectCacheForFamily(cache,'fable')` filters out. No claude cache file on this instance carries an Opus bucket TODAY, but one (`__global__.json`) carries a SECOND model cap (`Nimbus Quill` beside `Fable`), so the multi-cap shape is the vendor's, not a hypothetical; the reroute half is routine (6 fallback records across 2 sessions in the frozen capture alone). FIX: **a STANDING reroute makes the family AMBIGUOUS — both the model we ask for and the model we are given can refuse the turn** (the incident proves the first: its rejection was "You've reached your Fable limit" while opus was answering; the ledger proves the second is what gets billed) — so the answer is round 1's own doctrine, written three lines above its own code and not applied here: **UNKNOWN STAYS `null`, and `null` means NO projection, so every bucket counts.** `projectionFamilyFor(session, model)` is the ONE rule, asked at BOTH projection sites (the per-session pass and `quotaVerdictFor` — because the verdict is the site that authorises money, and fixing only the placement site would have left the billed continue): a reroute to another or to an unnameable family ⇒ null; a reroute WITHIN the same family (fable-5-1 → fable-4) is one cap and keeps its projection; **with no session it is exactly `familyOfModel(model)`, so every session-less caller is byte-identical**. A stamp left standing by an idle session therefore costs CONSERVATISM, never money — the same answer an unknown-model session already gets. `sessionModelFor` is unchanged: it still answers the REQUEST model for the notice text, `_wallScope`, `fireIdentityFor`, the model lock and `laneByEvidence`, which asks which cap a REJECTION was about and the CLI rejects the REQUEST.

**r2② A BLOCKED POOL WITH NO NUMBER IN THE SENTENCE.** A HOT pool's `exhausted` is the SOFT (hot-raised) bar while `deadBuckets` is filtered on the HARD one, so fix ③'s `rest = dead && live` dropped the live clause whenever nothing was under the hard floor — and that is exactly the band a hot pool exists for (提前切). Driven through the real `decidePoolSwitch` + real `poolBlockedNotice` (hot, proactive, current member 7d 96 % used = 4 % remaining, which is ≥ `THRESH.weekly.hard` 3 and < `.hot` 5, every other member quota-dead): `reason 'no-members'`, `deadBuckets []`, `liveBuckets ["5h 90%","7d 4%","Fable 80%"]` ⇒ `Pool "P": no member can serve it — out of quota. Conversations on it will hit a limit until a window resets, …` — the binding number is the only actionable content and it is gone, on a pool that really IS blocked. (Master is wrong a DIFFERENT way on the same state: it prints `out of quota (still available: 5h 90%, 7d 4%, Fable 80%)`, i.e. the §3 contradiction, listing the blocking bucket as available.)
