FIX: **THREE BANDS, NOT TWO** — `deadBuckets` (< hard), `lowBuckets` (≥ hard, under the hot bar) and `liveBuckets` (≥ the soft bar) — and the sentence names every band that is holding the decision back: `spent: 7d 1%; nearly spent: 5h 7%`, `nearly spent: 7d 4% (still available: 5h 90%, Fable 80%)`. **Cold decisions are byte-identical BY CONSTRUCTION** (`soft === hard` ⇒ `lowBuckets` always empty, `liveBuckets` the old split), and that is MEASURED over a shape matrix against a master copy rather than read off the diff: three rows identical, three changed, and the three that changed are the hot-band ones.

**r2 INVARIANTS.**
- **The family you PROJECT by is a different question from the model a session STATES.** One asks "which model is this conversation about", the other "which quota buckets can refuse its next turn" — and while the vendor is substituting a model, the honest answer to the second is "more than one, so project by nothing".
- **A fix is scoped to the QUESTION, not to the line the verifier found it on.** The same projection defect sat at the placement site AND at `quotaVerdictFor`, which is the site that authorises a billed continue; the verifier saw one, and fixing only that one would have left the money path open. (Third instance of this rule in this file.)
- **A negative control must be the thing it names.** §1's control reverts `sessionModelFor` AND the projection sites, because "master" is both; on §1's own fixture either revert reproduces (master's ladder makes the stated and substituted families coincide, so the new rule keeps the projection), but on §5's fixture they differ — and the suite asserts that difference so the control cannot quietly decay into a control for something else.
- **A pool that refuses must name the numbers that made it refuse, in the band they are actually in.** "Still available" and "out of quota" argue with each other; "nearly spent" does not, and it is the sentence the 3-5 % band deserves.
- **A band split that is invisible on one setting is byte-identical BY CONSTRUCTION, and that is a measurement.** `soft === hard` on a cold pool is the reason, and a shape matrix against a master copy is the proof.
- **A census reads CODE.** The rule's own doc comment names `projectCacheForFamily(cache, fam)`; the site census blanks comments before counting, or it counts its own prose.

**GATES.** test-fable-cap-pool-storm (98: real engine + real pool + real credential symlinks over the incident's own cache numbers; the REAL stdout consumer fed the incident's two records on a fake pty; a patched-copy PRE-FIX control per fix, each asserted to hit; §5/§5b the r2 projection pair with the round-1 copy, the rule's truth table off a real engine, the grep-derived projection-site census and the "§1's revert alone does not reproduce this" control; §6 the r2 band pair with BOTH a round-1 and a master copy and the byte-identity matrix) + test-pool-auto (83) + test-account-pool (33) + test-account-verdicts (45) + test-login-expiry (257) + test-auto-resume (222) + test-auto-resume-loop (205) + test-new-member-wake (119) + test-quota-model (299) + test-spend-paths (261) + test-stdout-registry (255) + test-harness-contract (316) + test-session-schema + test-architecture (140). Six product mutations were each verified RED in the asserts whose job it is, with per-mechanism resolution: reverting only the per-session projection leaves the verdict leg green and vice versa. (test-readings-attribution is 17 red in this environment — its headless-chrome §10/§17 panel legs — IDENTICALLY on the round-1 base commit; it is a heavy-tier suite and nothing here touches it.)

**ROUND-1 GATES (superseded by the r2 line above, kept for the record).** test-fable-cap-pool-storm (62: real engine + real pool + real credential symlinks over the incident's own cache numbers; the REAL stdout consumer fed the incident's two records on a fake pty; a patched-copy PRE-FIX control per fix, each asserted to hit) + test-pool-auto (83) + test-readings-attribution (446) + test-auto-resume (222) + test-stdout-registry (255) + test-session-schema + test-architecture.

### r3 — THE LOCK, THE RESTART, THE TEARDOWN, AND THE WIRING THAT WAS NEVER THERE (2026-09-13)

Four more, each reproduced against the r2 commit (e3cc388e) on the real engine + real pool + real credential symlinks + the REAL stdout consumer **before** anything changed.

**(A) THE MODEL LOCK IS THE REQUEST MODEL** — the owner's correction. The incident's conversation was **LOCKED**: its frozen meta (`data/incidents/inc-mtzcj5sl-lzmw/frozen/session-meta/cw-6-1788859480777.json`) reads `modelLocked: true, lockedModel: "fable[1m]", pickedModel: "fable[1m]", spawnModel: "fable[1m]"`, and `maybeRepinLockedModel` re-sends `/model` at every turn end where the served model drifted — i.e. the server KNEW the request model the whole time and was actively re-asserting it. Yet "newest wins" let a fallback-served `claude-opus-4-8` record outrank both the lock and the pick. MEASURED with those exact fields: **one** main-thread opus record after a restart ⇒ `sessionModelFor` `'claude-opus-4-8'`, `projectionFamilyFor` `'opus'`, the conversation moved personal → fish (Fable 100 % spent), the notice said *"its opus quota was at 66%"*, and `quotaVerdictFor` — the site that authorises an unattended continue — answered `usable: true` about it.

FIX: the ladder is **LOCKED > picked > spawn > served-IF-NOT-A-FALLBACK**. The lock sits above the pick because the two *cannot disagree on the path that exists* — picking while locked RE-TARGETS the lock on both sides (`chat-status-bar` sends `lockModel` with the pick; ws-handler's `set-model` rewrites `_lockedModel`), and both halves are pinned as source facts rather than assumed. And because the lock is ALREADY persisted and restored, **a locked conversation is right immediately after a restart** — before any stamp, pick or served model has been re-learned, which is the incident's own shape. A lock whose served model DISAGREES is r2's ambiguity one rung lower: `projectionFamilyFor` answers `null`, so both caps count (the belt for a stamp we never saw — an older build's meta, a feed that was down). The CLI-side `/model` control is kept intact (unlocked + served changed with no fallback on record still defines the session), and the rung is deliberately NOT extended to a plain pick: a `set-model` writes `/model` on the same tick, so a served model older than a pick is stale BY CONSTRUCTION and treating that as ambiguity would suppress the projection for one turn after every ordinary model switch.

**(B) THE REROUTE STAMP SURVIVES A RESTART** (round-2 verifier). `_servedViaFallback` was `persisted: null` on the argument that *boot-restore's ladder falls back to picked/spawn = the REQUEST model* — true of the **LADDER**, false of everything else. A restored UNLOCKED conversation whose reroute was announced BEFORE the restart gets ONE main-thread opus record; nothing on the session refutes it, `servedDefinesModel` says yes, and the identical cascade runs (link → the Fable-100 % member, verdict usable via it) — the incident verbatim, one restart later. The reroute is announced ONCE and `scope:"session"`, so it can never be re-learned from the stream; only the file can carry it.

FIX: persisted in session-meta by the two **granular consumers** (never at their call sites — that is the twin the §1b census bans), through a LAZY meta-store dep because session-stdout is constructed after the engine in boot order, following the ws-handler `set-model` idiom (spread what is on disk, re-list only what changed). **ONE write per reroute, not one per record** — the incident announced the same reroute twice and a meta write re-runs the ledger's account-by-time attribution, so restating a standing reroute also leaves `at` alone (it dates when THIS reroute began). Restored at **all three** boot-restore sites through ONE expression, enforced by a DERIVED census so a fourth restore path goes red instead of silently dropping the fact. The retirement clears it **on disk** too, and a stale restored stamp costs only CONSERVATISM: `projectionFamilyFor` answers null and the REQUEST model wins.

**(C) A LANE DEFERRAL IS SETTLED ON TEARDOWN** (round-2 verifier). `settleTurnLane` is reachable only from `noteTurnEnd`, i.e. claude's `result` record — and a turn can end by the **wrapper dying**. MEASURED: a rejected unscoped `seven_day` event followed by a pty exit (no result, no banner) left **NO bucket mark at all**, where MASTER's immediate write left one — asserted as its own control, so the drop is a REGRESSION against master rather than a pre-existing gap. A dropped wall is the money direction: the member reads healthy to every OTHER conversation. FIX: the consumer binds `_settleTurnLane` and session-stdout's teardown calls it, exactly as `_retireCompaction` does for the compaction claim — the claim's OWN named function, never a reach into engine state. Scope kept tight: only the MARK lands, because the demotion still belongs to the turn-classification rule. And the `LANE_DEFER_MAX` early return, which drops a wall in SILENCE, now warns with the member's name and emits `rate-limit-lane-dropped` — with its reachability **stated honestly beside the constant** (a turn's rejections share one key by construction, so the cap binds only through a mid-turn `wallRecordTarget` re-file, and five such keys in one turn has NOT been measured end to end).

**(D) ROUND 1's CONSUMERS WERE NEVER WIRED** — found while wiring (B), reproduced against e3cc388e. server.js hands session-stdout and session-brain **hand-picked `engine:` object literals**, and round 1's own `noteServedModel` / `noteModelFallback` were in NEITHER — nor in the engine's top-level destructure. So the call at `claude-stream-json.js:609` was a `TypeError` inside the **per-LINE try**, whose catch treats the record as "non-JSON noise": MEASURED by building that exact literal and feeding an ordinary assistant record through the REAL consumer — broadcast frames `["output"]` (the JSON line painted into the chat as raw terminal output), normalizer ops `[]` (**the message never rendered**) and `_servedModel` undefined. The whole round-1 fix was dead in production AND every claude chat session was broken, while three suites stayed green because every one of them passes the WHOLE engine. FIX: exported and passed at both literals, and the census DERIVES both halves — the consumer set from the registry, the required names from each consumer's own `const {…} = engine;`, the wired names from server.js's own literals.

**r3 INVARIANTS.**
- **A lock the server RE-ASSERTS to the CLI is the request model, and it outranks any served record however new.** The product was already writing `/model` at every drifted turn end; the ladder just had to believe its own instruction.
- **A fact the CLI announces ONCE cannot be re-learned from the stream, so it belongs in the file.** "Boot-restore's ladder falls back to the right thing" was true of the LADDER and said nothing about `servedDefinesModel`, `projectionFamilyFor`, or any other reader — **an argument that is true of one reader is not true of the field**.
- **A turn-lifecycle claim owes EVERY exit, including the one no record describes.** Second instance of this rule in this file (the compaction claim was the first) — and the second time the missing exit was the teardown.
- **A bound nobody can prove unreachable may not discard a wall in silence** — and the reachability argument belongs beside the constant, stating what was NOT measured.
- **A consumer's dependency list and the literal that satisfies it must be DERIVED from each other.** r2 added a derived census for the *test stubs* and left the *production* literal hand-written; the hole that census was built for was already open in server.js. A hand-written list is the tool this class has already defeated.
- **A mutant must go RED, not crash.** Mutant (B) first killed the run with a `TypeError` 140 asserts early, because a leg read the stamp the assert above had just decided on — a precondition deserves a loud red per leg.
- **Methodology, paid for in lost work:** *never mutation-test with `git checkout` against an UNCOMMITTED tree.* The battery's restore step wiped every product edit in this branch and they had to be redone from scratch; commit first, then mutate.

**r3 GATES.** test-fable-cap-pool-storm (**178**: §7/§7b/§7c the lock — the frozen meta's own fields, the truth table off a real engine, both `set-model` re-target source pins, a control proving the ladder rung and the projection rung are different defects, and the lock-drift rung's own harm on an Opus-spent member; §8/§8b the stamp through the REAL session-meta store and boot-restore's OWN hydration expression, plus the derived site census with its negative control; §9/§9b the teardown through the REAL consumer with a pre-fix patched copy, a MASTER control proving the regression, and the cap's counter with three controls; §10/§10b the derived wiring census and the CONSEQUENCE driven over server.js's own sliced literal) + test-stdout-registry (255) + test-pool-auto (83) + test-account-verdicts (45) + test-account-pool (33) + test-readings-attribution (446) + test-quota-model (299) + test-login-expiry (257) + test-auto-resume (222) + test-auto-resume-loop (205) + test-new-member-wake (119) + test-spend-paths (261) + test-session-schema + test-harness-contract (316) + test-ws-contract + test-architecture (140; server.js back inside the 2100-line ratchet). **Seven product mutations each verified RED in exactly the asserts whose job it is** — lock rung 9, projection lock-drift rung 6, `persistFallbackStamp` 8, boot-restore hydration 1 (the census, naming the two sites that dropped it), teardown settle 3, drop counter 3, server.js wiring 4 (including the raw-output consequence) — and test-stdout-registry stays GREEN under the last of those, which is the stated boundary: that suite censuses its own STUB, §10 censuses the production literal.

### r3-r2 — THE LOCK LATCHED THE MODEL THE CLASSIFIER SUBSTITUTED (2026-09-13)

Two findings from the round-3 adversarial verifier, each reproduced against the r3 commit (89048055) on the **real engine + real pool + real credential symlinks + the REAL stdout consumer** (`session-stdout.create()` → the registry → `claude-stream-json`) on a fake pty, before anything changed.

**(A) THE TARGET-LESS LOCK LATCH MADE THE SUBSTITUTE AUTHORITATIVE FOR PLACEMENT — a REGRESSION against r2.** r3(A) put the LOCK at the top of `sessionModelFor`'s ladder. But `src/server/stdout/claude-stream-json.js` has latched a **target-less** lock since long before this branch — `if (session._modelLocked && !session._lockedModel) session._lockedModel = session._servedModel;`, in master, where it only ever fed `maybeRepinLockedModel` — and that latch hands the new rung **whatever answered**. While a classifier reroute is standing, that is the SUBSTITUTE.

MEASURED (session `{_spawnModel:'claude-fable-5-1[1m]', _modelLocked:true, _lockedModel:null}` on `personal`, the only Fable-alive member; two real records fed to the real consumer — `{type:'system', subtype:'model_refusal_fallback', original_model:'claude-fable-5-1', fallback_model:'claude-opus-4-8', scope:'session'}` then one main-thread `{type:'assistant', message:{model:'claude-opus-4-8'}}`):

| | r2 (e3cc388e) | r3 (89048055) | r3-r2 |
|---|---|---|---|
| `_lockedModel` | `claude-opus-4-8` (latched, and **written to session-meta**) | `claude-opus-4-8` (same) | **`null`** — nothing written |
| `sessionModelFor` | `claude-fable-5-1[1m]` | **`claude-opus-4-8`** | `claude-fable-5-1[1m]` |
| `projectionFamilyFor` | `null` | **`opus`** | `null` |
| link | `personal` | **`fish`** (Fable 1.00) | `personal` |
| notice | none | `Pool "all": conversation "sess-lockless" moved to Member F (its opus quota was at 66%)` | none |
| `quotaVerdictFor` | via `personal` | **`usable:true` via Member F** | via `personal` |

The meta write is what makes it permanent, and it **defeats r3(B)**: a session hydrated exactly as `src/server/boot-restore.js` does (`_modelLocked:true, _lockedModel:null, _servedViaFallback` restored from meta) gets one opus record after boot and the latch overrides the very stamp that exists to make a restored conversation right — same measured outcome, one restart later.

Neither existing guard could catch it: after the latch `familyOfModel('claude-opus-4-8') === 'opus'`, so r2's `fb.to` rung and r3's lock-drift rung both compare opus against opus and pass, while the lock rung sits ABOVE `servedDefinesModel` in the ladder. **Reachable by construction, not by luck** — `src/ws-create.js` creates this exact state (`_lockedModel: data.modelLock ? (data.lockModel || data.model || null) : null`, whose own comment says *"No target => the server latches the first main-thread served model instead"*), boot-restore hydrates it from meta at both sites, and `src/lib/session-lifecycle.js` carries `modelLock` and `lockModel` as SEPARATE saved-config keys, so any config predating the review-added `lockModel` resumes locked-with-no-target. r3's own suite built this session as a STATIC object and asserted the ladder falls through — it stopped one record short of driving the latch that fills the field.

**FIX, TWO LAYERS, EACH WITH ITS OWN CONTROL.** ① **The latch asks the engine's own predicate**: `servedDefinesModel(session)` is added to the consumer's `} = engine;` destructure and to server.js's session-stdout `engine:` literal (so §10's derived census enforces the wiring), and the latch runs only when it says yes. It is asked AFTER `noteServedModel` on purpose — a record served by anything but the reroute target has already RETIRED the stamp and may latch — and a refused latch loses nothing, because the repin no-ops with the substitute latched anyway (`modelsMatch(served, locked)`). ② **The rung itself refuses a target that IS the standing reroute** (`!(s._servedViaFallback?.to && modelsMatch(s._lockedModel, s._servedViaFallback.to))`). That is not decoration: `src/ws-handler.js`'s `set-model {lock:true}` with no `lockModel` ALSO adopts `session._servedModel` — the UI row it serves says *"Lock to this model"* about the model on screen, so the user's words are left alone and the RUNG refuses instead — and a session-meta written by any build with an ungated latch carries the substitute for ever. Falling through invents nothing: `servedDefinesModel` refuses that same served model one line below, so the answer is the pick, else the spawn model — the REQUEST — measured byte-for-byte identical to r2's answer for both writers' shapes. The `_lockedModel` writer set is now a PRINTED, source-derived census (7 sites, each classified with a reason) because that field decides placement, and an unclassified writer or a dead row goes red.

**(B) WHAT A STALE REROUTE STAMP ACTUALLY COSTS** (the verifier's LOW finding — a CLAIM defect, not a code one). r3's `_servedViaFallback` row promised a stale stamp "costs only CONSERVATISM". Measured on the real engine + real pool (current member `cur` 5h 1.00 / 7d 0.40 / Fable 0.30; escape `esc` 5h 0.05 / Fable 0.10 but **Opus 1.00**): with no stamp the session projects fable and escapes (`link cur → esc`, usable true); with a stale stamp `projectionFamilyFor` answers null, EVERY bucket counts, `esc` is disqualified by an Opus cap the session is not using, and the conversation **stays on the member whose 5-hour window is 100 % spent** (`usable:false`, notice `no member can serve it — spent: 5h 0% (still available: 7d 60%, Fable 70%, Opus 70%)`). It can never authorise a move ONTO a spent cap — that is the direction that costs money — and it self-heals at the first main-thread record served by anything else (measured: `link cur → esc` on the next tick). **r2 strands on the identical fixture**, so r3(B) introduced nothing; persisting the stamp only makes the state survive a restart instead of being cleared by one. The row and the engine essay now say that, and the sentence is pinned by the suite.

**r3-r2 INVARIANTS.**
- **Putting a field on a money ladder makes every WRITER of that field a money path.** `_lockedModel` fed a repin for releases; the moment r3 put it above `servedDefinesModel` it decided placement, and the pre-existing latch became a money bug without changing a byte. Re-read every writer, and CENSUS the set rather than trusting the one the incident pointed at.
- **A model the vendor SUBSTITUTED is never a request, wherever it is copied to.** The predicate that knows this already existed; the defect is that a copy of the served model reached a request-shaped field without passing it.
- **Defence in depth is only defence if each layer has its own control.** The latch-only control must show the belt still holding (it does, and that assert is the proof the two layers are different defects); only reverting BOTH reproduces the incident.
- **A fix is scoped to the QUESTION — but when the second writer is a USER action, leave the user's words alone and make the RUNG refuse**, then prove the rung covers that writer with a functional leg instead of adding a second guard or a twin predicate (the alternative needed either a new `WS_CTX_CONTRACT` dep or a re-spelling of `servedDefinesModel`).
- **A sentence in a schema row is read by the next round as fact.** Measure what the state can actually do, including the direction that REFUSES rather than spends, and pin the sentence.

**r3-r2 GATES (superseded by r4's counts below).** test-fable-cap-pool-storm (**232**: §11 the blocker driven end to end through the real consumer, §11b the restored shape through boot-restore's own hydration expression, §11c the legitimate latch and the order that makes the retirement latchable, §11d the negative control as a three-file patched chain — consumer → registry → session-stdout — with the latch-only arm proving the belt and the both-reverted arm reproducing the incident; §12 the belt's four shapes + the r2-answer equivalence, §12b the `_lockedModel` writer census, §13 the stale-stamp measurement with r2 as its control and the claim pinned) + test-stdout-registry (**255** — its engine-dep census caught the missing stub dep; the stub MODELS the predicate rather than stubbing it falsy, which would silently disable the latch in every leg) + test-architecture 140 + test-ws-contract + test-session-schema + test-pool-auto 83 + test-quota-model 299 + test-readings-attribution 446 + test-auto-resume 222 + test-auto-resume-loop 205 + test-new-member-wake 119 + test-login-expiry 257 + test-spend-paths 261 + test-harness-contract 316 + test-peer-delivery 48. Five product mutations each verified red in exactly the asserts whose job it is: the latch gate (4), the belt (9), the engine literal (3), the top-level destructure (2 — the lost-binding class, now a DERIVED rule: every name in either `engine:` literal must be destructured from the engine), and the stale-stamp claim in both files (3).


### r4 — THE FACT WAS PLACED AFTER ITS READER (2026-09-13)

**THE FINDING** (the round-3 adversarial verifier, reproduced on the REAL pipeline — `session-stdout.create` → the registry → `claude-stream-json` on a fake pty, plus the real engine, the real pool and real credential symlinks — against the shipped r3-r2 commit before anything changed).

r3-r2 gated the target-less lock latch on `servedDefinesModel`, and asked it **AFTER** `noteServedModel` — deliberately, and correctly for the shape it drove: §11's reroute is announced by a SEPARATE `system/model_refusal_fallback` record that arrives FIRST, so by the time the assistant record lands the stamp is already on the session. **The incident's own transcript has the other shape, and it is the FIRST announcement.** The frozen tail (`data/incidents/inc-mtzcj5sl-lzmw`, session 9f4cd444-…, line **219** — the task brief says 218, measured 219) is ONE record that carries both facts:

```
{type:'assistant', isSidechain:false,
 message:{ model:'claude-opus-4-8',
           content:[{type:'fallback', from:{model:'claude-fable-5-1'}, to:{model:'claude-opus-4-8'}}] }}
```

…at **04:42:45.234Z**, and the `system/model_refusal_fallback` twin only at **04:43:36.398Z** — **51 seconds later**. For those 51 s the only thing on the wire that said "rerouted" was inside the very record the substitute answered. Both consumers read the served model at the TOP of their assistant branch while the content-block loop that stamps the reroute sits ~90 lines BELOW it, so the gate asked a question whose answer this record had already given and nobody had written down.

**MEASURED on the shipped d6c5a9ca**, session `{_spawnModel:'claude-fable-5-1[1m]', _modelLocked:true, _lockedModel:null}` (ws-create's `modelLock` with no `lockModel`), ONE record:

| | shipped r3-r2 | fixed |
|---|---|---|
| `_lockedModel` | `claude-opus-4-8` | `null` |
| `session-meta.lockedModel` | `claude-opus-4-8` (**permanent**) | absent |

§12's belt holds the PLACEMENT *while the reroute stands* — link `personal`, projection null, `sessionModelFor` = `claude-fable-5-1[1m]` on both arms. **But the meta write outlives the reroute it was taken from.** The stamp self-heals at the first record served by anything else — and that record is exactly what makes the bad lock visible: with the stamp gone the belt has nothing to refuse and the lock rung answers the substitute for the life of the conversation. Driven end to end (frozen record → one `claude-fable-5-1` record → a no-banner rejected unscoped `seven_day` → `result`) on the incident's own member (plan week 0.65 alive, Fable cap spent):

```
shipped:  sessionModelFor 'claude-opus-4-8'
          [wall] unscoped weekly rejection on Member W → the plan weekly lane
                 (no banner: the plan week reads 65% used, this session's opus cap unknown …)
          [wall] demoted Member W 7d until 2026-09-15T11:59:08Z      ← the §2 harm, ~2 days
          the Fable cap that actually refused the turn is never marked
fixed:    sessionModelFor 'claude-fable-5-1' · [wall] demoted … fable · sevenDay 0.65 untouched
```

i.e. ONE record reopened the incident's §2 harm through §11's own fix.

**THE FIX IS THE ORDER, NOT A THIRD GUARD.** `rerouteAnnouncedBy(msg)` (engine-side, one implementation both feeds ask, main-thread-only INSIDE the function so neither call site can get it wrong) extracts the reroute THIS record announces, and the two consumers stamp it through `noteModelFallback` **before** `noteServedModel` and before the latch. The later stamps stay exactly where they are as belts for the shapes this rung cannot see (a `<synthetic>`-model record, a system record with no assistant record at all) and cost nothing: `noteModelFallback` returns early on `prev.to === to` and deliberately leaves `at` alone, so restating a standing reroute moves no byte and writes no meta.

**AND THE VERIFIER'S OWN CLAUSE RIDES ALONG AS A BELT**: `!announced` on the latch — a record that ANNOUNCES a reroute never latches, whatever any stamp says. Today **either alone refuses this record**, and that is the point: the ORDER is a property of one function's statement order (one refactor away from being lost), the BELT is a property of the RECORD. Each has its own single-mechanism control, and only reverting BOTH reproduces the shipped shape.

**r4 INVARIANTS.**
- **A fact must be placed before its readers, and "its readers" includes the ones inside the same `if` block.** The gate, the retirement rule inside `noteServedModel`, and every later reader were all downstream of a stamp written ~90 lines further down; the code was right about WHAT to ask and wrong about WHEN.
- **Two records announcing the same event do not arrive in the same order twice.** r3-r2 measured the system-record-first shape and wrote the ordering comment from it; the incident's own transcript shows the block-first shape 51 s earlier. When a fact has more than one producer, drive the FIXTURE from the frozen evidence, not from the shape the last round happened to build.
- **A guard that is only correct because of statement order owes a second guard that is correct about the record** — and each owes a control that fails alone, or "defence in depth" is one mechanism with two spellings.
- **A permanent write outlives the transient state that justified it.** The belt made the latch harmless *while the reroute stood*; the harm surfaced when the reroute retired. Ask what a stored value means after the condition that produced it is gone.
- **A twin is enforced by a DERIVED census, not by a comment.** Removing the two lines from `session-brain.js` changes nothing observable in the shape §14e drives (there was no prior stamp to retire) — only the grep census over every file that CALLS `noteServedModel(` goes red and names the file. A comment saying "the parse has the identical two lines" is not a gate.
- **A census's window is MEASURED and PRINTED, never guessed** (measured gaps: session-brain 110/64 chars, claude-stream-json 278/226, window 1200), and it owes both directions — a capture with no ask above it and a capture whose stamp comes AFTER it (the shipped shape) must both fail, and the fixed shape must pass.

**r4 GATES.** test-fable-cap-pool-storm (**272**: §14 the frozen record verbatim from `scripts/fixtures/claude-fallback-block-record.json` — structural fields only, the owner's cwd/sessionId/branch/slug/parentUuid and the real `usage` block stripped — through the real pipeline; §14b the downstream lane harm end to end; §14c the ORDER-only, BELT-only and BOTH-reverted controls chained consumer → registry → session-stdout, the last one reproducing the latch, the meta write AND the false plan demotion; §14d the sidechain + the pure rule's truth table; §14e the same record through session-brain's OWN entry point; §14f the derived, printed census) + test-stdout-registry **255** (its engine-dep census caught the missing stub dep on the first run — the stub MODELS the new rule for the same reason it models `servedDefinesModel`) + test-session-brain-dark 4 + test-architecture 140 + test-pool-auto 83 + test-account-pool 33 + test-account-verdicts 45 + test-session-schema 5 + test-ws-contract 5 + test-auto-resume 222 + test-auto-resume-loop 205 + test-login-expiry 257 + test-readings-attribution 446 + test-new-member-wake 119 + test-quota-model 299 + test-spend-paths 261. Four product-source mutations each verified red in exactly the asserts whose job it is: the ORDER (§14f's census + §14c's belt-only arm), the BELT (§14c's order-only arm), BOTH (§14 + §14b, the incident itself), and the TWIN in session-brain (§14f alone, which is precisely why the census exists).


## EVERY SESSION CREATE AND EVERY KILL BLOCKED THE EVENT LOOP FOR 11-17 SECONDS (2026-09-09, userW's fleet pod)

**THE REPORT.** userW right-clicked the response-style chip, chose "Restart now to apply (Terminate + Resume)", and the chat window went blank for ~30 s. The server log (2.369.67, the same code as master) reads: `[session] killed` at T+0 · `[ws] heartbeat round tainted (tick 14s late, longest loop gap 13s)` at T+14 s · `[session] created` at T+30 s · another `longest loop gap 14s` immediately after. It is not that restart: seven days of that pod's log show **EVERY session create and EVERY kill — chat or plain shell terminal — followed by an 11-17 s event-loop block, 27 of 27**. The pod: 61 claude lock files under `~/.claude/sessions`, 61 live claude CLIs, **tmux NOT installed**, 3,074 processes (2,780 zombies), server RSS 1.6 GB with 107 threads.

**THE MECHANISM — THE COST WAS NEVER THE WAIT, IT IS THE FORK.** A 240 s CPU profile of the integrator's own production server (1.5 GB RSS, 57 threads) shows **17.3 s of SYNCHRONOUS `spawn` self-time in the parent**, 15.6 s of it from session discovery: `findTmuxTargetAsync` 10.7 s, `discoverClaudeSessions`' pgrep block 3.7 s, `getTmuxPaneMapAsync` 1.2 s. A micro-benchmark of `child_process.spawn('true')` in a node process gives **1.8 ms per spawn at 45 MB RSS, 18.8 ms at 543 MB, 72.5 ms at 1.5 GB**: fork(2)/posix_spawn copies the parent's page tables **on the calling thread**, so an "async" `execFile` still costs the parent that much wall time before any child runs, and `Promise.all` over N spawns serialises N forks back to back in ONE tick. On userW's pod: 61 locks ⇒ `tmux list-panes -a` (which FAILS, tmux is missing) ⇒ an EMPTY pane map ⇒ `findTmuxTargetAsync` spawns `ps -p <pid> -o ppid=` for EVERY lock ⇒ ~61 forks × ~130 ms ≈ the observed block. The same sweep runs on every `/api/sessions` poll (4.5 s cache, clients poll at 5 s) and again after every create and kill, plus `pgrep -P <childPid>` per live session on every 15 s cache miss. **The 2.235-era comment directly above `execFileP` already diagnosed "each sync fork is 100-300ms under load" and fixed only the WAIT (sync→async), not the FORK** — 2.242.0 moved the blocking, it did not remove it.

**THE FIX — NO SPAWN PER ITEM, and the rule is not "few".**
1. **The parent of a lock's pid** comes from `/proc/<pid>/stat` field 4 through THE process reader (`cli-identity.readPpid`), parsed after the LAST `)` because comm may contain spaces and parens — the same rule `procStartTicks` uses for field 22. `findTmuxTargetAsync` also short-circuits on an EMPTY pane map, which is the shape on every machine without tmux.
2. **The children of a live session's `_childPid`** come from the union of `/proc/<pid>/task/<tid>/children` (`cli-identity.readChildPids` — children are recorded against the THREAD that forked them, so asking only the main thread is half an answer). The per-session `pgrep -P` is gone **and so is its 15 s `_childPidCache`**, which existed only to blunt the fork and was also a correctness cost: a wrapper that forked a new claude inside the window stayed unmapped.
3. **TMUX IS OPTIONAL.** `getTmuxPaneMapAsync` asks `tmuxOnPath()` — `fs.statSync` over the PATH entries, memoised 60 s, never a `which` (that would be one more fork per sweep on exactly the machines this fix is for) — and caches the map for `TMUX_MAP_TTL_MS` = 4 s so a create/kill burst shares one. One `tmux list-panes` per sweep where tmux exists; **zero children where it does not**.
4. **The no-/proc rung is ONE `ps -eo pid=,ppid=` FOR THE WHOLE SWEEP** (`parentIndex`, memoised `PROC_TABLE_TTL_MS` = 2 s, deliberately under the discovery cache's own 4.5 s), never one exec per pid — macOS is "full support" in the README and a procfs-only reader would answer `null` for every pid there.
5. `isLockClaude` keeps the `procStart` file read first and, where there is no usable stamp, now asks THE identity (`isCliProcess`) on any procfs machine — which is the DAEMON snapshot's own rule (`pidLooksClaude`), i.e. one implementation — leaving the `ps -o comm=` twin reachable only where there is no /proc at all.
6. The SYNC tmux twins (`getTmuxPaneMap`/`findTmuxTarget`) are DELETED. They had **no callers anywhere** — the kb line "sync variants remain for boot paths" described boot-restore's OWN `execFileSync` calls — and they spelled the retired per-pid fork. Dead code that spells a forbidden shape is how the shape comes back.

**THE DAEMON ALREADY DID IT RIGHT, AND THE LOCAL PATH DID NOT MATCH IT.** `agentd`'s `computeDiscoverySnapshot` (the `agentd.localDiscovery` sweep) scans locks with `pidLooksClaude` — pure /proc — and spawns nothing. But the orchestrator-side `discoverClaudeSessions` ran `getTmuxPaneMapAsync` + `findTmuxTargetAsync` per lock **on the devSnap branch too**, so turning the flag on bought a device round trip and then paid the forks anyway. Both paths now cost the same nothing.

**MEASURED (same fixture, same box, one variable).** 50 locks + 50 live sessions: **101 spawns / 360.8 ms → 1 spawn / 6.6 ms**. At 1.5 GB RSS (Buffer.alloc, touched): **7,140.6 ms → 74.2 ms** cold (the 74 ms IS the one tmux fork) and **3,540.9 ms → 1.0 ms** warm. At userW's shape — 61 locks, no tmux, 1.5 GB — **8,441.8 ms / 123 spawns → 3.0 ms / 0 spawns**, and 4,115.6 ms / 62 spawns → 1.1 ms / 0 on the warm sweep. `spawnSync('/bin/true')` in that same 1.5 GB process: 67.2 ms.

**HONEST BOUNDARY.** The pod itself was not touched (read-only forensics) and no fix was verified against it; what is reproduced here is the MECHANISM at the pod's shape on a box of the same size. The 2,780 zombies on that pod are unexplained by this work and unaddressed by it — they make every `ps`-style read slower but they are not what forked. Nothing changes for ssh/device discovery: `hosts.js` runs remotely, `data/bin/vibespace-usage-scan` is untouched, and boot-restore's sync variants stay (a boot path has no loop to block yet).

**INVARIANTS.**
- **"Async" fixes the WAIT, never the FORK.** A spawn is paid by the PARENT, in proportion to the parent's own memory; measure it at the size the server actually runs at, not at a test harness's 45 MB.
- **A per-item child process on a polled path is a defect, not a cost** — the bound must be per SWEEP. `Promise.all` does not make forks concurrent; it makes them consecutive inside one tick.
- **A cache in front of a fork is a symptom.** Removing the fork removed the 15 s staleness with it; a cache that exists to blunt a cost nobody should be paying hides both.
- **Optional dependencies must be optional at ZERO cost**: probe by statting PATH, never by spawning, and remember the answer.
- **Where two implementations of one fact exist, the cheaper one is the rule** — the daemon's lock scan was already fork-free and the local sweep was not; "hostId is a parameter" is about the DECISION, not just the data.
- **Delete dead code that spells a retired shape**, and prune its allowlist rows with it (test-writer-sweep §17 fails on a dead entry, which is how the two `ps -o ppid=` rows were found).
- **A zero that comes from doing no work proves nothing**: the census asserts the sweep discovered all 55 locks and verified every one RUNNING *before* it asserts the spawn count, and the negative control is `master`'s own module over the SAME fixture (111 spawns) with the same PATH — an emptied PATH would have broken master's identity rung for a reason that has nothing to do with forks.
- **An instrument that patches `child_process` after the module under test destructured it counts zero for ever** — this suite fell into that trap while being written (its wiring pin read `execs=0` with the fix reverted), so the installer is hoisted above every require and two positive controls prove the census can see both a direct spawn and session-store's OWN destructured `execFile`.
- **A fixture binary must be one that survives being renamed**: `/bin/sleep` can be a uutils multi-call binary that dispatches on argv[0] and exits 1, which silently gave the first draft of the no-`procStart` leg five dead pids (a copy of `/bin/sh` blocking on stdin is the shape test-local-discovery-device already uses, for the same reason).

Gates: **test-discovery-spawn** (40 asserts: the /proc reads, the no-/proc rungs DRIVEN over a re-rooted procfs, the census over 55 locks + 50 live sessions, master's own module as the negative control, and four product mutations each verified red) + **test-architecture §45** (the grep-derived spawn census over session-store / discovery-facts / cli-identity, PRINTED, every survivor allowlisted with a reason, dead rows red, runs inside `npm run build`) + test-writer-sweep / test-local-discovery-device / test-discovery-interpret / test-restore-smoke / test-session-schema / test-fixture-isolation unchanged and green.

**r2 — THE SAME QUESTION HAD A SECOND READER, AND IT WAS THE ONE ON THE KILL PATH (2026-09-09, the round-1 verifier; reproduced first).** Round 1 deleted the per-session `pgrep -P` from the discovery sweep and left its TWIN: `refreshWebuiPids()` in server.js loops `for (const [id, s] of activeSessions)` and runs one **synchronous** `execFileSync('pgrep', ['-P', String(meta.childPid)])` per live session — the SAME "what did this wrapper fork" question, answered the retired way, and it is on the incident's own trigger: `src/ws-handler.js` calls it **IN-BAND on every kill** (between `activeSessions.delete(...)` and `broadcastActiveSessions()`) and `src/ws-create.js` calls it 3 s after every create. Synchronous is strictly worse than the sweep's `execFileP`: the caller waits out the fork AND `pgrep`'s own runtime (~80 ms while it walks a 3,000-process `/proc`), one after another. **MEASURED** on this box, the real loop body vs this branch's `readChildPids`, same fixture (N wrapper processes each with two real children, real meta files), one variable: N=10 → **761.5 ms / 10 spawns vs 0.8 ms / 0**; N=22 → **1,671 ms vs 1.1 ms**; and at the pod's shape — **61 sessions at a real 1,560 MB RSS — 9,180.6 ms / 61 spawns vs 2.3 ms / 0**, with the two arms producing the **IDENTICAL 244 pids**. So after round 1 a create or a kill on that pod still blocked the loop for ~9 s while the sweep it had just fixed cost 3 ms. The comment two lines above the loop had claimed since the cache was written that it needs "no pgrep/process-tree traversal" — **a comment stating an invariant three lines from its violation is a bug report**, and this is that class again.

**AND THE ENFORCEMENT WAS BLIND TO IT BY CONSTRUCTION.** §45's file set was a literal array of three modules and server.js was not in it. Mutation-proved on the real tree: inserting a fresh per-session `execFileSync('ps', ['-p', …, '-o', 'ppid='])` INSIDE `refreshWebuiPids` left the census **`ALL PASS`**, while the same shape inserted into session-store's `findTmuxTargetAsync` went red — so round 1's claims (1) "the per-session `pgrep -P` … DELETED" and (5) "a new per-item spawn reddens the build" were both false for this reader. **A hand-written file list is the tool this class has already defeated** (test-writer-sweep §17 r7 is the first strike; this is the second), so both halves of §45 now DERIVE their scope and PRINT what they walked:
- **(a) THE PID-QUESTION CENSUS, repo-wide.** Every tracked JS file the server or the daemon runs (`git ls-files` through the sanitized git env — never a readdir, because `data/bin` holds a 64 MB untracked `rclone`; the only exclusions are `scripts/`, whose suites deliberately SPELL the forbidden shapes as controls — and an assert proves no in-scope file requires anything from there — and `public/`, the built bundle, whose tracked copy would carry cli-identity's own allowlisted calls under a second filename), every spawn whose argv names a process-table tool (`ps`/`pgrep`/`pidof`/`pstree`/`lsof`/`fuser`, the spawn line plus the two after it so a wrapped call is not missed). **16 sites**, each with a row saying why it is not per-item; that is the half that would have caught server.js. The boot-restore `fuser`/`pgrep -f` rows are named BOOT-ONLY with the reason (`restoreSessions` runs once, before this server serves anybody — deliberately out of scope, since the incident is create/kill/poll).
- **(b) THE SWEEP-MODULE CENSUS.** Every spawn, in the modules that answer the sweep's own named facts — and the file set is resolved by **looking up where each fact is DEFINED** (`discoverClaudeSessions`, `refreshWebuiPids`, `readChildPids`, `readPpid`, `findTmuxTargetAsync`, `getTmuxPaneMapAsync`, `listOpenCodexRolloutPaths`), so moving a function keeps its enforcement and a fact that grew a twin or lost its home goes red in four places at once (verified by renaming `refreshWebuiPids`). This is the half that catches a per-session `git` or `stat` — a fork (a) cannot see by construction.

**WHAT THE SHAPE CENSUS CANNOT SEE, SAID OUT LOUD — r2's answer, whose first half r3 had to correct (see below).** r2 CLAIMED (a) could only miss a spawn that is not a process-table question, and that was wrong: it matched CALLEE NAMES, so a process-table tool spawned through a promisified alias was invisible to it. Since r3 the rule is the TOOL in argv[0], and the blind spot is a tool name held in a VARIABLE. (b) is blind to a module that neither defines a sweep fact nor is one — a per-session `execFile('git', ...)` added to `src/ws-handler.js`'s kill case would pass both. That gap is covered by the OTHER kind of census, which is file-blind on purpose: **test-discovery-spawn counts every `child_process` entry point the real code takes** while a real `discoverClaudeSessions` runs over 55 locks (§3) and while the real `refreshWebuiPids` runs over 20 live sessions (§8), so a fork on either hot path is counted wherever it was written. The shape census is the fast, always-on half; the consequence census is the one that cannot be reasoned around. Neither alone is the enforcement.

**r2 INVARIANTS.**
- **A fix is scoped to the QUESTION, not to the file you found it in.** "Who are this wrapper's children" had two readers; fixing the one in the module named after discovery left the one on the kill path — which is the trigger the incident report actually names.
- **A synchronous spawn costs the fork AND the child's runtime**, so the cheapest-looking call site can be the worst one: `pgrep` walking a 3,000-process `/proc` is ~80 ms of blocked loop that no amount of "it's just one call" reasoning covers.
- **A census's file set must be DERIVED and PRINTED, never typed** — and when the derivation cannot cover a whole class (a `git` spawn is not a process-table question), that is a second census, not a wider regex.
- **A grep-derived scope needs a self-proof**: (a) asserts it really covers the incident's own four modules and that excluding `scripts/` is honest (nothing in scope requires it), because a census that silently narrows is a census of nothing.
- **A negative control on a shared tree is a DELTA.** Both splice controls measure strays-after minus strays-before, so they describe what THEY added and stay diagnostic on a tree somebody else has already broken.
- **server.js is a bootstrap that cannot be required, which is not a reason to leave it untested** — the gate slices the SHIPPED function text out of the file and runs it with its free names injected, with `git show master:server.js`'s copy of the same function as the negative control over the same fixture.
- **A "0 spawns" assert needs the fixture to have something to find**: the leg asserts the shells really forked 2N grandchildren and that both arms name the same 4N pids, so the fix is proven to lose nothing — and it asserts `session._childPid` is still stamped, which is the other thing that function is for.

Gates after r2: **test-discovery-spawn 54** (§8 = the shipped `refreshWebuiPids` over 20 live sessions with two real children each, master's own copy as the control, plus `VS_WEBUI_N`/`VS_WEBUI_RSS_MB` to re-take the pod-shape number) + **test-architecture 135** (§45 rewritten as the two derived censuses with four negative controls; four product mutations verified red: the `ps` reader back in `refreshWebuiPids` reddens BOTH halves, a non-process-table per-session spawn reddens only (b), the reverted product fix reddens §8, and a renamed sweep fact reddens the derivation).

**r3 (the round-2 verifier, reproduced first): the census matched CALLEE NAMES, so the retired shape walked back in through a promisified alias — on the kill path.** r2 derived and printed the census's FILE set and left its CALL-NAME set typed: `SPAWN_CALL` is the alternation `execFileSync|execFileP|execFile|execSync|spawnSync|spawn|execImpl`, each requiring a `(` immediately after, so **`execFileAsync(` and `sh(` match none of them**. Two live process-table spawns were therefore absent from the census's own printed 16-site inventory and carried no reason row — `src/ws-handler.js:1353`'s `execFileAsync('pgrep', ['-f', session.socketPath])` (the file's OWN idiom: defined at :192 and handed to ws-create through ctx at :242) and `src/incident.js:98`'s `sh('ps', ['-eo', 'pid,ppid,lstart,etime,rss,stat,args'])`. **MUTATION-PROVED on the real tree**: a per-session `pgrep -P` loop spelled that way, spliced into ws-handler's kill case between `refreshWebuiPids()` and `broadcastActiveSessions()` — the incident's own trigger — left `test-architecture` **ALL PASS (135)**, `test-discovery-spawn` **ALL PASS (54)** and `test-ws-contract` **ALL PASS (5)**. The file-blind consequence census could not see it either (the fork is inside neither `discoverClaudeSessions` nor `refreshWebuiPids`), and the same alias spelled INSIDE `refreshWebuiPids` was invisible to both halves too — it went red only through the ad-hoc string assert "no `pgrep` survives in server.js code", which exists for exactly one file. **This falsified r2's own stated boundary**, which claimed (a) misses only spawns that are *not* process-table questions.

**THE FIX, BOTH HALVES.**
- **THE DETECTOR DERIVES FROM THE TOOL, NOT FROM THE CALLEE.** The signal this census claims to census is the ARGV, so `TOOL_FIRST` (`<ident>('ps'|'pgrep'|'pkill'|'pidof'|'pstree'|'lsof'|'fuser',`) unions with `SPAWN_CALL` at the site rule — a promisified alias is still a fork. The trailing comma keeps `_trimGapDom('top')`-shaped calls out and `top` is deliberately not in that list; the union still covers `execFile('top', …)`. MEASURED over the same 243 tracked files: **16 sites → 18, zero false positives**, exactly the two live ones. A fifth negative control drives the retired shape twice — as a bare line (the rule) and spliced into ws-handler's REAL kill case (the consequence, `+1` stray).
- **AND THE ROW IS REMOVED RATHER THAN REASONED ABOUT.** The kill path's `pgrep -f <socketPath>` — "which dtach process owns this socket" — now asks THE process reader: `cli-identity.pidsMatchingCmdline`, /proc-first like `readPpid`/`readChildPids`, reading every `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` into ONE reused 64 KiB buffer (a read that FILLS it re-reads that pid whole, so a giant argv is answered rather than truncated), with the no-/proc rung ONE `pgrep -f` for the WHOLE question. Matching is a literal byte substring where `pgrep -f`'s is an ERE, i.e. **strictly narrower** — the safe direction for a caller that SIGTERMs what it finds, and it cannot miss the real holder, whose argv carries the path verbatim; a needle containing a space is matched against the joined form because `/proc` separates argv words with NUL. `src/incident.js` keeps its row with a reason (ONE per HUMAN-TRIGGERED capture, whole-table form, on no poll).

**MEASURED, same box, 3,400 processes, median of 7, one variable** — the scan is FLAT in the server's own size while the fork it replaces is not:

| RSS | this scan (block = wall) | `pgrep -f` block / wall |
|---|---|---|
| 58 MB | 23.7 ms | 2.0 ms / 87.6 ms |
| 559 MB | 23.7 ms | 24.4 ms / 110.6 ms |
| 1,564 MB | 23.6 ms | 69.4 ms / 155.2 ms |

At the shape the incident was reported on (1.6 GB) that is 69 ms of blocked loop down to 24 and 155 ms of latency down to 24. **HONEST COST, stated rather than hidden**: on a freshly booted 58 MB server it is 24 ms of block where a fork was 2 — bought back four times over in end-to-end latency, and a predictable cost that stops growing with the server is the whole point of the class this fix belongs to.

**r3 INVARIANTS.**
- **A census must derive the thing it claims to census.** r2 derived the FILE set and left the CALL-NAME set typed, so the same class walked back in one alias away: the signal here is the tool in argv[0], not what the caller named its wrapper.
- **State the boundary as narrowly as the code allows, and re-state it when it moves.** With the fix, (a) sees a process-table tool spelled as a LITERAL whatever the callee is called, and is blind to a tool name held in a VARIABLE; (b) is still blind to a module that neither defines a sweep fact nor is one. Both gaps are covered from the other side by the file-blind consequence census, which now counts the kill-path socket lookup too.
- **Prefer removing a row to reasoning about one.** An allowlist entry is a promise every future reader must re-check; a rung that starts no child process is a property.
- **Publish the trade, including the shape where the fix is worse.** A flat cost that beats a growing one at the size the product actually runs is a decision, and a decision has to name what it gave up.
- **An assert that reads `undefined === undefined` is not an agreement** — §9's "both arms found the same pids" now requires the arms to have found the pids at all (it passed hardest when neither ran).
- **Slice by the anchor that is unique to the region you mean.** `if (session.socketPath) {` also opens the broken-stdin detector 780 lines earlier; slicing THAT one builds a body full of names the leg never injects, i.e. a red that says nothing about the fork under test.
- **A census reads CODE**: the comment above the fix names `pgrep -f` on purpose (it says what this replaced and what it cost), so the leg blanks whole-line comments before asking whether a `pgrep` survives — otherwise the sentence explaining the fix fails the fix.

Gates after r3: **test-discovery-spawn 65** (§9 = the SHIPPED kill-path body sliced out of ws-handler and run with a fake `process` that records SIGTERMs instead of sending them, over three real processes carrying the needle, with `git show master:src/ws-handler.js`'s copy as the negative control on the same fixture — 0 spawns / 23.6 ms against 1 spawn / 84 ms, the IDENTICAL pid set, the session's own attach pty excluded, plus the no-/proc rung driven over a re-rooted procfs) + **test-architecture 139** (TOOL_FIRST, the two new rows, negative control ⑤ in both forms, and the tightened boundary). Product mutations verified red: master's kill-path body back in place reddens §9 twice (ALL PASS 65 → 2 FAILED); the verifier's own alias mutation in the kill case reddens §45 twice by name and line; removing `TOOL_FIRST` from the site rule reddens exactly three asserts (the dead `src/incident.js` row plus both halves of control ⑤).

## A PARSE THAT COULD NOT SEE A CAP RETIRED IT (2026-09-09, quota-model r6 — found by an adversarial verifier against this branch's own r5)

**THE SHAPE.** r5 gave a producer the right to RETIRE a model-scoped limit the cache file holds and the current read does not name (`authoritativeScopes`), because `mergeLimitSets` can only ADD and a cap the vendor stops reporting was otherwise resurrected for ever with its last, possibly exhausted, number. The gate was `u.scopedWeekly?.length ? ['model'] : null` — **a fact about the ARRAY**: it says a model cap was seen, never that ALL of them were.

Seven call sites are fed by THREE parsers. Driven over ONE account state — plan 20 %/20 %, a Fable cap at 10 %, and a `seven_day_opus` at **100 % stated with no `resets_at`** — they answer differently:

```
parseCliUsageText      -> plan, model:fable, model:opus     (the ⟳ CLI-panel rung, 1 of 7)
parseOAuthUsage        -> plan, model:fable                 (the bare-token ⟳ + the four host legs, 5 of 7)
parseGetUsageResponse  -> plan, model:fable                 (the engine's control-channel probe, 1 of 7)
```

So the panel establishes the spent cap and any of the other six then **retires it**, end to end through the real modules and the real write path: `accountRemaining` `{remaining:0}` → `{remaining:80, known:true}`. That is inc-msof8i22 — "the pool stayed on an account whose Opus was spent" — re-opened by the mechanism built to end its mirror image.

**REACHABILITY IS STRUCTURAL, NOT INCIDENTAL.** The control-channel probe is the FIRST rung of `POST /api/usage/refresh`, ahead of `refreshViaCliPanel`, so on any account with a live local claude chat session the "authoritative" list always came from a parser that omits reset-less named caps. No preserve-merge protects it either: the panel replaces `scopedWeekly` wholesale when non-empty, `writeUsageCacheForKey` falls back to `prev` only when the list is EMPTY, and the bare-token leg hands the parse to the write path verbatim. And the only signal on a real cap deletion was a `console.log`.

**THE FIX — COMPLETENESS IS A PROPERTY THE PARSE STATES.** Each producer counts what it DROPPED (a `weekly_scoped` entry it could not NAME; a bucket-shaped field it could not read a number from; a `Current week (…)` line its own regex did not match) and only a parse that dropped nothing marks itself, through the ONE rule every site asks — `quotaModel.authoritativeScopesOf(parse)`. r5's second condition is kept deliberately: an enumerated but EMPTY list still states nothing, because it cannot be told apart from "my scoped parsing broke" and retiring a spent cap is the money-losing direction.

**AND A BUCKET WITHOUT A RESET IS STILL A BUCKET.** Both named-field loops required `v.resets_at` while the array branches beside them in the SAME parsers accept an entry without one — one parser, two answers for one payload shape (measured: 3 of this instance's reset-less scoped anchor readings carry `source: 'control'`, i.e. that array branch really produces them). r3/r4 had already settled what such a bucket means — a STATED SPEND is decisive and counts, it merely may not name a DEADLINE — so the requirement is gone. It is the routine shape: **704 of 5631 scoped readings and 615 of 7978 seven-day readings on this instance carry no reset at all** (692 of the scoped ones from the ⟳ panel). Honest boundary: every reset-less bucket ever recorded here sits at u=0, so the exact spent-and-reset-less combination is LATENT on this instance, not live — but it is the shape 2.305.0 found in a real payload, and two suites' pinned assertions ("a scoped field with no reset is still skipped") were pinning the pre-r3 belief that such a bucket is useless. They are inverted on purpose, each with its essay and a replacement negative control.

**INVARIANTS.**
- **Completeness is stated by the producer, never inferred from a non-empty array.** "I saw one" and "I saw all of them" are different claims, and only the second may delete somebody else's.
- **A claim about a READ must not be expressible in the store that read is written to.** The mark is a NON-ENUMERABLE, SYMBOL-KEYED own property: `JSON.stringify` drops it, a spread drops it, a preserve-merge of a stored file drops it — every one of those losses meaning "no authority", the safe direction — and no JSON a vendor, a device or a file could hand us can FORGE it. (This store's oldest law, from the established-window incident: a fact only ONE producer may state does not belong in the object all the others rewrite.)
- **When the parse result and the write payload are different objects, the gate belongs to the parse** — `refreshViaCliPanel` writes `{...cliPanel, …}` and must ask `cliPanel`.
- **A call site whose failure mode is SILENCE owes a functional test, not another pin.** Asking the spread type-checks, satisfies the wiring pin's shape, and kills the retirement at the one producer that most deserves it; mutation-tested, it reddened nothing else in the suite. §⑱f therefore drives the REAL `refreshViaCliPanel` through the REAL `setupUsage` with a fake `claude` on `CLAUDE_CMD` and reads the consequence.
- **A filter written when a list was ADDITIVE becomes a lie when that list is promoted to an AUTHORITATIVE enumeration.** The `!resets_at` skips predate the retirement by years and were correct then; the promotion is what made them dangerous, and nothing re-read them.
- **A test file's own scratch copies must be git-ignored by a rule that covers the FAMILY, not by one line per section** (r5's `vs-qmr4-mut-*` prefix was never added, so a SIGKILL could strand a file in `src/` that dirties the tree and blocks every push — §⑱0 now asks git itself, and SKIPs loudly with git's own failure when there is no repo to ask).

**GATE.** test-quota-model 293 (§⑱a the cross-producer parity over one vendor state, with the pre-fix skips restored one parser at a time as its negative controls; §⑱b the end-to-end ladder with the verifier's own numbers reproduced by a pre-fix world AND a single-mechanism control showing the parser half is what made the gate lethal; §⑱c completeness is falsifiable per drop shape; §⑱d the mark is per-read and unforgeable; §⑱f the REAL panel refresher, with "ask the spread" as its negative control; §⑱e the retirement line names the claim it drops). Product-source mutation matrix: OAuth parser drop → 8 red, control parser drop → 3 red, authority ignoring the claim → 9 red, unconditional completeness → 1 red, panel site asking the spread → 2 red, log line dropping the numbers → 1 red; restored → 0.


## A WALL LANDED ON THE MEMBER THE TURN STARTED ON (inc-mttbrtc0-6049, 2026-09-08 23:47Z, production journal + anchors)

**THE SHAPE.** The pool re-pointed one session's credential link twice inside a SINGLE turn — `data/slot-transitions.jsonl` carries all three rows, per session, with timestamps (23:44:48, 23:46:00, 23:46:36). The CLI re-read the credentials (2.1.257's `rpe()` re-reads on mtime, which is exactly what a re-point bumps) and its NEXT request, made with the member we had just moved TO, came back rejected with that member's own 5h window ("resets 8:30pm"). `demoteWalledAccount` attributed it to the TURN-PINNED slot — the member the turn had STARTED on — and wrote it with `captureRateLimitEvent(source:'wall')`, which had **no window check at all**. The anchor streams recorded the whole thing (identities anonymised):

```
victim      23:43 on-demand         5h u=0.91  resets 00:19   <- its own
            23:46 rate-limit-event  5h u=1     resets 00:20   <- its own wall
            23:48 WALL              5h u=1     resets 03:30   <- FOREIGN
            23:53 on-demand         5h u=1     resets 00:20   <- owner restored it BY HAND
true owner  23:42 on-demand         5h u=0.84  resets 03:30   <- ITS own window
            23:47 rate-limit-event  5h u=0.84  resets 03:30
```

Two harms, and the second is money: the panel showed the victim "resets 8:30pm" until the owner refreshed manually (he read it as a banked reset being consumed), and the victim was **demoted for three hours on another member's wall** — a usable member excluded from the pool.

**WHY EVERY EXISTING GUARD MISSED IT.** `guardReadingTarget` (reading-lag rule ②) judges by the WEEKLY window only, because 2.369.73 r2 MEASURED that a five-hour reset names a TIME and not an ACCOUNT (47 distinct 5h resets shared across identities — they snap to the clock). That refutation stands and is untouched. And the wall was exempted from the guard outright, for a reason that is half right: a wall's `resetsAt` is often a bounded GUESS (`now + 5h`), and a guess compared against a real window differs every time, so guarding walls would have archived every exhaustion mark on the instance. The turn pin itself was justified by "a re-point cannot reach a running CLI" — which **the readings-by-slot essay itself refutes**.

**WHAT IS ACTUALLY SOUND.** A window that is RUNNING cannot change its reset before it ends. So a stated reset that contradicts the target's own still-future window REFUTES that target — and refutation needs no uniqueness, which is exactly why the r2 measurement does not bite. But refutation identifies NOBODY. Measured over every anchor stream on this instance (comparisons where the previous stated reset was still in the future at the next reading): a RUNNING 5h reset moves >120 s in **4.31 %** of consecutive readings (105/2434) and a running 7d in **1.46 %** (99/6783), while an EMPTY one moves in **32.97 % / 41.13 %** — which is B-8b12, and is why `windowState` fences empty windows off first. Inspected, that running-window residue is dominated by two things that are not "the window moved": the banner's own bounded guess, and the **A-B-A signature of this very defect** (a foreign reading lands and the account's own next reading puts it straight back — the victim's `+11400s` wall followed by `-11400s` on-demand above IS the incident). We do not get to call the residue zero.

**THE FIX — TWO INDEPENDENT WITNESSES, AND ONLY THEIR AGREEMENT MOVES A BYTE.** The LEDGER identifies (`slotAt(webuiId, <when the rejection arrived>)` — our own first-class record of who held that link at that instant, and the evidence the turn pin throws away); the WINDOW only corroborates. Either silent ⇒ the turn pin stands, unchanged. They disagree ⇒ write NOTHING and say why, because refusing can never write a foreign window onto a member. Only a vendor-STATED reset counts (`parseLimitBanner` returns `{kind}` and no time, so the banner path is untouched and the exemption's real half is preserved). The decision is PURE (`quotaModel.wallAttribution`); the engine composes it in `wallTargetFor`. **Signals are attributed BEFORE they are aggregated**: merging by kind and keeping `Math.max(resetsAtMs)` IS the mechanism — the two rejections above (a legitimate 00:20 at 23:46 and a foreign 03:30 at 23:48) collapsed into one bucket that then carried the later member's window and was written onto the earlier one. **The demotion follows the write**, because fixing the panel while leaving the demotion behind would fix the cosmetics and keep the cost. A refused wall is ARCHIVED with its reason into the same archive-never-destroy store the window guard uses, so one grep answers "what did we refuse and why" for readings and rejections alike.

**INVARIANTS.** A REJECTION IS A READING — it is evidence about itself and gets the same by-window attribution as a value reading; being the only producer nobody checked is how it became the only one that could write a foreign window unopposed. A rule that can REFUTE is not thereby a rule that can IDENTIFY — say which one you have, and let the thing that identifies do the identifying. When two witnesses disagree, the safe action is the one that writes nothing, not the one that picks a favourite. A guess is not a statement: only a vendor-STATED value may be read as evidence, and the producer that states none must keep the old path exactly. Attribute per SIGNAL, then aggregate — an aggregation that runs first silently merges two members' facts into one. And a fix that moves money must be asserted on the money: the regression drives the REAL producer twice with a real re-point between them and asserts BOTH halves (the window and the demotion), with a PRE-FIX patched copy of the product module as the negative control, which reproduces the incident. scripts/test-readings-attribution.mjs §18 + §18b (417).

**ROUND 2 — the adversarial verifier found three, all reproduced first, all measured.**

**① THE INCIDENT'S OWN DAMAGE STILL LANDED, because a rejection is written TWICE.** Round 1 put the attribution rule on the turn-end aggregate (`demoteWalledAccount` → `wallTargetFor`). But a rejected `rate_limit_event` is also written the instant it arrives, per RECORD, in `recordRateLimitEvent` — that write is what makes the pool act in the same tick — and that one asked nothing: `guardReadingTarget` is structurally unreachable for a rejection (`win` is deliberately `null`), so the foreign 5h window landed on the pinned member exactly as before, and the turn-end pass, which correctly filed the wall on the true owner, had no reason to go back and repair a bucket it never touched. **The round-1 suite could not see it because its fixture sent BOTH rejections on the SAME bucket**, so the pin's own turn-end write happened to overwrite the foreign value. On two different buckets — a member walling on its weekly while the NEXT member walls on its five-hour, which is what a mid-turn re-point produces — the victim keeps a stranger's reset: measured on the real engine, `victim.fiveHour = {utilization:1, resetsAt:<the other member's>}`, `accountRemaining` `{remaining:0, known:true}`, ~3 h of exclusion. That is the incident, after the round-1 fix. **The fix is `wallAttributionFor`, the rule over KEYS, asked by both writers** (`wallRecordTarget` per record, `wallTargetFor` at the aggregate) over the same two witnesses, so the two writes of one rejection can never disagree about where it belongs — and attributing at the WRITE is what makes a repair unnecessary: nothing foreign is ever stored to be repaired. A rejection we refuse is archived under its own moment (`rejection:<bucket>`, distinct from the turn-end `wall:<bucket>`), because refusing both writes is two facts, not one line written twice; the session-scoped half (pool switch, wall signal) still runs on the archive path, since we refused to write a NUMBER, not to notice a wall. **And the whole rejection moves together.** One wall reaches us through up to three producers and only the `rate_limit_event` carries a window — both banner paths state no time at all (`parseLimitBanner` returns `{kind}`; owner ruling: never parse text for times), so they have no evidence of their own and fall back to the turn pin, i.e. the member the record write has just PROVED innocent. Making the rule per-record without this would have marked TWO members for ONE rejection. A proven re-file is therefore recorded on the session for the rest of the turn (`_turnWallRefile`), the banner follows it and says so, and it dies in `noteTurnEnd` beside the other two turn pins — because a re-point only reaches the CLI on its next request. With no proof this turn the banner marks the pin exactly as it always has: this is a redirect on evidence, not a new rule. **Two things went wrong while wiring that and both are now mutation-tested.** (i) The proof must move the WRITE and not the SIGNAL: `demoteWalledAccount` resolves `member` — and therefore the identity group every earlier signal is filtered against — from the LAST signal's key, so redirecting the banner's `noteWallSignal` silently dropped the pinned member's OWN legitimate wall from the entire turn-end pass (measured: `sessionWalledMembers` held only the re-file target, and the victim's own weekly demotion never happened). The signal keeps the pin's key; the turn-end pass reaches the same destination by asking the same proof. (ii) The proof may only be cleared AFTER the demotion, which is its last reader — clearing it in `noteTurnEnd` beside the other two pins, where it obviously belongs, put the banner's mark straight back on the member the turn had just proved innocent.

**② NOT EVERY WINDOW REFUTES EQUALLY WELL — round 1 asserted the residue was small and cited a number measured on a different quantity.** Round 1's 4.31 % measured the drift between two CONSECUTIVE readings seconds apart; the rule actually compares a reading against its account's own last `on-demand` stamp (what `establishedWindows()` serves from `.window-<key>`), which can be hours old. Re-measured with the predicate the rule uses, over all 7798 claude anchor rows on this instance, empty-window fence applied, each account judged against its own stamp:

```
                  judged  refuted           of the refuted: states ANOTHER
                                            account's own window at that instant
7d      own producer   4109       0            —
        session prods  1160       0            —
scoped  own producer   4029       0            —
        session prods   700       0            —
5h      own producer   1706      13  (0.76 %)  —
        session prods  1055     137 (12.99 %)  101  (73.72 %)
```

A **weekly** window (7d, and every model-scoped bucket, which is weekly by construction) has never once contradicted its own account, across 9998 judged readings, seven identities and a month — the same conclusion 2.369.73 r2 reached from the other direction. A **five-hour** window is EVIDENCE, not proof: three quarters of the readings it refutes state another account's own window at that instant (true positives, mis-filings of exactly the kind this rule catches), and the honest bound on the residue is the OWN-PRODUCER row — 13 readings where an account's own `/usage` panel, which cannot be mis-filed (its key and its credential dir are one decision), contradicted its own previous stamp. Inspected, all 13 are ONE account alternating A-B-A-B between two resets 90 minutes apart at a constant 0.89 utilization: that is not a window moving, it is **B-9213 inside the plan bucket** — more than one limit collapsed into one field. So `refutationStrength(kind)` is now a function of the window kind and nothing else: `decisive` (weekly — may refuse a write on its own), `corroborating` (five-hour — may only confirm an identification the LEDGER made independently), `none` (anything unmeasured; a new backend's bucket must earn `decisive` with its own measurement, never inherit claude's). **The asymmetry is the point, and it is money in both directions**: refusing a wall is not the free conservative option it looks like — a mark that never lands leaves the pool sending turns to a member the CLI has just refused, which is what `guardReadingTarget` warned about when it exempted walls in the first place. At 13 % of 5h walls, round 1 would have bought the panel fix with a new leak (reproduced end to end: with every kind decisive, an ordinary 5h wall on a member whose panel stamp disagrees is REFUSED — `utilization` stays 0.1 and the journal says "refusing to mark"). The incident itself is a five-hour wall and is still fixed, because there the ledger identifies independently and the window only corroborates.

**③ THE SHIPPED STATUSLINE DELETED THE CANONICAL HALF — the sixth loss on one line.** `data/bin/vibespace-usage` is the highest-frequency writer on the instance (up to once per 8 s per account) and an allowlisted exception to the one write path, because it ships as a single file to hosts with no checkout. It rebuilt the cache object from a hand-written list of fields to PRESERVE, and that list did not name `limits`. Measured against the REAL tool over a migrated copy of a real cache file: **three limits in, zero out**, the `overage` object gone with them, and the next reader re-deriving the limits under THIS producer's name and clock — so the model claimed a model-scoped Fable cap had been measured by the statusline at a time the same file's `scopedFetchedAt` contradicts, and the panel section that renders per-limit provenance went blank for exactly the account class this producer touches. That preserve list has now been forgotten six times on this one file: `scopedWeekly` (2.70.0), the org identity, `spend` (B-87fe), `corroborated`, the established window (which is why THAT moved to a sidecar), and `limits`. **The fix is not a seventh entry.** The tool no longer enumerates what to keep — it SPREADS the stored object, states only what it measured, and DROPS the one limit it is able to measure (the plan one), whose reading it has just written into the legacy buckets, where `liftCacheObject` reconstructs it stamped with that object's own `source`/`fetchedAt` — the statusline's own, and the only provenance it is entitled to. Every other limit survives byte-identical with the provenance of the producer that DID measure it; a producer that cannot express a window state or another limit's age can never forge one; and the tool never AUTHORS the array on a not-yet-migrated file (a one-element list claiming to be all of them would delete that file's scoped cap the moment anything lifted it). One field is deliberately NOT inherited: `corroborated` is a verdict about a different reading, and this producer has no observation to re-earn it. The reconstruction rung is claude-only — codex names its own limits and none of them is called `plan`.

**ROUND-2 INVARIANTS.** **A rule belongs at every moment the thing it judges is written, not only at the one you were looking at** — count the writers before deciding where the guard goes (this one had two, three tests away from each other). **And when one fact reaches you through several producers, moving one of them means moving all of them** — the producers that carry no evidence must follow the one that does, or a fix that stops marking the wrong member starts marking two. **But move the WRITE, not the bookkeeping the aggregate identifies the subject from** — a key that also selects which other facts are in scope is not a free thing to redirect. **And a piece of per-turn evidence dies after its LAST reader, not where its siblings die**: `_turnWallRefile` looks like the two turn pins and is cleared two statements later than they are, because unlike them it is consumed by the turn-end pass itself. **A fixture whose two inputs collide can hide the defect it was written for**: §18's two rejections shared a bucket, so a later write masked the earlier one; §18d sends them on different buckets and the pre-fix negative control reproduces the incident. **A measurement is only evidence about the quantity it measured** — "consecutive readings seconds apart" is not "a reading against a stamp that can be hours old", and a number quoted for the wrong quantity is worse than no number, because it ends the argument. **Separate a rule's TRUE positives from its false ones before calling it unsound**: three quarters of the 5h refutations were the defect being caught; the honest bound came from the one producer that cannot be mis-filed. **Refusing is not automatically the safe side** — name the harm in BOTH directions and measure the rate at which you would inflict each. **Evidence has a strength, and the strength belongs to the evidence's KIND, not to the caller's judgement**; an unmeasured kind is worth nothing, so the next backend has to measure its own. **A list of what to KEEP is a promise every future edit must remember; a spread of what was there is a property** — after the second loss, invert the default instead of extending the list. **A producer may only stamp provenance on a number it actually took** — the cleanest way to guarantee that is to make it structurally unable to express the others. **An assert that cannot fail is not an assert**: the first version of the five-hour control ran in a fixture that stamps no 5h window, so the rule short-circuited on "no evidence, no refusal" and it passed whatever the strength rule said — it now asserts its own precondition first, and goes red under mutation. **A wiring pin should freeze the mechanism, not the count**: "exactly two call sites carry `slot:`" froze how many rejection paths exist rather than what each must do, and the third one broke it; it now asserts that no wall signal resolves the slot freshly, and that a site with no slot to state carries its lane instead.


**ROUND 3 — THREE WAYS A COUNTING BUCKET STOPPED COUNTING (2026-09-09, an adversarial verifier on this branch; each reproduced end to end before being fixed).** The model exists to stop an EMPTY window being read as evidence. Round 3 is its mirror: three mechanisms that dropped buckets which WERE evidence, each costing money in the same shape — the bucket vanishes from `accountRemaining` / `weeklyDeadline` / `bucketRems` / the anchor stream, and the pool keeps spending against a member the CLI has already refused.

**① A STATED SPEND WAS NOT DECISIVE.** `windowState` read the reset BEFORE the utilization (`if (used == null || resetsAt == null) return 'unknown';` ahead of `if (used > 0) return 'running';`), so "we know the spend but not the deadline" collapsed into `'unknown'` — and `bucketCounts` drops `'unknown'` exactly as it drops `'empty'`. Reachable on ordinary data, not a corner: the `· resets` clause of a claude panel line is OPTIONAL (`parseCliUsageText`; scripts/test-cli-usage-parse.mjs pins the reset-less shape), `refreshViaCliPanel` deliberately refuses to project the 5-hour one ("its window starts with the first request; a stale one would be a lie"), `parseOAuthUsage` maps a missing reset to 0 — and **four of the nine live claude cache files on this instance** hold `fiveHour` with no reset. Measured end to end through the real write path: a member 95 % through its burst window read `{remaining: 80}` and `decidePoolSwitch` answered **`healthy`**.
