① **The ceiling did not exist for one of its five declared producers.** The codex reset credit's gate was `if (authorizeSpend)`, and `server.js` never passed `authorizeSpend`/`noteSpend` to `usage-pool-engine.create()` — so in production it was dead code. It was invisible from two directions at once: the census's `GATED_RE` matched `spendGuard` anywhere, and the file it matched is the file that CONSTRUCTS the guard; and the leg that "proved" the gate sent a payload matching **no branch at all**, so its assertion was true for an unrelated reason. Reproduced: with the per-identity hourly ceiling at ZERO the credit is still spent. Fix = the deps are deleted and the producer asks the module-local guard directly; `GATED_RE` matches a CALL, so construction alone no longer counts as asking. *A gate that depends on a dep the one real caller does not pass is a gate nobody has; a census that reads "mentions the guard" is blind to a producer inside the file that builds it; and a leg that never reaches the path proves nothing — it needs a positive control.*

② **An oat-only subscription was refused every unattended turn, permanently, with a factually false reason.** The guard asked `memberLoginState` — the SLOT reader, deliberately oat-blind because re-pointing a symlink cannot hand a token to a running CLI — about an ACCOUNT. For B-211a `oatOnly` (spawnable, serving turns, **no credential file at all**) that answers "missing/unusable", so auto-resume, the Stop nudge, job notifications, peer messages and the reset credit were all refused `identity-cannot-serve` — and unlike the hour/day caps, that refusal never expires. Fix = `accountCredentialState` (the ACCOUNT reader `accountLoginState`, which the panels and the repair already use); the slot reader keeps its own callers. *The credential FILE is not the ACCOUNT, and here the wrong answer is permanent.*

③ **Every budget above 800 was unenforceable.** `MAX_STAMPS = 4 * 200` truncated the ledger while the settings UI offered 2000 and 10000. Measured: 1500 charged spends against a 1000/day cap counted 800 and authorized the 1501st. Fix = retention derived from the live limits (still bounded) AND the clamp lowered to the schema's own `max`, which the suite now re-derives FROM the schema. *The offered range and the enforceable range are one set.*

④ **The ladder charged a full turn for a delivery that opens no turn.** A `notification` steered into a turn already running is folded into it and bills nothing, yet it consumed the identity's hourly budget — so ~12 mid-turn Background Work notifications exhausted the ceiling on zero turns and the next auto-resume continue was refused `hour-cap`. The verifier's proposed fix ("charge only when idle") is recorded as WRONG: claude's cli-inbox and codex's `thread/queue/add` both defer a mid-turn delivery into its own billed turn. Only `notificationDelivery(caps) === 'steer'` + `kind:'notification'` + a turn in flight is free — read off the caps row, never a backend id — and because that is a PREDICTION the charge is WITHHELD, then settled by the wrapper's own `peer_message_result{mode}`. *Over-charging a money ceiling is not the conservative direction: it spends the bound that protects the expensive events on events that cost nothing.*

⑤ **The paid-overage refusal had no staleness bound.** `asOf` was captured and never consulted, so a record that stopped being refreshed refused every unattended turn indefinitely — including the continue for a session that is by definition idle and therefore produces nothing to refresh it with — and `retryAfter` printed an instant in the past. Measured here: seven live records, `asOf` ages up to **33 h on a cache refreshed 4 minutes ago**, and **0 of 7 carrying a `resetsAt`** (so `resetsAt` alone would be inert and a few-hour window would let real money through). Fix = the blocking claim expires on its own passed `resetsAt` or after 7 days, an undated record cannot claim the present, `stated`/`evidence` keep what the record said and which rung expired it, and the refusal detail carries the age. Dormant here by measurement (all seven read `inUse:false`). *Only the claim that BLOCKS needs a date — and it needs one.*

⑥ **(round 2, found while verifying ④) The settlement had no correlation rule, so a Stop made a billed turn free.** The wrapper's `peer_message_result` has six emitters and TWO of the three `ok:false` ones are about a DIFFERENT, earlier message (a queued peer item dropped by Stop, or removed from the queue, handed back so the ladder can re-stash). Round 1 consumed a pending entry on any answer, so a Stop landing between our write and the wrapper's reply shifted the entry, and the frame's REAL answer — `mode:'queued'`, a billed `thread/queue/add` — then found an empty queue and charged nothing. Reproduced end to end against the real ladder + real guard. The split is exact and is now an ASSERT over the wrapper's own emitters (every `ok:true` carries a `mode`; every `ok:false` carries `text` and none), so a mode-less answer settles nothing and the third `ok:false` (the wrapper's own delivery failure) simply strands its frame, charged nothing. Two more halves came out of the same reading: the settle queue tracks EVERY frame written on that rung, charged or not — the wrapper answers in write order, so a queue holding only the predicted-free frames hands a charged peer message's answer to the notification's entry — and a frame stranded past 120 s (>4× the wrapper's own 30 s `thread/queue/add` budget) is DROPPED rather than left to absorb the next message's answer. *A correlation-free settlement must derive its match rule from the PRODUCER's own emitters — and assert that derivation, because it is a claim about someone else's code — and it must track every frame it wrote, or the ordering it relies on is not the ordering it has.*

Each of the six was mutation-tested against the PRODUCT source: reverting a fix turns 1–9 asserts red, restoring it returns ALL PASS. Round 2 also added the controls that would have caught its own blind spots — a permanent negative control for a file that constructs the guard but never asks it (with the retired regex beside it, proving it would have passed), a "the census can read the schema" assert so the schema⇄clamp check cannot go vacuous, and three positive controls on the steer exception so it can never widen silently.

**r3 — THREE MORE (2026-09-09, each reproduced first).** ⑦ **The `Spending` settings category was never rendered, so every ceiling this work added was unreachable.** `SETTINGS_CATEGORIES` is not an ordering hint — it IS `SettingsUI._renderContent`'s render loop (group by `schema.category`, then render by iterating THAT array), so an unlisted category is grouped and dropped. Replaying that loop over the real schema: **118 settings, 108 rendered, 10 NEVER RENDERED** — the seven `spend.*`/`pool.*` rows (every money ceiling, the overage consent, the EDF reserve floor) plus three `opencode.*` rows invisible since they shipped; a search for `unattended` matched 5 rows and rendered 0 sections ⇒ "No settings match your search." There is no second write path (`serverSetting()` reads the sparse `data/settings.json`, which only this UI writes — measured live: 30 keys, none `spend.*`). Live consequences: `pool.reserveFloorPct` defaulted to 15 and changed every switch/placement decision with no way to set it back to 0, and the guard's inbox item, the pool's blocked notice and the overage chip all pointed at "Settings → Spending", a place that could not be opened. Fix = two array entries + **`test-architecture` §44**, a build-time census asserting the CONSEQUENCE (every row reaches a section), pinning the UI's own coupling, failing an EMPTY listed category too, with both controls. ⑧ **The pre-fire money gate still failed OPEN at auto-resume's own layer.** P8 says "at EVERY layer"; §1.4 named two and r2 fixed those two — but `src/server/auto-resume.js` has a third (`catch { gate = true; }` + `.catch(() => deliver())`), and r2 verified the fix by GREPPING the other two files. Driven against the real module: gate `false` ⇒ 0 continues, `true` ⇒ 1 (both controls), **THROWS ⇒ 1 billed continue, REJECTED PROMISE ⇒ 1**. Masked in production today, but the mask lives in two other files and one natural refactor (making the wiring lambda `async`) removes both halves. Fix = one named `gateFailedClosed()` (journal + `spend-gate-error` telemetry), `gate = false`, and a rejection handler that never delivers — as `then`'s SECOND argument, because master's trailing `.catch(() => deliver())` also caught a throw from `deliver()` itself (`save()` on a full disk) and answered it by delivering AGAIN; the delivery's own failure now gets its own line and no retry. The arm survives, exactly as for a `false` verdict. ⑨ **The producer census was FILE-granular**, so a new producer in any already-gated file was invisible — ①'s class one level up, in the four files where the next producer is most likely to land. Reproduced by replaying the shipped body over a scratch file with one gated and one ungated producer: verdict `GATED`, unwired 0. Fix = a per-SITE verdict where "gated" means a gate call is still IN SCOPE ABOVE the site, scope decided by INDENTATION rather than a byte window (measured: the ladder's own gate sits 6,285 chars above its last rung, so any window wide enough for the real code would call the scratch file gated too and the control would be theatre), with whole-line comments blanked (auto-resume's JSDoc `@param deps.authorizeSpend (…)` satisfied the call-shaped regex — r2's rule was true of the regex and false of its input), calls to locally gated helpers counted as anchors, and `ALLOW` keyed by (file, primitive). r3 invariants=**an ordered list a renderer ITERATES is a switch, not documentation**; **a defence-in-depth rule must be verified AT the layer it names — a source regex on two other files proves nothing about the third**; **a census answers per SITE or it answers about the file that happens to contain the site, and its scope rule must be measured against the real code before the control it enables is worth anything.**


**r4 — TWO MORE (2026-09-09, each reproduced first), plus one this branch found by mutation-testing its OWN new leg.** ⑩ **The delivery ladder AUTHORIZED an unattended turn against one credential slot and DEBITED a different one**, so the per-identity ceiling could be bypassed indefinitely on the authorized account while an account nobody asked started refusing its own legitimate unattended turns. `deliverToConversation` resolves `identity` as `session ? null : {…}` — null on the local-session branch by design — and handed that null to the charge, so `spend-guard.note()` ran `identityOf(session)` a SECOND time, at charge time. The rpc rung makes that window wide on purpose: it WITHHOLDS the charge and defers it to `settleRpcDelivery` up to 120 s later, while a codex session's slot follows the pool DEFAULT (the engine's per-session pass skips codex) which the 30 s pool timer re-decides. Measured on the real guard + real ladder with a 1/hour cap and `identityOf` flipping A→B inside that window: **three deliveries authorized against a cap of one**, ledger `{B:3}`, A debited 0 — its ceiling never bound, and B now refuses its own auto-resume continue. The PURE verdict already carried the resolved slot; throwing it away was the whole defect. Fixed at all four pairs (the other three escaped only because of how their code happens to be arranged today, not because the question has one answer), and the guard now COUNTS the shape in production (`chargesUnhinted`, journalled + `spend-charge-unhinted` telemetry) so the rule has a measurement rather than a grep. ⑪ **The design's §1.4 row was marked CLOSED while one of the three fields it names still had zero readers** — `grep -rn spendControlReached src/` returned only its writer. Not a money leak (codex REJECTS those requests rather than billing them), but the leak the row itself describes is real for it: nothing marks a window spent, so both panels drew friendly donuts and `accountRemaining()` ranked an account that cannot serve a single request as the one with the most headroom. The record was the defect, so both halves were fixed: PURE `spendControlState` beside `overageState` (three-state, dated on the SAME `OVERAGE_STALE_MS`, refusing under its own name in the `identity-cannot-serve` class — `spend.allowOverageTurns` deliberately does not unlock it, because that setting consents to SPENDING, not to being refused by the vendor), both panels rendering it from that same verdict, and a census that walks the fields the row CLAIMS and requires the row to name each one's reader. ⑫ **(found by mutation-testing r4's own new leg) An assert that could not fail**: §5c's peer-message leg put its awaiting transport behind a `findPeer` that answered null, so the ladder ran to its charge synchronously and the leg stayed GREEN with the product fix reverted while the four legs around it went red; an order probe printed `authorize → CHARGE → (the driver re-points)` with the transport never called. The rung is now reached, with a control that says so before the money is read.

**r4 invariants**: **CHARGE WHAT YOU AUTHORIZED** — the authorizer is entitled to resolve the slot and its verdict CARRIES it so the charge can name the same one; a charge that re-derives a mutable identity asks the question twice, and two-phase accounting makes that window as wide as the settlement. **A rule that holds "because nothing awaits in between" is a property of an arrangement of code, not of a design** — state it at every pair and give it a production counter, not a grep. **A record that says a thing is finished must name every field it claims**, because that record is what the next round reads. **Only the claim that BLOCKS needs a date**, and a second staleness window for one physical fact is a twin. **An assert that cannot fail is not an assert — including the ones this round wrote**; mutation-test your own new legs, not just the product code they were written for.

**r5 — TWO MORE (2026-09-09, each reproduced first).** ⑬ **The persisted ledger was PRUNED AT BOOT with the DEFAULT limits, so every restart forgave spend an owner-raised cap had counted.** Retention is a function of the caps (r2's ③) — but at the one place it is applied to the ledger on disk, the caps are NOT KNOWABLE: the guard is constructed inside `usage-pool-engine.create()` at `server.js:434`, and `serverSetting` reads `persistenceRouter.readSettings`, which `setupPersistence()` only assigns **318 lines later** (`server.js:752` → `src/routes/persistence.js:600`). Measured against the real router and the real PURE module at that instant: `router.readSettings = undefined`, `serverSetting('spend.unattendedPerIdentityDay') = undefined`, `budgetLimits() = {12, 60, 200, 80}` — the DEFAULTS — so `stampCap` was **264** rather than the live one. Reproduced end to end on the real guard: a ledger holding **500** same-day stamps for one slot with the owner's day cap at 500 (the schema offers 2000, and 10000 for the instance row) came back holding **264**, and the guard then authorized **236 more unattended turns today instead of 0**, on every boot — and this instance restarts several times a day. Pruning can only DROP, so the later `note()` calls (which do use live limits) can never restore them; this is r2's ③ verbatim — *a ceiling the settings UI offers and the ledger cannot count is not a ceiling* — re-opened through the boot path, and the suite missed it because §1 and §3 both build the guard with a WORKING `serverSetting`. Fix = **prune wide at load**: a named `A.LOAD_RETENTION` (`CAP_MAX` + headroom = 10064 stamps, the widest any offerable cap can count), because dropping a stamp is irreversible and forgives money while keeping one costs bounded memory, and the first `note()` re-prunes with the live limits. ⑭ **The delivery ladder authorized SYNCHRONOUSLY and charged only after an awaited transport, so the per-identity hourly ceiling was bypassed by the in-flight concurrency count.** Nothing between the two re-read the ledger and nothing reserved, so N deliveries dispatched in one pass all read the same pre-charge counts. Reachable in production: `src/jobs.js:210` fires `deliverToConversation` fire-and-forget from loops over `this.jobs.values()` (run-completion at :511 and the boot catch-up pass at :593, which can notify several missed jobs in one tick), and `/api/agent/msg/send` is a concurrent HTTP route into the same ladder — while the 30 s `_notifyRate` floor is keyed PER CONVERSATION and therefore does not serialise across them. Measured with the real guard + the real ladder, cap 2/hour, five DISTINCT conversations resolving to ONE credential slot (the design's own "nine conversations can sit on one subscription") dispatched in a single synchronous pass: **5 delivered, 0 refused, 5 turns charged against a cap of 2**; sequentially on an identical world: 2. `chargesUnhinted` stays 0 throughout, so r4's counter is structurally blind to this shape. The other three producers are NOT affected — each runs authorize → act → note with no await between — which is exactly why the fix is in the AUTHORIZER rather than in the ladder. Fix = **an authorization HOLDS what it authorized**: `authorize()` returns `v.hold`, an in-memory reservation that `pendingCounts` adds to the caps exactly like a charge, until `note()` converts it, `release()` gives it back, or it times out at `RESERVE_TTL_MS` (3 min > the ladder's own 120 s settle window, ≪ the shortest window the caps count). A hold is deliberately NOT a stamp: the stash rung — the ladder's normal fallback for an unreachable conversation — opens no turn, and the ladder gives its hold back at **ONE** release point (a `finally`, because enumerating the nine exits plus two throwing catches is the fragility that `finally` removes), while a withheld rpc frame is released when the wrapper CONFIRMS the steer and charged when it says the frame became a turn. Holds are not persisted, on purpose: a restart kills the in-flight deliveries they stand for. Two shapes fell out of the fix and are pinned: **a PROBE must not hold** (auto-resume asks the ceiling twice per fire — before its pre-fire gate and after it, on the slot the continue lands on — and with the first call reserving, the second refused its own request and NOTHING ever fired at cap 1/hour), and **the ladder's rungs must stay in the same function as its gate** (round 1 of this fix split them into a helper, and §2's per-site census went red on all four rungs — correctly: a helper the gate merely calls is exactly the shape r3's census exists to catch).

**r5 invariants**: **do not make an irreversible decision with limits nobody can answer yet** — a module constructed before the settings reader exists must prune to the WIDEST retention its own clamp allows, because the two error directions are not symmetric (over-retention is bounded memory, under-retention is money). **Two-phase accounting is only as strong as the window between the phases**: an authorization that has not yet become a charge must still bind the ceiling, or the bound is per-caller-scheduling rather than per-identity. **A hold is not a charge** — it is released by the exits that opened no turn, and there must be exactly ONE of those. **A "may I?" probe must not consume the thing it is asking about.** **A counter that measures one shape (`chargesUnhinted`) is blind to the next one** — the new shape got its own (`holdsExpired`/`holdsOpen`, asserted 0 across every producer). **When the census goes red at a refactor, read it before working around it**: it was describing the invariant, not the diff.

**GATE**: `scripts/test-spend-paths.mjs` (261, fast tier) + `test-architecture` §44 (inside `npm run build`) — §2 is a grep-derived census over `git ls-files -- src server.js data/bin` minus `src/lib/`, PER SITE since r3, it PRINTS every site with the anchor that gated it, its (file, primitive) allowlist rows die when they stop matching, and its controls are a synthetic producer in a scratch tree (the same producer, wired, is the positive one), a file that constructs the guard without asking it, a mixed file holding one gated and one ungated producer, and a gate that exists only in a JSDoc block. The rest drives the REAL modules — including, since r3, all four arms of auto-resume's own pre-fire gate against a patched copy of the real module. Since r4 it also drives §5c (the charge names the slot the authorization resolved, on all four producers, with a patched copy of the real ladder carrying r3's shipped line — byte-compared against `4ceba626` — as the negative control) and §7b (a census over the fields the design's §1.4 row CLAIMS, with r3's own verdict text as its control). Since r5: §1's reservation rules, §3d (the production boot ordering pinned on the real files, then the ledger driven through it), §5d (five concurrent deliveries on one slot, with the sequential run as the positive control) and §5e (the probe, the withheld steer and the hold census) — each with a negative control that is a PATCHED COPY of the real module, and all six mutations (boot prune / no hold / no release / probing holds / no give-back at settle / no give-back when stranded) verified red before restoring.

## GATE SUITES CLAIMED MACHINE-GLOBAL NAMES (2.369.76, heavy tier RED on 40ad936d)

**THE SHAPE.** The heavy tier for 2.369.75's commit went red on test-chat-paging: first `npx esbuild` (unminified rebuild in the suite's worktree) failed with no stderr, then the retry's `git worktree add --detach /tmp/vs-chatpage-smoke HEAD` failed with status 128. Neither is a product failure. The suite's worktree path, chrome profile dir (`/tmp/vs-chatpage-chrome`) and ports (3990/9340) were fixed strings; a verifier agent — told, as every verifier is, to run the gate suites from its own checkout — ran the same suite in the same minute. Its `git worktree remove --force` deleted the heavy run's tree mid-build, and its `worktree add` recreated the path before the heavy run's retry got there. The heavy tier's machine lock (2026-09-07 round 2) serialises heavy RUNS; it cannot cover a process that never takes it.

**THE CENSUS.** 28 suites carried the shapes: 23 with quoted `/tmp/vs-<name>` literals, 17 with `const PORT = NNNN`, three suites sharing 3989/9339, four sharing 3991/9341, five using `3951 + (process.pid % 20)` (a 1-in-20 collision, and two of them share one range). 2.369.46 had fixed exactly one (test-chat-e2e :3995) inline — the third strike is where an idiom becomes shared: `scripts/scratch.mjs` (`scratch(name)` = `/tmp/vs-<name>-<pid>`; `freePorts(n)` holds every listener open until all are chosen, because closing each before the next `listen(0)` can hand the same port back). It is deliberately not a `test-*.mjs` — the tier census would demand a tier for it.

**THE GUARD.** test-ci-gate §6 already had `machineGlobalFixtures` (the 2026-09-07 detector: a literal that IS a port, a `/tmp` literal that reaches the filesystem) but scanned FAST-tier suites only — the tier that collided was the other one. It now scans both tiers, and pins that the scanned set contains the heavy tier and the suite that collided. The detector's own fixtures (scripts/fixtures/machine-global-shapes/) are the controls; test-ci-gate's `/tmp/vs-ack-smoke` assertion is a control READING a fixture, not a claim, and the rewrite reverted its accidental hit.

**INVARIANTS.** A lock covers the processes that take it and nothing else — when the colliders are other agents, the fix is a name nobody shares, not a bigger lock. A suite's scratch names are part of its correctness on a shared box. The second inline fix of a class is the signal to make the idiom shared and the detector tier-wide.

## A MEMBER BECAME USABLE AND NOBODY NOTICED (2026-09-08 02:00–02:31, from this instance's own journal)

**THE SHAPE.** Pool "全部" hit 0 % on every member. The owner added a subscription in the middle of it. Eight fable conversations were parked on the newcomer **while it had no usage reading at all**, re-armed for a reset **eight hours away**, and released only when the owner typed a prompt into one of them seven minutes later.

**THE JOURNAL, VERBATIM** (`journalctl --user -u vibespace`, account ids resolved from `data/accounts.json`; the newcomer `sub-000000000005` was named "39AI Max" that night and is "Member U" now):

```
02:00:02 [notice] Pool "全部": no member can serve it — spent: 5h 0%
                  (still available: 7d 62%, Fable 34%)…
02:03:29 data/accounts.json: the record is created
02:18:27 data/subs/<new>/.vibespace-login-status.json: {"state":"success"}
02:18:33 [pool] per-session switch <pool>/<sid>: <old> → <new> (fam=fable, from 0%)   ×8
02:18:33 [notice] Pool "全部": conversation "<name>" moved to 39AI Max
                  (its fable quota was at 0%)                                          ×8
02:18:37 [auto-resume] sess-5-…: armed for 2026-09-08T10:20:00.000Z
           (re-armed at fire: 39AI Max: no usage data | Member F: 5h 0% < 10% |
            Member Q: Fable 0% < 5% | Member P: 5h 0% < 10% |
            Member B: 7d 1% < 5% | Member Y: 7d 0% < 5%)
02:18:40 [auto-cli] quota refresh <new>: failed (drift 0pt)
02:22:10 [auto-resume] <opus sid>: pool switched to Member U — continued automatically
02:25:01 [auto-resume] sess-5-…: disarmed (user sent a prompt)
02:31:43 [auto-cli] quota refresh <new>: ok (drift 4pt)
```

The opus conversations at 02:22:10 are **not** the feature working: one of their turns ended, and a turn end is the one event that already re-evaluates the pool. The fable ones had nothing to end.

**THE POOL NEVER GOT TO DECIDE.** It re-evaluates on turn ends, on streamed usage records, and on a 30 s timer for auto pools. A conversation that is ARMED on exhaustion produces neither of the first two, and the timer only re-ranks the pool — it never re-examines who is waiting. So "a member became usable" reached nothing:

* **(A) A LOGIN SUCCEEDING IS ALSO A QUOTA EVENT, and nothing treated it as one.** Nothing read the newcomer's usage when its login landed. The auto-cli loop is the only other reader, and its one attempt at 02:18:40 failed, which doubled its backoff from 5 to 10 minutes — the next read was 02:31:43, thirteen minutes later, exactly as `5*60e3 * 2^1` predicts.
* **(B) A MANUAL REFRESH THAT REVEALS A USABLE MEMBER TOLD THE POOL NOTHING.** `/api/usage/refresh` writes the reading and answers `{success:true}`. It re-runs no pool decision and does not touch the conversations armed on exhaustion. The owner clicked it, watched the number change, and the conversations kept waiting for 10:20Z.
* …and at 02:31:43, when the auto-cli read finally succeeded, **that changed nothing either** — a cache write is not an event anybody subscribes to.

**THE FIX IS ONE EDGE, because a third producer of a fresh reading would otherwise forget it too** — and in this incident the third producer *had* forgotten it. `engine.onMemberReadingFresh(memberId, why)` (src/server/usage-pool-engine.js) does exactly two things:

1. **RE-DECIDE the pools this member belongs to** — `maybePoolAutoSwitchForPool(id, {force:true})`. `force` drops that pool's 10 s event-KICK gate for this one call: the gate throttles per-RECORD kicks, and a member's first reading is not a kick, it is the fact the whole decision was missing. The 180 s dwell belt is untouched, so this can no more oscillate than any other evaluation.
2. **RE-EXAMINE the conversations that are WAITING** — every armed session on this member, or on one of its pools, goes through auto-resume's own `fireNow`.

**Each half is load-bearing for a DIFFERENT shape, and finding that out took mutation testing.** The first spelling of the regression armed the conversations in both legs — and half ① then looked decorative, because deleting it changed nothing: the PRE-FIRE GATE (`beforeAutoResumeFire`) runs `maybePoolAutoSwitch` itself, so half ② was doing half ①'s work as a side effect of the spending machinery. The honest split is:

* **half ② is the incident's own shape**: the conversations were ALREADY parked on the newcomer since 02:18:33, so the pool re-decide moves nobody — the per-session pass `continue`s long before it reaches its own `fireNow`, and a switch-driven nudge is structurally unreachable.
* **half ① is the shape where nobody is armed**: two live conversations on a spent member that have not hit the wall yet. Nothing fires, so the gate never runs, and only half ① can move them.

`engine.onMemberLoginSuccess(memberId, why)` is the login half: ONE read through the EXISTING caps-routed rung (`probeQuotaForKey` → claude's `refreshViaCliPanel`, i.e. `claude -p /usage` with the official binary making the fetch; codex's app-server twin), then the same edge. **No new vendor surface**, and it is the human's own login action that triggers it — the same class as the ⟳ button, not a timer.

**IT DOES NOT SPEND BY ITSELF.** Every release goes through `fireNow → attemptFire`, i.e. the 2026-09-07 loop breaker (same-identity quarantine, 3/hour cap, backoff) AND the pre-fire gate that re-verdicts the target and vetoes the turn if it is still blocked. There is no bypass, by construction: this module only says "look again".

**THE PRODUCERS, all three.** `src/server/account-usage-routes.js` hangs the login half on the SAME transition detection the expiry sweep already uses — `loginFingerprint` read off THE ANSWER THE ROUTE ALREADY HOLDS, never a second credential reader — because all three finalize routes are polled every 3 s for up to 5 minutes and a side effect hung on the raw answer would spawn ~100 CLI panels per login. It gets its OWN latch (`lastWakeFingerprint`), deliberately not the sweep's: two of those routes call `sweepLoginExpiry` UNCONDITIONALLY for a documented reason, so sharing one map would couple "we already swept" to "we already read the usage" and disabling either would silently disable the other. All five login-success exits wake **the record the login LANDED in** (`'moved'` ⇒ `movedTo`; the auto-merge exit ⇒ the SURVIVOR, since waking the throwaway spawns a panel read for a record about to stop existing and leaves the one the pool holds unread). The call is fire-and-forget: a login must never wait on, or fail because of, a quota read. `src/usage-routes.js` takes the edge on all three LOCAL rungs of `/api/usage/refresh` (session / cli-panel / bare token); the `host` branch takes none of them — a remote machine's pool is not ours to decide for. And the auto-cli loop in server.js takes it on a successful refresh, which is what makes "a third producer cannot forget it" a true sentence rather than an aspiration. The edge is deliberately NOT buried inside `refreshViaCliPanel`: the pre-fire SPEND GATE calls that function too, and a gate that releases conversations as a side effect of asking a question is not a gate.

**COMPOSITION, NOT A TWIN.** `readingForeignForWake()` asks the window-fingerprint work that landed in 2.369.73 (inc-mts8a8mr-ulmm) whether the stored snapshot is really this member's. That work ships TWO rules and this asks the SECOND on purpose: ① the LAG SHADOW is about a reading ARRIVING on a session after that session's link moved — it needs a session, a re-point and an age, and the producers already applied it before the snapshot reached disk; ② the WINDOW IDENTITY GUARD (`decideReadingTarget`) is the question a wake actually has. It is reached through the same `establishedWindows()` + `windowGroupOf` the write-time guard uses, so there is ONE predicate — and it is deliberately not called "shadowed", because that word already names rule ①. READ-ONLY: `guardReadingTarget` is the WRITE path and it archives; a wake may only decline to act. A brand-new member has no established window for anything to contradict, so the incident's own case still acts.

**"NO READING" IS A REFUSAL TO ACT, NEVER A VERDICT OF 0 %.** The edge names its refusals: `no-reading`, `stale-reading` (older than 10 min), `foreign-reading`, `member-not-usable`, `wake-floor`. It is idempotent and rate-floored per member (20 s), and **the floor is stamped only when the wake ACTED** — a declined wake costs one file read, and stamping it would let a producer that called a millisecond before its own cache write eat the wake the real one needed.

**NOT READY IS NOT FAILED.** The auto-cli loop skipped on the roster's `loggedIn`, which is `parseAuth`'s boolean: TRUE the moment an accessToken *string* exists, whatever its expiry. A credential file whose access AND refresh tokens have both expired therefore reads as "logged in", the panel spawn cannot possibly answer, and every failure doubles a backoff that never had a chance — the satisfiable gap `src/login-state.js` exists to close. The loop now asks `autoCliReady`, which is `memberLoginState`, the credential-FILE reader the pool's slot validation already uses. The FILE reader is the right one and not the account-level `accountLoginState`: this rung is `refreshViaCliPanel`, which DELETES `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` from the child's env and points `CLAUDE_SECURESTORAGE_CONFIG_DIR` at the account's own dir, so a long-lived token cannot serve it. Finally, the login-edge read counts as one of the loop's OWN attempts (`lastMemberReadAt`), because both schedulers spawn the SAME panel through the SAME rung and two attempt clocks race.

**HONEST BOUNDARIES — what this fix is NOT claimed to explain.**
* The brief for this work said the newcomer's `.credentials.json` was written at 02:24, six minutes AFTER the auto-cli failure. **That is not verifiable today and the available evidence points the other way.** The file's mtime is now 12:19 (the pool re-mints that symlink and utimes-bumps the target on every re-point — which is exactly why `credsTokenSig` exists instead of an mtime check), and the loop's own `!a.loggedIn` gate means an accessToken string must already have been on disk at 02:18:40 or no attempt would have been made at all. The sibling files agree: `.claude.json` and `.vibespace-login-status.json` are both stamped 02:18:27.
* So `autoCliReady` is **not** claimed to be what would have prevented that particular failure count. It closes an adjacent, reachable hole (the doubly-expired file), and the "member has no credentials yet" half was already covered — `parseAuth` answers `loggedIn:false` with no file, and the journal shows no auto-cli attempt for the newcomer before its login landed. Both halves are measured in the suite so the distinction cannot rot into a claim.
* What IS reproducible, and what the regression actually replays, is the load-bearing half: **after the reading existed, nothing re-drove the pool and nothing looked at the conversations that were waiting.**

**INVARIANTS.**
* **A member becoming usable is an EVENT, not a state someone will eventually poll.** The pool re-evaluates on turn ends and streamed records; a conversation that is armed produces neither, so it can wait out a reset that stopped being relevant hours ago.
* **Every producer of a fresh reading takes the SAME edge** — and "a third producer cannot forget it" is only true if you go and wire the third producer that already exists.
* **A wake has two halves and each needs its own control, in the shape where only it can answer.** If a control survives deleting the mechanism it names, the mechanism was being done by something else and the assertion was decorative.
* **A polled route may not carry an unbounded side effect** — gate on the credential fingerprint of the answer the route already has, never on a second read of the file; and give a second side effect its own latch, or disabling one silently disables the other.
* **Two belts get two controls.** A leg that leaves the second belt standing proves nothing about the first (the fingerprint gate looked protected until the engine's own 5-minute floor was wound back).
* **When two schedulers spawn the same child through the same rung they must share ONE attempt clock.**
* **A boolean that is TRUE for a state it was never asked about is a gate that spends money** (`parseAuth.loggedIn` vs `loginState.usable`).
* **"No reading" is a refusal to act, never a verdict of 0 %** — and a refusal names itself.
* **A rate floor is stamped when you ACT, not when you are asked.**
* **Do not reuse an established word for a different rule.** Rule ① is the shadow; rule ② is the guard. One name for two things is how this subsystem grows twins.

**r2 — THE WAKE COULD SPEND ON A MEMBER IT HAD JUST PROVEN SPENT (adversarial verifier; all three reproduced before they were fixed).**

**(a) HALF ② FIRED CONVERSATIONS THE POOL COULD NOT MOVE.** Round 1's half ② filtered on POOL MEMBERSHIP and fired. That is right only in the shape it was written for. In every shape where the pool structurally CANNOT move a conversation onto the newcomer, the continue went out anyway — billed to the member the conversation was already stuck on — and the in-chat card named THAT member as recovered, which is this batch's own invariant ("a card explaining a billed turn must name the thing that caused it") inverted. Three real production states reach it:

* a **MANUAL pool** (`auto:false`): `maybePoolAutoSwitchForPool` returns at the top, so half ① is a no-op and the link never moves;
* a **REMOTE conversation** (`s.host`): the per-session pass skips it BY DESIGN, same outcome;
* a **COLD pool** (`hot:false`) where half ① did move the link: the pool broadcast `pool-auto-switched` so the CLIENT restarts the conversation, and master's ONLY other `fireNow` call site guards that same spend with `if (a.hot)` and says why. Round 1 had no `hot` gate at all.

MEASURED on the real engine + real AccountManager pool + real per-session symlinks + real auto-resume, armed by the REAL rejection producer (`recordRateLimitEvent` + `noteTurnEnd`): with a manual pool, master's own mechanisms (`maybePoolAutoSwitchForPool` + three auto-resume ticks) deliver **0** continues and the link stays on the spent member; one call to `onMemberReadingFresh` delivers **1**, onto a member whose cache reads `utilization: 1, status: "limited"`, with the card `账号 Member F 已恢复可用，已自动继续这个任务。` The remote shape is identical. **The pre-fire gate cannot catch any of them**: `quotaVerdictFor` answers a POOLED scope with "any member usable" — true, and beside the point.

**THE FIX ASKS THE FACT THIS MODULE ALREADY NAMES.** `fireIdentityFor` is "the identity a CONTINUE fired into this session would land on" — it is auto-resume's own `fireIdentity` dep, so `attemptFire` re-resolves the very same fact. Half ② asks it TWICE: once BEFORE half ① is allowed to move anything, once after. A session is fired only when the continue lands on `memberId`, would have landed there before half ① ran too, and (if its scope is a pool) that pool is HOT. Read only after the re-decide, "the wake moved this session onto the new member" is indistinguishable from "it was already parked there" — and the first of those is a spend half ① has already accounted for: it fires a hot pool itself and restarts a cold one through the client, so half ② adding a second continue there is a double spend, not a rescue. Half ②'s contract collapses to one sentence: **it serves only the conversations nothing could move** — which is exactly the shape round 1's own essay says it exists for.

**WHAT THE COLD RULE COSTS, DELIBERATELY.** A cold pool's conversation is released by the TIMED path at its armed reset rather than early. That is latency; firing anyway is money, spent on whichever member the CLI still holds — the restart is a CLIENT action, so with no browser connected nothing restarts, the link says one member and the running CLI still holds another's credentials, and nothing in this process knows whether a client obeyed a broadcast.

**(b) THE CARD'S ORDERING WAS NOT BYTE-IDENTICAL, CONTRARY TO THE ROUND-1 COMMIT.** Round 1 hoisted the `/^account usable again/` arm ABOVE `kind === 'now'` to make room for `cause`. That silently changed a PRE-EXISTING pair which the engine itself writes both halves of: the near-arm at `scheduleWallProbe` (`armIfEnabled(id, session, Date.now() + 45000, 'account usable again')`) followed by a real pool switch firing with `kind:'now'` (the per-session pass and the pool pass). Measured against `master:src/server/auto-resume.js`: master says `账号池已切换到 X`, round 1 said `账号 X 已恢复可用` — a real pool switch described as a recovery. No suite covered the pair, so the claim was untested as well as untrue. `cause` may only REFINE master's precedence: **`moved` still outranks it** (if the pre-fire gate re-pointed us, the continue lands on a member the wake never spoke about, so "the pool switched to X" is the true sentence), and below `cause` master's order is restored verbatim. All eight reachable pre-existing shapes are now byte-compared against master's own copy, loaded through `git show` under the sanitized git environment. The pair itself is pinned where the other `continueNoticeFor` pairs live — test-auto-resume-loop §3, beside the DRIFT guard that already names both engine strings (that suite is now 191, and the round-1 ordering makes it red on its own).

**HONEST BOUNDARY, IN THE SOURCE AND IN THE SUITE.** Deleting the before/after snapshot changes no outcome TODAY — auto-resume refuses the second fire on its own (`session._arFiring` is raised for the whole gate, and a delivered continue deletes the arm); the mutant delivers the same one continue with the same card. It stays because half ②'s money-safety must not be OWED to another module's private in-flight flag, and because the skip is what makes half ②'s contract testable rather than a sentence in a comment. §8f drives that interlock directly so the claim is not decorative.

**r2 INVARIANTS.**

* **Pool membership is not a spend authorization.** A wake that RELEASES a conversation must prove the continue lands ON the member that became usable — the pooled verdict ("any member usable") is structurally unable to answer that question.
* **A guard master states with a reason at its own spend site is owed at every new spend site.** `if (a.hot)` existed, with its reason written next to it, at the only other `fireNow` call site.
* **Ask the fact the module already NAMES for that question, at both instants that matter.** "Where would a continue land right now" already had a function; the bug was asking a different question (membership) and asking it once.
* **A claim of byte-identity is a MEASUREMENT, not a reading of the diff.** The pair round 1 broke is written by the engine 900 lines away from the function it changed.
* **A redundant guard must say it is redundant, and hand the suite the mechanism that really protects.** Otherwise the next reader either deletes it as dead or trusts it for a safety it does not provide.
* **A declared-but-never-passed test control is an unmeasured shape.** `mkWorld`'s `auto` parameter was documented as a real control in round 1 and no leg ever passed it; all three shapes above live behind exactly such controls.

**r2 GATE.** §8 of the same suite, on the same real engine/pool/symlinks/auto-resume: (a) manual pool, (b) remote conversation, (c) cold pool — each asserting 0 continues, the NAMED skip reason, and no "recovered" card; (d) the incident itself still releases both conversations; (e) the login shape where half ① moves the link and fires it ITSELF, asserting exactly ONE continue worded as the switch it was; (f) the `_arFiring` interlock driven directly, both ways; (g) the card's precedence table plus the eight-shape byte comparison with master. **The negative controls are PATCHED COPIES of the real engine** written beside it (they must be siblings or their relative requires do not resolve), each replacement's hit count asserted, unlinked on exit, swept by PID liveness at start, and gitignored so a SIGKILL can never leave the tree dirty for the release gate. Each of the three guards, removed on its own, goes red; so does round 1's `continueNoticeFor` ordering.

**r3 (adversarial verifier, round 3 — both reproduced on the real engine/routes before anything was changed).**

**(a) HOTNESS IS A CAPABILITY VERDICT, AND HALF ② WAS READING THE USER'S CHECKBOX.** r2's cold gate asked `!pa.hot`. The engine does not: the pool-level path computes `const hot = !!a.hot && poolCaps.hotSwitch === 'verified'` and spends only `if (hot)`, and `capsOf('codex').hotSwitch` is `'impossible'` (the 2026-08-24 experiment — CODEX_HOME is canonicalized at startup and the tokens live in process memory), so a codex pool ALWAYS cold-restarts. So a codex pool carrying `hot:true` got, from the capability gate, exactly the billed continue the pool-level path had just refused — r2's own failure mode arriving through a door r2 did not check. MEASURED on the real engine + real AccountManager codex pool + real auto-resume, armed by the real codex rejection producer (`recordCodexQuotaSignal({type:'task_failed', codexErrorInfo:'usage_limit_reached'})`), in the incident's shape: `wake = {acted:true, fired:["sess-9-…"], skipped:[]}`, the continue delivered, the card `账号 ChatGPT New 已恢复可用，已自动继续这个任务。`, and `cold-restart broadcasts: 1` — the same conversation was simultaneously told to restart through the client, while the continue lands in a CLI that cannot re-read credentials and therefore bills the member it was spawned on: the exhausted one. **REACHABILITY IS PERSISTED STATE, NOT A HYPOTHESIS**: until 2026-09-05 manage-agents rendered the "Hot switch (no restart)" row inside a bare `if (a?.pooled && !selectedHost)` with no backend gate, and `PATCH /api/accounts/pool/:id` still writes `hot: req.body?.hot` with no caps gate. The flag is inert on every other path (the engine gates on caps; the client now hides the row), so a user who set it saw nothing happen and had no reason to unset it. Note the per-session pass's own `if (a.hot)` is unreachable for codex (it `break`s for non-planC backends), so the caps-gated pool-level guard is the ONLY `fireNow` guard a codex pool can reach — and that is the one half ② disagreed with. Fix: ask the same predicate the engine asks, `!(pa.hot && capsOf(pa.backend).hotSwitch === 'verified')`.

**(b) THE AUTO-MERGE EXIT SPAWNED A PANEL FOR THE RECORD IT WAS ABOUT TO DELETE.** `/api/accounts/subscription/:id/finalize` woke `req.params.id` unconditionally, above the merge branch — and `onMemberLoginSuccess` → `probeQuotaForKey` → `refreshViaCliPanel` runs its roster check AND its `execFile` synchronously inside the route (the first real await is inside the `new Promise` executor), so the throwaway is still in the roster at spawn time; `mergeSubscription` then deletes it. MEASURED through the real routes factory + real AccountManager: `CLI PANEL SPAWNS: 2 ["sub-<throwaway>","sub-<survivor>"]`, `merged=true`, `throwaway still exists: false`, and `usage-cache/<deleted-id>.json` left on disk. Nothing in accounts.js or the routes removes usage-cache entries when an account goes away (zero grep hits for `usage-cache` in either file) and `establishedWindows()` reads every `.json` in that directory with no roster filter, so the litter is not inert. The code contradicted **its own comment three lines away** ("THE SURVIVOR is the record the pool holds … the throwaway above is about to stop existing") — a comment where a gate was needed. Fix: hoist the `dup` lookup (a pure roster read, and the only thing that knows whether this record will still exist in a moment) ABOVE the wake and gate the pre-merge wake on `!dupOf`; the merge branch already wakes the SURVIVOR, and the catch — above BOTH the rethrow and the `merge-account-live` answer — wakes this record, because **a deferred wake must not become a dropped one**: the fold failing is a reachable, documented exit where the fresh record is still the one holding the login. BELT: `refreshViaCliPanel` now asks the roster again AT THE WRITE and returns false, so a record removed mid-probe (the merge, or an ordinary "remove this account") can never leave a cache file or a window sidecar behind.

**WHY (b) WENT UNMEASURED.** §6 EXIT 4 asserted only `w.probes.includes(w.PANDY)` — never that the throwaway was NOT probed. Worse, it reached for an email SETTER this `AccountManager` does not have and then took its own SKIP branch, so the entire auto-merge exit was unmeasured. The route's dup lookup reads `x.email || (a name containing @)`, so `am.rename` is all the leg needed; it now asserts the merge really happened before asserting anything about it.

**r3 INVARIANTS.**

* **A capability is a VERDICT; a settings flag is a WISH.** When the module already computes the verdict for its own spend, every other spend site must ask the same expression — reading the raw flag turns a capability gate into a bypass of the guard beside it. A flag that is inert everywhere else is *more* dangerous, not less: nobody has a reason to turn it off.
* **A side effect that spends money must be ordered after the decision that says who will exist.** The merge lookup was a pure read available at any point; the wake was placed before it only because that is where the sweep already was.
* **A comment that states the invariant three lines from the violation is a bug report.** The code said "the throwaway is about to stop existing" and then read its usage.
* **A gate added to skip work must name every exit that still needs the work.** Gating on `dup` without the catch-side wake would have dropped the login edge on the blocked-merge exit entirely.
* **A guard on a window between a check and a write belongs at the WRITE.** Ordering fixes the one caller you know about; re-asking the roster before writing fixes the class (account removal is an ordinary user action too).
* **An assert that only says who WAS woken cannot see who else was.** Positive-only assertions about a set are how a second, wrong element hides.
* **A leg that SKIPs on a capability it guessed at is an unmeasured shape** — the same lesson as r2's declared-but-never-passed control, one layer down: this one silently skipped because it probed for an API instead of using the one the code under test actually uses.

**r3 GATE.** §8c′ drives a REAL codex pool (real `createCodexSubscription` + `createPool({backend:'codex'})` + `updatePool({hot:true})`), armed by the real codex rejection producer, with the session ANSWERING the `rpc-rate-limits` probe through the wrapper's own error reply so the 20 s waiter settles instead of racing the wake (without that, the wall probe re-arms mid-gate and the continue is dropped for an UNRELATED reason — a leg that would go green on the wrong fact); it asserts 0 continues, the named `cold pool` skip, no second restart broadcast and no "recovered" card, with a first assert that the two backends really disagree about hotness so the leg cannot be vacuous. Its negative control is a patched engine copy carrying ONLY the raw-flag spelling — it delivers the continue and writes the card. §6 EXIT 4 now takes the merge for real and asserts the survivor is woken, the throwaway is NOT (`probes.length === 1`), and no usage-cache entry is keyed to the deleted record; its negative control is a patched copy of the ROUTES module with the pre-fix ordering, which produces two spawns and the phantom cache file. §6 EXIT 4b drives the blocked-merge exit through a real `merge-account-live` refusal and asserts the edge is still taken, on the record that kept the login. §9 drives `refreshViaCliPanel` through the real `setupUsage` factory with a FAKE `claude` on `CLAUDE_CMD` (zero vendor cost, real `execFile` → real parse → real writes): a live account still gets its cache file AND its window sidecar (the belt is a refusal, not an off-switch), an account removed while the panel runs gets neither, and the negative control — a patched copy of `src/usage-routes.js` with the roster re-check removed — leaves both.


**GATE.** `scripts/test-new-member-wake.mjs` (115, fast tier) — the incident replayed on the REAL engine + REAL AccountManager pool with real per-session symlinks + REAL auto-resume + the real rejection producer, and the REAL `account-usage-routes` factory for the login exits. §1a pins the shape (parked on an unread member, so nothing can switch); §1b is master reproduced (three ticks pass, still waiting for the far reset — then one call to the edge continues both); §1c switches each half off in the shape where only it can answer; §2 refuses no/stale/foreign readings and a member that is itself spent; §3 pins both floors and the polled-route fingerprint gate *with the engine floor wound back*; §4 measures the `loggedIn` gap against the real `parseAuth`; §5 proves the quarantine and the 3/hour cap still hold against readings that keep arriving; §6 drives all five login exits through the real route factory plus the auto-cli producer; §7 pins §ban-safety. Sixteen mutations of the product source were run against it — every one goes red, and the three that survived the first draft are why §1c and §3 are written the way they are. §8 is r2 (above), §8c′ + §6 EXIT 4/4b + §9 are r3 (above). Suite total 115.

## A LOW-USAGE ACCOUNT SUDDENLY JUMPED TO 93 % (inc-mts8a8mr-ulmm, 2026-09-08 05:27Z — the reading was one request older than the link)

**THE REPORT.** Owner: *"a low-usage account suddenly jumped to 93% 7d"*. The account (Member P) had been at 11–13 % all day, on its own `/usage` panel readings, before and after.

**WHAT ACTUALLY HAPPENED**, reconstructed entirely from this instance's own stores and journal:

| time (UTC) | event | evidence |
|---|---|---|
| 05:27:00 | the pool moves 9 sessions Member Y → Member P | `slot-transitions.jsonl`: 9 `per-session-switch` rows + 1 `pool-switch` row; journal `[pool] auto-switch … (exhausted, from 2% left)` |
| 05:27:16 | a response to a request made **39 s earlier with Member Y's token** arrives; `readingSlotFor` had resolved the turn's slot at its first reading = Personal | Member P's own anchor stream 05:27:39: `sevenDay {u:0.93, resetsAt:1789030800}` — **Member Y's window** — beside a `fiveHour` and a `scopedWeekly` that are Personal's own |
| 05:28:38 | the pool reads Personal as "5 % left" and moves everything to **Member F** | journal `[pool] auto-switch … (exhausted, from 5% left)`; 10 more ledger rows |
| 05:29:39 | Personal's own `/usage` panel restores it to 13 % | its own `on-demand` anchor |
| 05:31:40 | the pool moves everything back to Personal | 10 more ledger rows |

**Two false pool switches inside 98 seconds, caused by our own write.** 2.369.68's slot rule is right and stays — a reading belongs to the credential slot the process was handed, pinned for the turn — but it cannot see that the slot moves while requests are IN FLIGHT. A re-point reaches the CLI on its NEXT request, never the response already on the wire; the pin is set by the turn's FIRST reading, and the first reading of that turn was a straggler.

**THE EVIDENCE THE RESPONSE CARRIES ABOUT ITSELF.** A rate-limit payload names the WINDOW its numbers are counted in, and a weekly window is an ACCOUNT FINGERPRINT: measured over the whole anchor corpus of this instance (7 live subscriptions, 30 days, 6 634 anchors carrying a weekly reset), **every identity has exactly ONE weekly reset phase** (`resetsAt mod 604800`), stable across the entire period, and **the seven are all distinct**. A roll adds exactly one week so the phase survives it; the ±60 s wobble between the `/usage` panel's and the event stream's spelling of the SAME window is normal. So the record can be asked which credentials produced it, and that outranks the slot, the turn pin and the OTel observation — all of which describe what we BELIEVE the process is reading.

**THE FIX** — one PURE rule (`src/reading-lag.js`, imports nothing), two clauses, wired at every server-side value producer through `readingSlotFor` / `guardReadingTarget`, and mirrored VERBATIM into the shipped statusline tool:
- ① **the LAG SHADOW**: after a re-point at T, a reading whose window matches the PREVIOUS slot's established window and NOT the new one is filed on the PREVIOUS slot; the first reading that matches the NEW slot ends the shadow and it stays ended; when neither window decides (two members genuinely sharing a phase, or no established window yet) it falls through to the r3 statusline clause — *the link moved but the numbers did not*; BOUNDED at 10 min so a session that never speaks again cannot pin a slot forever. It is applied BEFORE the turn pin is set, so a pin created by a shadowed reading pins the PREVIOUS slot.
- ② **the WINDOW IDENTITY GUARD**: a reading whose window contradicts the target member's own established window is NEVER written there — re-filed when EXACTLY ONE account's window matches (identity groups collapsed), else ARCHIVED to `data/archive/readings-window-mismatch.ndjson` with the reason, the target's own window and its age, + telemetry + one journal line per session per verdict transition. **No evidence ⇒ no refusal.**
- **the established window is STAMPED AT THE WRITE** (`cache.ownWindow`) by the ONE producer whose key and whose credential dir are the same decision — `refreshViaCliPanel` — and is never read back out of `sevenDay.resetsAt`, which is precisely the field a mis-filed reading overwrites; `captureRateLimitEvent` and `markLimitBanner` preserve it explicitly, because both base their write on the identity group's FRESHEST sibling.

**THE SECOND BRANCH, and it is two different things.** Member F's own anchor stream carries `rate_limit_event` anchors 04:11:03Z–05:03:04Z with Member Y's window and 7d 0.89–0.91, while the pool had moved the sessions to Member Y at 04:10:02Z. Version telemetry dates that window to **2.369.67 — before the readings-by-slot fix (2.369.68) shipped**, so those rows are residue of the refuted `orgVerifiedKey` routing (the sessions' OTel-observed org was Member F; the journal logged `session … observed on Member F while linked to Member Y` every 10 min through it). The repair that shipped with that fix could not reach them: its only evidence is *the member's own credential file DATED its death*, which covered ONE member here (`[migrate] readings-by-slot: {"members":1,…}`) — everything mis-filed **between two logged-in accounts** survived it, which is exactly the "silent half" its own header names. **But the same shape recurs under the CURRENT code**: 06:11:39Z and 06:12:39Z put Member Y's 0.95 on Member F again, at 2.369.69, with `fetchedAt 06:10:46Z` — 20 s AFTER the link had moved to Member Y, i.e. the numbers were freshly and correctly Member Y's. That producer was a session whose TURN PIN had been set before three re-points and never cleared. The shadow cannot speak there (no recent re-point explains it); the **window guard** is what catches it, and it re-points the turn pin to the proven account so the rest of the turn follows the evidence.

**THE DATA REPAIR** (`repairByWindow`, migration `2026-09-refile-readings-by-window`) uses the evidence the earlier one lacked. Measured on a COPY of this instance's stores: 7 identities established from their own panel readings, **464 anchors re-filed, 0 archived, 39 pairs voided, 7 caches seeded with `ownWindow`, 0 foreign caches**, `rates.json` archived + dropped, 7046 records in and 7046 out (archive-never-destroy, verified by line count), idempotent, 51 ms. **460 of the 464 predate 2.369.68**; the 4 that postdate it are the incident's own two rows plus the 06:11/06:12 pair. Member Y's own 95 % is REAL (EDF put the sessions on it; its own panel readings climbed 0.60→0.95 over 12 h) and is untouched — the repair moves a row only when its window names exactly one live account, and archives with a reason otherwise.

**INVARIANTS.**
- **A reading is evidence about itself.** When a record names the window its numbers are counted in, that outranks every piece of bookkeeping we keep about which credentials a process holds: bookkeeping describes intent, the window describes what answered.
- **A rolling identity must be compared by the part that does not roll.** The weekly PHASE, not the absolute `resetsAt` — otherwise every legitimate window roll reads as a foreign account.
- **A fingerprint must name the thing it claims did not change.** The r3 clause says *the numbers did not move*; fingerprinting only the window would have shadowed nearly every reading following a re-point between two members sharing a phase. Caught by this branch's own shared-phase leg, in this branch's code, before it shipped.
- **The fact a guard reads may never be the fact a bad write produces.** Deriving the established window from `sevenDay.resetsAt` would let one mis-filed reading redefine the account and immunise every later one.
- **No evidence ⇒ no refusal**, and every refusal is archived with the reason, the target's own window and its age — so "the account's window genuinely moved" stays distinguishable from "this reading was mis-filed".
- **A write that moves money is not silent.** The shadow and the guard each speak once per session per verdict transition; the incident's whole diagnosis was "which account did this number go to, and why", and the journal had nothing to say about the write that caused it.
- **A repair whose only evidence is "this member's login died" can only ever see the visible half.** The silent half — two logged-in accounts — needs evidence carried by the data itself.

**R2 — TWO REAL DEFECTS IN THE FIX ITSELF (adversarial verifier; both reproduced before being believed).** They are the same species: a fact was trusted further than the measurement supports it.

**(a) ONE STATUSLINE RENDER DISARMED THE WHOLE FIX.** `ownWindow` was a FIELD of the usage-cache snapshot — and that object is REBUILT WHOLESALE by every reading producer. The highest-frequency one is the SHIPPED statusline hook (`data/bin/vibespace-usage`, once per 8 s per account, our own write, entirely legitimate): its `out` literal preserves `scopedWeekly`, `scopedFetchedAt`, the org identity and `spend` **one field at a time** — the 2.70.0 "Fable-vanishing" class its own comment names — and simply never listed the window. The two writers that WERE taught to preserve it (`captureRateLimitEvent`, `markLimitBanner`) are the low-frequency ones; the highest-frequency one clobbered. Reproduced twice: a real run of the shipped file over a cache carrying the window (`BEFORE …sevenDay:1789142400 → AFTER undefined`, keys `fiveHour,sevenDay,scopedWeekly,orgUuid,orgName,spend,overallStatus,fetchedAt,source`), and then end to end against this branch's OWN §14 harness (real engine + real AccountManager pool + real per-session symlinks): one legitimate render per member with each member's own numbers and own window took the established windows `[true,true,true] → [false,false,false]`, `establishedWindows()` went empty, `decideReadingTarget` degraded to *"no established window to contradict"*, the lagging response landed on the low-usage account again, and **the SECOND FALSE SWITCH happened** — byte-for-byte this branch's own `stampWindows:false` negative control. Every gate stayed green because no leg ran a statusline render before §14. On this instance 32 anchors already carry `source:'passive'`, so the path is live; the same clobber shape exists at the bare-token ⟳ (`JSON.stringify(u)`) and at the codex snapshot writer.
*The fix is not a fifth entry in a hand-written preserve list* — that list is what failed, five times, on this one file. **The window moved OUT of the object every reading producer rewrites**, into `data/usage-cache/.window-<key>` (`windowSidecarName`, no `.json` so every usage-cache scanner keeps ignoring it — the `.slot-<session_id>` sidecar beside it already relies on that rule). `refreshViaCliPanel` writes it; `establishedWindows()` reads it, KEYED OFF THE CACHE FILES so a sidecar left by a removed account can never become a re-file target; the two preserve blocks are **deleted**, and the ⟳ and codex writers need no change at all — which is the sign the class is closed rather than patched.

**(b) THE FIVE-HOUR RUNG WAS A NEW MISATTRIBUTION OF EXACTLY THE CLASS THE GUARD EXISTS TO PREVENT.** `compareWindows` let `fiveHour` alone produce an identity verdict, so every `five_hour` `rate_limit_event` (which carries NO weekly component) reached that rung. The corpus refutes the premise — measured read-only over `data/usage-anchors`:
- a SAME-account 5 h reset sits >120 s from that account's own still-future panel-stamped value on **7.2 % (33/456)** of Member F's readings and **7.4 % (47/639)** of Member P's — a `'differ'` about its rightful owner (real pair: stamped `1788780540`, read `1788786600`, Δ 6060 s);
- DIFFERENT identities carry IDENTICAL 5 h resets constantly — **47 distinct colliding values**, because they snap to the clock (`resetsAt mod 1800` peaks at 0/600/1140/1200/1740) — an `'agree'` with a stranger;
- scanning the real 30-day corpus for the exact guard state (target's stamped 5 h still future, reading >120 s away, EXACTLY ONE other account matching) yields **50 moments** where a legitimate reading would have been re-filed onto a stranger.

Proven at the pure rule with those numbers (`refile → bstack`, and `archive` when no sibling lines up) and END TO END through the real producer: Member Y kept its stale 0.2 while Member B received 0.55 under a confident journal line. **A five-hour window identifies a TIME, not an account** — so the weekly half is now the ONLY identity evidence, the rung is deleted from both spellings, and a reading with no weekly component short-circuits `decideReadingTarget` with the honest reason `reading states no weekly window` (the target usually HAS an established window, so *"no established window to contradict"* would be a false sentence in the journal and the archive). Identity is therefore decided WITHOUT a clock — `nowSec` and `absNear` are gone, because comparing weekly PHASES survives a roll by construction. The suite had pinned the refuted premise as correct (§13 asserted a 5 h mismatch is `'differ'`) and no leg drove the guard with a `five_hour` event, which is why this shipped green.

**R2 INVARIANTS.**
- **A fact only ONE producer may state does not belong in the object all the others rewrite.** Preserving it is a promise every current AND future writer has to keep; moving it is a property. The list had already lost `scopedWeekly`, the org identity, `spend` and `corroborated` — the window was the fifth, and it was the one the money decisions read.
- **When a class recurs, close it structurally instead of extending the list.** The measure of the fix is that two of the three clobbering writers needed no change.
- **Ask what a field IDENTIFIES, and measure it before believing it.** "The window names the account" is true of the weekly half and false of the five-hour half; the same sentence covering both turned a guard into a source of the harm it was written to stop.
- **A guard with no evidence must write, not move.** `'unknown'` is the answer for a reading that names no account, and it must never be spelled like `'differ'` (which re-files) or like a match (which steals).
- **A reason string is an assertion about the system.** It reaches the journal AND the archive record, and it is the whole diagnosis a human gets.
- **Mutation-check each mechanism separately, and anchor the fixture to the condition the retired code needed.** The first version of these legs used the corpus's raw seconds — now in the PAST — so the restored rung (which only fires while the target's stamped 5 h is still FUTURE) could not redden them: a leg that cannot fail is not a leg.

**R2 REGRESSIONS.** §13 states the measured facts (5 h drift + collisions) instead of the refuted premise, with the retired rung as a permanent negative control; the two mechanisms (the deleted rung, and the no-weekly short-circuit) get one leg each because the short-circuit fires BEFORE `compareWindows` is consulted, so neither alone reddens the engine-level leg. §15 gains the CLOBBER leg — the incident replayed AFTER one real statusline render per member, driven through the SHIPPED FILE'S OWN BYTES, with a negative control that is a PATCHED COPY of that same shipped file with only the storage decision reverted (the patch is asserted to hit, and it reproduces the whole incident including the second false switch) — and the FIVE-HOUR leg through the real producer, with a positive control proving the WEEKLY guard is still armed in the same world. Mutation-checked: restoring the 5 h rung reddens 2, widening the short-circuit reddens 2, both together (the true pre-fix rule) redden 7 including the engine-level leg, and reverting the storage decision reddens 19. Re-measured on a COPY of this instance's stores: 464 anchors re-filed / 0 archived / 39 pairs voided / **7 window sidecars seeded, 0 caches carrying an in-snapshot window**, 7059 records in and 7059 out, idempotent, 52 ms. test-readings-attribution 283.


**Suite**: test-readings-attribution 259 — §13 the pure rule (weekly phase, roll invariance, jitter, three-state compare, the shadow on the incident's own timing, the bound, the r3 fallback, the guard's write/refile/archive/ambiguous outcomes, identity-group collapsing) + the byte-identical mirror in `data/bin/vibespace-usage` driven functionally through the SHIPPED bytes; §14 the incident replayed against the REAL engine + REAL pool + real symlinks (the lagging response lands on the burned member, the "low-usage account" keeps its own number, the anchor follows, **ZERO second switch** from a fresh engine's own pool evaluation) with the PRE-FIX negative control that reproduces the second switch end to end, the shared-phase leg that only the shadow can answer (+ its own negative control), and the STALE TURN PIN branch; §15 the guard in the live producers (archive with the reason, inert without evidence, rejections deliberately un-guarded, `ownWindow` surviving both session-attributed writers, source pins); §16 the repair on a fixture shaped like this instance's stores. Mutation-checked: disabling the shadow reddens 2 legs (its journal line and the shared-phase case), disabling the guard reddens 7 (the stale-pin branch and the archive branch).

**ROUND 3 (adversarial verifier — the fix's own clock was wrong in two places; both reproduced with the real engine + the real pool + real symlinks + the SHIPPED tool's own bytes, three-arm matrix).** ① `decideLagShadow` short-circuited on `shadow-expired` BEFORE consulting either window, and the shipped statusline had no re-point instant to give it — it passed the age of its last OBSERVATION (`.slot-<id>`'s `at`, i.e. the last render under the OLD slot, which necessarily precedes the re-point that ended it and therefore systematically OVERSTATES). One ordinary render of an IDLE pooled TERMINAL after the pool moved LINK→SPARE — 30 min since its redraw, link re-pointed one second earlier — answered `shadow-expired` and filed LINK's 0.96 (carrying LINK's own weekly window) onto SPARE with `source:'passive'`, and the pool made **the SECOND FALSE SWITCH** (→ FISH, "exhausted, from 4 % left"). Observation age 5 min ⇒ untouched, so the mechanism is isolated to the age bound; and MASTER's tool (9d6e6180, whose r3 rule has no clock at all) ⇒ untouched, so this was a **REGRESSION vs master**, unconditional once the tool has written an `at` once — also reproduced with NO window sidecars (a fresh instance): master keeps 0.11, the branch filed 0.93/0.96. **Fix**: the clock bound moved BELOW both window rungs in BOTH spellings (a clock is a PROXY for "were those credentials still in play"; the window is the ANSWER, and a proxy may never overrule what it stands in for — the bound now only decides where nothing else can, which is the only place it was ever load-bearing), `repointAgeMs == null` is UNKNOWN and does not expire, and the statusline dates the RE-POINT from the link the pool re-MINTS (`repointPoolSymlink` = symlink-to-temp + rename ⇒ `lstat(link).mtimeMs` is that instant; measured 7200 s untouched / 0.001 s after), keeping `st.at` only as forensics. The server side is unchanged in reachable states: `lagShadowFor` already BREAKS its ledger scan at `SHADOW_MS`. ② **The 8×/minute producer was the one value producer with no window guard at all** — the shadow only speaks when a re-point explains the reading, so every other path wrote whatever `accountKey()` resolved; `decideReadingTarget` moved INSIDE the mirror sentinels and the statusline now re-files onto the one matching account or REFUSES, recording the reason in `.window-refused.ndjson` beside the cache (no `.json`, per-day shard on overflow). A shipped single file has no identity-group table, so an org-merged login reads as TWO matches and is refused rather than re-filed — the conservative direction. **INVARIANTS**: a clock is a proxy and the evidence outranks the proxy, so a time bound belongs BELOW every rung that can actually answer; **unknown must never be spelled like expired**; an "honest upper bound" is only honest if the test it feeds is safe in that direction (the comment correctly said `st.at` is never earlier than the re-point — which is exactly why it must not be compared against an expiry); ask what a value DATES, and prefer the first-class record we already own (the symlink we mint ourselves) over a proxy; **a rule shared by two spellings must be shared WHOLE** ("no shipped caller has needed it yet" is not a tier boundary); and **two barriers against one harm need one control each, read where each barrier speaks** — with the guard in place, reverting only the ordering moves no money, so §15 reads the ordering control at the `.slot-<id>` state file and judges the money with a PRE-FIX copy that reverts both. Mutation-checked: the clock-first ordering in both spellings reddens 5, the observation age reddens 3, removing the statusline guard reddens 4. Migration re-measured on a COPY of this instance's stores (live data): 7 identities / 7 receivers, **475 anchors re-filed, 0 archived, 39 pairs voided, 7 window sidecars seeded, 0 caches carrying an in-snapshot window, 2 foreign cache snapshots archived and rebuilt**, 7082 records in and 7082 out, idempotent, 51 ms. **test-readings-attribution 310.**

**ROUND 4 (adversarial verifier — one HIGH defect, measured on a COPY of this instance's stores before it was believed): THE REPAIR WAS MANUFACTURING THE MISATTRIBUTION IT EXISTS TO CLEAN UP.** The by-window migration established each account's window as `{sevenDay, fiveHour:null, scoped:{}}` — it threw the model-scoped half of the evidence away — so it decided every anchor on its 7-day bucket alone. That is a blind check, not a smaller one, because **an anchor is a snapshot of a usage-cache FILE and that file has two writers**: the statusline rewrites `{5h, 7d}` from its own payload and PRESERVES whatever `scopedWeekly` the file already held (it has no scoped buckets of its own — the 2.70.0 Fable-vanishing rule its own comment names). A reading that landed on the wrong key before the window guard existed therefore leaves a record that is ITSELF A MIX: another account's 7d on top of **this stream's own** Fable bucket. Measured here: **444** such records against **32** whose halves are both foreign and **4466** clean ones — so **443 of the 476** re-files moved complete with a Fable bucket whose weekly phase the destination contradicts, and `repairCachesByWindow` then rebuilt a cache from one of them (`best` = the newest record with an agreeing 7d, `refiledFrom` naming the other member) and copied its value, its `resetsAt` and its `scopedFetchedAt` over the target's own. `src/account-pool-auto.js` reads `cache.scopedWeekly` in `accountRemaining` / `weeklyDeadline` / `bucketRems`, so that is a misattributed reading that can flip a pool switch — the 2.305.0 scoped-bucket class, created by the repair.

**The fix moves PER BUCKET, never wholesale.** ① each identity now establishes its **scoped** phases from its OWN on-demand rows under the same two thresholds as the 7d (measurably the same phase as its 7d on all seven identities here, every cluster inside the ±120 s tolerance, all seven distinct); ② `repairAnchorsByWindow` asks `decideReadingTarget` one bucket at a time — the PRIMARY half is `{5h, 7d}` (one payload, one producer, one credential, so the 5h travels with the 7d and never alone), the record follows its primary half and carries ONLY the buckets that agree with where it lands, every other bucket is archived with its own reason and the archive line keeps the ORIGINAL WHOLE RECORD; a record with no agreeing bucket is archived, not moved; ③ `repairCachesByWindow` picks `best` only among records ALL of whose judgeable weekly buckets agree, `_cacheFromAnchor` filters on the account's window as a second independent barrier, and — because a per-bucket move can leave the newest own-window record with no scoped bucket — it now preserve-merges the previous snapshot's scoped bucket (through the same filter) instead of replacing it, exactly as all three live writers do; ④ a cache whose 7d agrees but whose scoped bucket does not is stripped in place, a shape the rebuild branch structurally cannot reach. **The live writer path needs no change and the reason is pinned**: every `guardReadingTarget` call is handed a producer PAYLOAD, a `rate_limit_event` names exactly one bucket, and a `get_usage`/panel/codex snapshot is one credential's single response — the mix is manufactured downstream by the per-key preserve-merge, and each preserve reads the TARGET's own previous list after the guard has moved `key`.

**R4 INVARIANTS.**
- **A record assembled by more than one writer must be judged by more than one verdict** — ask what wrote each field before deciding the record as a unit.
- **Establishing "the account's window" from a subset of the evidence is not a weaker check, it is a blind one**, and a repair that cannot see half of a record will move that half onto a stranger.
- **A reason string answers the question its line is about.** `decideReadingTarget`'s reason answers "does this bucket belong to the stream it sits in"; quoting it on a drop produced 443 archive lines reading *"window matches the target"* about a bucket that was dropped.
- **"No evidence ⇒ no refusal" governs REFUSING TO WRITE, not MOVING.** An unjudgeable bucket may stay where it is; carrying it to another account is a positive claim (705 scoped entries here state no `resetsAt`, and `bucketRemaining` still reads their utilization).
- **A rebuild is a WRITE and inherits the preserve rule every other writer has** — replacing the snapshot from an anchor that states no scoped bucket DELETES the model cap the pool decides on.
- **Two barriers against one harm need one control each, read where each barrier speaks**: after a full pass the anchor half has already cleaned every stream, so the `best` picker is driven ALONE over an uncleaned store rather than left as a guard nobody can delete and redden.

**R4 MEASUREMENT (both repairs over ONE frozen copy of this instance's stores).** r3: 476 re-filed whole, **443 rows land carrying a Fable bucket the destination's own phase contradicts**. r4: 476 re-filed (32 whole, 444 partial), 444 buckets archived with their own reason, 0 records archived, 41 pairs voided, 7 identities established and all 7 able to receive, 7 sidecars seeded WITH their scoped phases, 1 foreign cache archived + rebuilt keeping its own Fable bucket, rates dropped, 7096 records in and 7096 out, **census: 0 contradicting weekly buckets in any stream and 0 in any cache**, idempotent, 52 ms. Suite: **test-readings-attribution 340** (§16b, whose negative control is a PATCHED COPY of the real module with exactly this round's five decisions reverted — each replacement asserted to hit — and whose money assertion is the real `decidePoolSwitch` moving off the account under the fix and staying on it under r3).
