Mutation-checked, each mechanism alone: 8 / 12 / 3 / 4 / 2 / 3 red; restored, green.

## THE "HISTORY IS HIDDEN" ROW NEVER SHOWED ON THE ONE LIST THAT NEEDED IT (2.369.70, caught by the Actions mirror)

The sidebar's `_render()` early-returns "No sessions" when the filtered list is empty (and no hosts / no search / not the tasks tab). The OpenCode "stopped conversations are hidden — its background service is off" row is rendered by the workbench builder, i.e. AFTER that return, so an instance whose only conversations are the hidden OpenCode ones — the exact user the row exists for, whose list is empty BECAUSE the history is hidden — never saw the way back. The plugin suite's leg 4 was green on the dev machine for the wrong reason (unrelated claude sessions keep the list non-empty) and red on the GitHub Actions runner (no sessions at all) for two pushes.

Fix: the empty branch calls `_renderServiceHintRows(sessions)` before returning (nothing wipes `listEl` after it, which is why the "workbench-only" pin now allows exactly that one extra call, immediately before the `return`). Regression: test-opencode-plugin leg 4b forces `_allSessions = []` + no hosts and asserts "No sessions" + the row + its Enable action; A/B with the fix reverted goes 3 red.

Invariants: **an early-return branch is a render path** — every row that is "gated on broadcast state" must be reachable from every branch that can paint the list, or the branch that happens on the poorest instance is the one that lies; **a leg that passes because of the developer's own data is environment-dependent** — the Actions mirror is the instance with nothing, read it after every push (memory: push后要看CI镜像).

## QUOTA READINGS WERE KEYED BY THE ORG THE CLI STARTED ON (2026-09-07, owner: "为啥不是从根源上解决假数据而是加 guardrail")

The visible symptom was absurd: a member whose Claude login had been **wiped on 2026-09-02 22:55 PDT** kept receiving limit-banners and Fable-bucket readings for five more days. The first instinct — "make logged-out members reject readings" — was rejected by the owner as a guardrail over a lie, and he was right: the logged-out member is only the case where the lie becomes *visible*.

**The mechanism.** Every VALUE reading — statusline ingest, `rate_limit_event` capture, limit-banner marks, the auto-cli panel result's attribution, the anchors they feed, and the OTel "corrections" — was keyed by `orgVerifiedKey()` = the OTel-observed org. That org is the identity the CLI cached in its config dir **at SPAWN**. The credentials a *running* CLI reads can be re-pointed later — 2.1.257's `rpe()` re-reads `.credentials.json` on an mtime change, which is exactly what the pool's hot switch does and exactly why the re-point bumps that mtime. So after any hot switch a session's readings were filed under the account it was SPAWNED on, for the rest of its life. 2.369.66 had already fixed the BLOCKING half with this same correction (a rejection is a fact about the credential SLOT); this is the VALUE half, and it had been left standing on the refuted premise the previous day.

**The silent half is the dangerous one.** Between two logged-in members nothing looks wrong at all: a Fish-billed session spawned under Member B files Fish's numbers under Member B. Both panels move, both anchor streams learn, and the estimator's rates are trained on the mixture. Dry-run over a COPY of this instance's stores: **3 foreign cache snapshots, 248 foreign anchors across 3 identity streams (145/102/1), 44 foreign attribution entries** — and a second member wiped at 12:39 that same day received a reading at 19:24, i.e. the defect was still producing damage while the fix was being written.

**The fix is that there is now ONE resolver.** `readingSlotFor(session, at)` — the validated credential slot, PINNED FOR THE TURN and cleared by `noteTurnEnd` — is the twin of `rejectionSlotFor` and deliberately shares `wallSlotFor`'s body, because a reading and a rejection are facts about the same thing. `resolveUsageKey` resolves `sessionBillingMember` too, and **`sessionReadingMember` and `orgVerifiedKey` are DELETED**: keeping two functions for one question was the bug's home, and the one that was wrong owned every value on the instance. The observation survives as `corroborateReading()` — logged once per session per 10min, telemetered, and stamped on the write as `corroborated:true|false`, a label that moves nothing.

**Why the pin is a TURN.** Every producer calls `maybePoolAutoSwitch` the moment it writes, so one turn's ~20 readings would otherwise be split across the members *our own re-points* moved to while they were arriving — numbers credited to an account that served none of that turn. A re-point reaches the CLI on its NEXT request, so the turn boundary is the first moment a switch can be true.

**The statusline needed its own answer.** `data/bin/vibespace-usage` was keyed by `VIBESPACE_ACCOUNT_KEY`, fixed at SPAWN, under a comment claiming "a hot re-point is a known attribution seam handled by the ledger's time-based records" — wrong on both halves (the CLI does re-read, and the statusline cache is not the ledger; nothing ever re-attributed it). ws-create now also ships `VIBESPACE_ACCOUNT_LINK` = the credential SYMLINK itself, and the tool `readlink`s it per write: the slot resolved by the process that produced the reading. The REMOTE branch deliberately has no twin, with the reason written down — a host's credentials are tarred at spawn and the engine's re-points skip host sessions, so there is no later switch to be wrong about.

**A symlink has no history — so give it one.** `data/slot-transitions.jsonl` records every re-point `{sessionId, poolId, from, to, at, why}` (sessionId null = the pool default moved). accounts.js is the SINGLE WRITER — `ensureSessionPoolLink` / `setPoolTarget` are the only two functions that move a link, so spawn, engine, manual route, signed-out self-heal and account removal are covered by construction. `slotAt(sid, ts)` answers by TIME; **no record means UNKNOWN, never agreement**.

**The repair is root-cause too.** A one-shot migration (`2026-09-reattribute-readings-by-slot`) walks the four stores using evidence in strength order: the transition ledger, then each member's own last-known-good marker (a wiped credential file's mtime — decisive and inference-free), then an OPTIONAL operator-provided frozen journal whose `[pool] per-session switch` lines backfill into the ledger (a line we cannot place in TIME is COUNTED, never guessed). A foreign cache snapshot is archived with a reason and **rebuilt from the newest anchor predating the wipe**; a foreign anchor is archived and the record after it re-chained with `costSince:null` (the cost is real, the Δu is not); `rates.json` is dropped so the estimator re-learns; an attribution entry is re-attributed when the ledger can place it and archived otherwise, which makes the by-time walk fall back to that session's previous un-refuted entry. Idempotent, restart-safe, archive-never-destroy.

**And the panel stopped lying.** "Updated 3min ago" is the sentence the incident could not survive. Every panel now names the SOURCE of its latest reading, adds the corroboration verdict when one exists, and — for a member that cannot produce readings at all — shows its last REAL reading with "signed out since <stamp>", plus an explicit warning when the newest cached reading POSTDATES the death.

**Invariants.**
- TOP-PROCS LIST WENT EMPTY ON A BUSY BOX (release gate, 2026-09-08): `ps aux` output is unbounded by process count AND argv length — 2463 processes plus one `claude -p <29 KB prompt>` made 4.4 MB, past sysinfo's 4 MiB execFile maxBuffer, so topProcs answered [] and the sysinfo op shipped no processes (test-sysinfo-op red on the release gate; the panel would show nothing). Fix: one PS_MAX_BUFFER = 32 MiB on every ps call (width flags are GNU-only and would break the BSD rung); test-sysinfo-op's fat-ps leg drives a 5.5 MB fake `ps` through the real read() with a 4 MiB negative control. Invariant: a child's stdout bound must be sized from the WORST real table (count × longest argv), never a round number.
- **A reading is keyed by the credentials the producing process was handed** — the symlink we control, never a telemetry attribute about it. Where a producer has no session (the auto-cli panel), that is its spawn's config dir, and the key and the config dir must be ONE decision.
- **A telemetry attribute that names an identity is not proof of which token authorized a request.** `organization.id` was measured wrong twice (2.361.0 and B-2c9b) before the owner's post-mortem; the "≥25min stale token" forensic was itself made *with* the channel it was meant to validate — it measured how long the CLI keeps REPORTING its spawn identity, not how long it keeps BILLING it. **A channel may not be its own control.**
- **Two functions answering one question means one of them is wrong**, and it will be the one nobody re-derives. `sessionReadingMember`/`sessionBillingMember` diverged for exactly one release — the former is DELETED since 2026-09-07 — and the wrong one owned every value on the instance.
- **A mutable fact that our own handler changes must be resolved once per EVENT, not per record** (the 2.369.66 rejection lesson, now applied to readings — the scope is the turn, because that is what defines it).
- **State with no history cannot answer a late question**; if late consumers exist, record the transitions rather than letting each one invent a proxy.
- **"Unsatisfiable guard" is a claim to re-measure, not inherit.** 2.369.66 deleted a credentials leg from `validateBillingSlot` as unsatisfiable; `parseAuth` reports `loggedIn:true` for a login whose access AND refresh tokens have both expired, so it was satisfiable and it was the gap that let dead-account readings look authorised — and the same gap made such a member a legal SWITCH TARGET (`ensureSessionPoolLink` checks the same boolean). `healthyPoolMembers` excludes it with **no fallback**: moving a conversation onto credentials that cannot authorize a request is not routing around a dead account, it IS the dead account, and an all-unusable pool must reach the loud `no-members` state rather than pick one anyway. The auth-FAILURE mark keeps its escape hatch because that one is a live inference; a credential state is a fact.
- **Memoize a health answer on the FACT, not on a clock.** The credential state gates both switch targets and a user-facing warning, so a 30s time box would keep answering "signed out" for 30 seconds after a re-login. Keying the memo on the file's mtime+size costs one stat (the cheap half) and makes a change visible on the very next call.
- **An archive that can lose what it exists to preserve is not an archive** — the transition ledger's rotation named shards `Date.now()`, so two trims inside one millisecond overwrote each other; it appends to a daily shard now.
- **A panel that reports only freshness is reporting the wrong thing** when attribution is the failure mode; name the producer, and let a source nobody recognises print itself verbatim rather than be absorbed into a familiar bucket.

**ROUND 2 (adversarial verifier — six defects, every one reproduced first; each fix carries a regression assert AND a mutation-tested negative control).** ① **MAJOR: a REMOTE CODEX reading was routed into the host's CLAUDE bucket.** `usageCacheKeyFor`'s first rule ("a remote session with no account → `host-<id>`") is a CLAUDE fact — usage-routes seeds `_hostUsage` from those files, the remote statusline harvest writes the host's `__global__` into them, the on-demand ⟳ overwrites them with an OAuth panel and the Agents machine rows render them as that machine's own quota. Codex remote chat is supported (ws-create: "codex remote chat rides the SAME keeper") and `recordCodexQuotaSignal` has no host gate, so the moment readings and rejections shared ONE resolver a remote codex session's `rate_limits_updated` snapshot began OVERWRITING the host's claude numbers (reproduced: `{"limitId":"codex",…}` replacing the host's `sevenDay 0.11`, printed "via unknown" because the codex snapshot carries no `source`) AND disappearing from codex's own panel, whose disk seed only matches `cxs-*`/`__global_codex__` — while `noteWallSignal` on the same session still keyed `codexQuotaKeyFor` = `__global_codex__`, so a reading and its wall twin landed on different identities. The rule is now a TRANSFORM OF THE ANSWER rather than a backend test — whatever machine identity `resolveUsageKey` names, only `__global__` (the claude machine login) has a per-host form; an account-billed session never resolves to it, so "no account" is implied, and every other machine identity (codex's today, a future harness's tomorrow) passes through untouched. A `backend === 'claude'` spelling would have silently moved remote OpenCode/ACP sessions OFF the host bucket they already used, so that spelling is pinned as a mutation. ② **The daemon's sealed-orders reflex wrote no ledger row.** `agentd._execute` re-points a pool credential link while this server is DOWN (calling `repointPoolSymlink` directly — that machine has no AccountManager), so `slotAt()` answered with the last ORCHESTRATOR row: a confident WRONG answer, not the "unknown" the ledger promises, in exactly the window where a late consumer has nothing else to go on (and `repairAttribution` would then re-attribute an entry to a member the session had already left, stamped `repairedBy`). The device already reports every field the ledger needs, so the reconcile callback appends through `accounts.noteDeviceRepoint()` — idempotent under a replayed log (the daemon clears it only on ack, and the in-memory dedup dies with the process), `sessionId` derived from WHICH link moved (a plan-C link lives under `poolLinksDir`, anything else is the default), an unresolvable `from` left null rather than guessed — which keeps "accounts.js is the SINGLE writer" physical instead of a grep that hides a hole. ③ **A conversation whose LAST attribution entry was archived kept the refuted account in the ledger forever.** UsageHistory's re-bake only recomputes `if (e.sid && attrib[e.sid])` — correct when the baked value came from the WALK ("for sids without any, the baked value is the only record we have"), false the moment this repair empties a sid, and such sids exist by construction (the old OTel corrective record fired whenever the observation disagreed with `attribAt`, which answers `acct:null` for a sid with no entries, so a conversation's FIRST and only entry could be a corrective one). Clearing `.attrib-rebake-v1` could never reach them. The repair now NAMES the emptied conversations (`data/usage-history/.attrib-emptied.json` — appended and kept, because it stays true) and the re-bake falls through to the session-meta account for them. ④ **`deathMarkers` equated "the local credential dir is wiped" with "this account cannot produce readings"** — but a wiped dir plus a valid LONG-LIVED TOKEN (B-211a) is a supported, spawnable, reading-PRODUCING configuration (`resolveForSpawn` → `{oatOnly, CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN}`; the session still carries `_accountId = sub-X` and `VIBESPACE_ACCOUNT_KEY = sub-X`). Measured on a fixture: the panel rewound from 42%/61% to a 6-day-old 10%/20%, the fresh anchor dropped, the instance-wide `rates.json` deleted, the attribution row archived — and `/api/usage` would have rendered "signed out since <5 days ago>" plus the amber warning for an account that works. `src/login-state.js` now answers TWO questions: `loginState` (the FILE) and `accountLoginState` (the ACCOUNT — file OR token, dying at the LATER instant, and an oat even DATES an account whose dir never existed). The callers choose deliberately, in writing: the panels and the repair ask the ACCOUNT question; `validateBillingSlot`/`memberLoginState` keep the FILE one, because a token stored in accounts.json cannot be delivered by re-pointing a symlink to a RUNNING CLI, and making the slot account-aware would turn a wiped member into a valid switch TARGET. `AccountManager.OAT_TTL_MS` now READS the constant from login-state (one definition, suite-pinned). ⑤ **`corroborated` survived the preserve-merge**: it is a verdict about ONE write, but `writeUsageCacheForKey`, `refreshViaCliPanel` and the remote-statusline harvest inherited the previous producer's, rendering "via own /usage panel · not corroborated" about a reading the account made about itself (a session-less producer has nothing to corroborate WITH). All three now apply `captureRateLimitEvent`'s own rule — `undefined ⇒ delete`, a verdict the caller DID supply is honoured (an unconditional delete would be accept-and-ignore) — and a drift guard over EXECUTABLE lines requires every usage-cache merge to DECIDE the label for its own write (the first version of that guard matched a comment and passed while the fix was reverted). ⑥ the kb's own stale sentences — the WALL essay a reader reaches FIRST still advertised `sessionReadingMember` as live and the credentials leg as "an unsatisfiable guard", both of which this change deletes and reverses — marked REFUTED in place, guarded PER MENTION (these essays are single giant paragraphs, so a line-level rule passes as soon as any other sentence on the line says REFUTED — measured). **R2 invariants**: a key rule that holds for ONE backend must SAY so (a resolver that absorbs another's job inherits every unstated assumption of the code it replaced); "X is the SINGLE writer" is an invariant only while every writer routes through X — a grep that passes because the second writer lives on another machine is the hole, not the proof; a repair that changes what a store KNOWS must tell every derived store what it changed (the re-bake's "leave it" rule was true only of the world before the repair ran); the credential FILE is not the ACCOUNT — ask the question the consumer actually has, and write down which consumers must NOT inherit the wider answer; provenance belongs to the write that made it (a merge preserves IDENTITY, never a VERDICT); and a guard that scans source must scan EXECUTABLE source at the granularity of the thing it guards. 

Gate: **scripts/test-readings-attribution.mjs (139)** — every producer × {before switch, after hot switch, after logout} against the REAL engine and a REAL pool with real per-session symlinks (driven through the producers, never by injecting a key), the pre-fix negative control (orgVerifiedKey's rule applied to the same state files the reading on the spawn-time org), the turn pin, the ledger, login-state incl. the satisfiable gap, the migration on a fixture shaped like this instance's stores, and a headless-chrome 375×667 leg of the real panel with a live-member negative control. `test-usage-anchors` and `test-auto-resume-loop` — both shipping OUTSIDE the release gate — were added to it in the same change.
## THE PROCESS LIST WENT EMPTY ON THE BUSIEST MACHINES, SILENTLY (2026-09-07, found by the release gate going red)

`test-sysinfo-op`'s oldest assert — "top procs with pid+rss" — went red on the dev box mid-session, then again on a re-run, having passed an hour earlier. The suite was innocent and so was the daemon: `sysinfo.read().procs` really was `[]` on a machine running 2,404 processes.

**The measurement.** `topProcs()` ran `ps aux --sort=-rss` through `execFile` with `maxBuffer: 4 * 1024 * 1024`. Command lines on a box hosting ~60 agent worktrees are long, so `ps aux` output is ~1.8 KB per process: measured **4,258,412 bytes at 2,404 processes** — past the cap. `execFile` kills the child and errors; the BSD fallback leg (`ps aux`, same 4 MiB) errors identically; and the code then did `resolve([])`. No log, no error field, no way to tell "this machine has no processes" from "we threw the answer away". The full-table twin `listProcs()` had the same shape with 8 MiB — `ps axo <cols>` measured 4,244,406 bytes, i.e. already at half its cap, and its cap is applied AFTER parsing, so the WHOLE table has to fit.

**Why it matters beyond a red suite.** Those rows feed the System panel's process manager and, worse, the memory-pressure alert's `top: …` line — which is printed *because* the machine is under pressure. The one artefact that says "here is what is eating the box" disappears exactly when the box is being eaten. This is the degrade-gracefully class again (2.276.0 writer sweep, 2.340.2 lost binding): **a catch broader than the failure it was written for hides its own bug, and only a path that logs the message VERBATIM ever gets found.**

**Fix.** One named constant `PS_MAX_BUFFER = 64 MiB` (~36k processes) shared by both readers, the `topProcs` timeout raised 5s→8s to match its twin, and BOTH final failure paths now `console.warn` the verbatim `ps` error saying the list will be empty. Nothing about the parse, the cap or the sampling changed.

**Invariants.** ① A buffer sized for a laptop is a silent truncation on a server — size it from a measurement and name the number in the comment. ② A degrade path that returns an EMPTY collection must say so out loud; an empty list and a discarded answer are different facts and callers cannot tell them apart. ③ A reader whose whole job is "what is this machine doing" must not be the thing that stops working when the machine is busy.

**Test.** scripts/test-sysinfo-op.mjs — a fake `ps` on `PATH` (in a child process, so `PATH` never leaks into the rest of the suite) emitting ~6 MiB of plausible output: the shipped module still returns rows, and the NEGATIVE CONTROL — the same table against a copy of `src/sysinfo.js` with the pre-fix 4 MiB constant spliced back in — returns the empty list this fixes. (An assert that cannot fail is not an assert.)

## STEER-ALL PUT 25 MESSAGES IN THE TURN AND SHOWED ONE (2026-09-07, owner "我刚才在那个codex session里全给插入了，但是我只能看到我最后插入的一条消息")

In a long-running codex session the owner pressed **Steer all** on a 25-item queue. Every steer succeeded — the model demonstrably got all 25 — and the chat window showed exactly ONE new bubble: the message typed after the last resume.

**Where the messages came from.** The app-server's input queue belongs to the THREAD, not to the wrapper. The live buffer of the wrapper that came up at 07:06:43 UTC carries `queue_changed n=25` two seconds after boot, and every one of those 25 `clientUserMessageId`s is a webui msgId minted **~10 hours earlier**, by the wrapper this one replaced. So the queue was INHERITED across a Terminate+Resume while `queueMeta` — and every bubble the wrapper had ever written — started empty (all 25 `queue_op_result` frames carry `msg_id: ""`; the single message with a real msgId is the one typed at 07:38:03, the one bubble the owner saw).

**The hole.** A user bubble comes from the record the wrapper writes as the text passes THROUGH it (`handleInput`). For a submission it never typed, the only live notice is the app-server's own carrier — `item/completed {item:{type:'userMessage', id, clientId, content}}` (0.153.4 `UserMessageThreadItem`; the rollout persists the same thing as `item_completed` / `UserMessage` / `client_id`, 28 of them at 07:38:44-45, each immediately after its `response_item`, with **no `user_message` event anywhere**: the queued path's carrier does not exist on the steered path). That kind fell off the end of `_handleItemCompletedInner` with **no branch and no breadcrumb**, so twelve-plus messages entered the turn in silence. Reproduced against the shipped wrapper in-suite: 12 steers OK, 1 bubble.

**A second defect the same session data exposed:** every message the user TYPES renders twice after a reload. One message is written by BOTH producers — us (as it is sent, carrying `webui_msg_id`) and codex (when the app-server commits it, carrying its own `msg_…` id and nothing of ours) — and `recordFingerprint` keys the first by ID and the second by CONTENT, so `mergeCodexRecords` dedupped neither. Measured through the REAL reader over the owner's real 66MB rollout + live buffer: 3/3 typed messages doubled.

**FIX, at three layers:**
1. **Wrapper (`data/bin/codex-chat-wrapper.js`).** `item/completed` routes `userMessage`: `recordInboundUserMessage(clientId, userInputToContent(content))` writes the bubble for any submission ENTERING the turn whose id we have not already recorded. A landed `turn/steer` records it AT ONCE, in queue order (the commit twins arrived 42s later on the owner's session — a message that went in must be visible when it went in; a REFUSED steer records nothing, that message is still queued). `recordedUserCids` remembers every id whose bubble already exists (chat-input msgId, the generated `queued-…` id, the peer cid), and an item with **no** clientId is ours by construction — only `turn/start` carries none. The record's `webui_queue_id` marker is out-of-band on both readers; the queue ROW advertises the same clientUserMessageId as its `msgId` so the strip row and the bubble's `Steered` chip join.
2. **Normalizer (`src/codex-message-manager.js`).** `webui_queue_id` is a SECOND-CLASS identity: stamped into `webuiMsgId` (`_stampUserIdentity`) so the chip joins, but it does not suppress peer detection (most inherited items were agent-to-agent messages and must keep their labelled card) and `recordKey` strips it like `webui_peer`, so both copies of one message mint the same id.
3. **Store (`src/codex-session-store.js`).** The inherited bubble's marker is stripped from `recordFingerprint` (both copies are written inside the turn that commits the message, so their turn-scoped content keys agree), and the ID-KEYED twin gets a **claim**: an id-carrying copy of ours claims its content key and the next codex-side copy of that content consumes it and is dropped — n copies in, n bubbles out. Ours survives, so the chip join and the live message id are preserved.

**INVARIANTS.** (a) **Every kind an upstream stream can emit is either routed or NAMED** — the wrapper is an allowlist too, and it had no `else`; a kind that is neither gets logged once verbatim and counted in `meta.unhandledItems` (`noteUnhandledItem` / `NO_RENDER_COMPLETED_ITEMS`), because the absence of that breadcrumb is exactly how `userMessage` stayed lost. (b) **State that outlives the process is not ours to assume**: the app-server's queue survives a wrapper restart, so "we have a bubble for everything in it" was only ever true for the wrapper that filled it — anything entering the turn under an id we do not know needs a record. (c) **A message that enters the turn must be visible when it enters**, not when the server gets round to committing it. (d) **The twin rule is FORWARD ONLY**: a claim covers copies that come after it (ours is always written first), because letting a late claim swallow an EARLIER codex record would delete an old message from history the moment its text was typed again after the buffer had rotated — a millisecond tie renders twice instead, since a duplicate is a nuisance and a deletion is data loss. (e) **A falsy id is not an identity** — a `webui_msg_id: ''` copy keys on content like codex's, or it is a stranger to its own twin.

**Gates.** scripts/test-codex-p2-wrapper.mjs ⑥ (the REAL wrapper against a stub that hands it an inherited queue: steer-all → 12 bubbles in order, the late commit twins add nothing, a DRAINED item gets its bubble from the twin alone, our own message is never re-recorded, the unhandled-kind breadcrumb — plus a PRE-FIX control run verbatim from `git show master:data/bin/codex-chat-wrapper.js`, which reproduces "12 steers, 1 bubble"). scripts/test-queue-steer.mjs ⑨ (the redacted rollout-shape fixture scripts/fixtures/codex-steer-all-rollout.jsonl → 12 bubbles from the rollout alone and 12 from the 24-record rebuild, with negative controls at 24 and 2; the drained cross-turn twin; two identical texts staying two; the forward-only guard; the peer-card guards; and a headless-chrome leg that renders the real normalizer's messages with the REAL renderer and counts twelve bubbles, ten of them chipped `Steered`).
## A CODEX CONVERSATION SAT IDLE FOR 32 HOURS WITH QUOTA AVAILABLE (2026-09-08; owner ruling: auto-resume is generic)

**Symptom.** codex thread `01a0733f…` (webui `sess-13-1788764799305`, backend codex, `accountId: null` = the machine's codex login, no pool) hit its usage limit and never came back on its own. `data/auto-resume.json` was `{"armed":{},"fires":{}}` and the journal held **zero** `[auto-resume]` lines for that session, ever.

**Timeline, from this instance's own stores (read-only).**
- `2026-09-07 13:16:40Z` — the app-server refuses the turn. **99** records in that thread's own rollout (last at `13:19:28.411Z`, re-counted 2026-09-08 17:5x PDT), all `codex_error_info: usage_limit_exceeded`, message *"You've hit your usage limit … or try again at Sep 13th, 2026 8:36 PM."* — `resetsAt: null`, `rateLimits: null`. The live-buffer count of the camelCase twin is **no longer reproducible**: that buffer was replaced when the owner resumed the thread (it now starts 2026-09-08 22:43Z), which is itself the reason a wire-shape claim has to be re-measured rather than inherited.
- `13:20:19Z` — the last reading: `limitId:'codex'`, sevenDay utilization **1.0**, `resetsAt 1789356983` (= Sun Sep 13 2026 20:36:23 PDT — the prose, to the second).
- 32.6 h of silence (`codex-rate-limits` telemetry gap 13:20:19.976Z → 21:55:00.608Z, 1955 min).
- `2026-09-08 21:55:00Z` — a FRESH window on the SAME lane: `limitId:'codex'`, sevenDay **0 %**, a NEW `resetsAt 1789509303`. Quota available. Nothing woke it.

**Four independent breaks, each sufficient on its own, all measured** (the fourth was found by re-measuring the wire while verifying the first three).
1. **The harness classifier looked for a word the CLI never says.** `EXHAUSTION_RE` matched `usage_limit_reached` and five workspace variants; the wire says `usage_limit_exceeded` (codex-core's rollout, snake_case) and `usageLimitExceeded` (the app-server, camelCase — its own 0.153.4 schema documents the translation: *"This translation layer make sure that we expose codex error code in camel case"*). Census over the whole local rollout corpus (re-run 2026-09-08): `codex_error_info` takes exactly three values — `usage_limit_exceeded` **102**, `cyber_policy` **6**, `unauthorized` **1** — and the regex matches **none** of them. `task_failed` never carried `codex_error_info` at all in any spelling the regex would take — so codex auto-resume was structurally dead from the day it shipped (2.368.20) and no test could see it, because every fixture spelled the enum the way the regex did.
2. **The wrapper read the error off the wrong object.** `turn/completed` with `status:'failed'` carries its `TurnError` on `params.turn.error` (0.153.4: `TurnCompletedNotification {threadId, turn}`, `Turn.error` — *"Only populated when the Turn's status is failed"*). The wrapper read `params.error`, which does not exist, and emitted `task_failed {error: '', /* no enum */}`.
3. **Nothing anywhere could act on "the window reopened EARLY".** The reset was SIX DAYS out, so `armIfEnabled`'s 26 h ceiling was RIGHT to refuse a timed wait — and then returned null, leaving the conversation with nothing watching. The only thing that could ever have continued it is the 21:55Z record, and a quota reading's ONLY effect on a waiting session was to DISARM it.
4. **And the enum's SHAPE was assumed, not measured** — the r4 lesson of the B3 batch, landing on the very field this release turns on. `CodexErrorInfo` is a serde **externally-tagged** enum, so a variant that carries data arrives as a ONE-KEY OBJECT. Verbatim from this instance's own live buffer (the incident thread's, `data/session-buffers/sess-13-1788764799305.buf`): `"codexErrorInfo":{"responseStreamDisconnected":{"httpStatusCode":null}}`. Every fixture in the repo — and the first draft of this release's own reader — assumed a bare string, and `String({…})` is `"[object Object]"`, which is **truthy**, so the object form did not even fall through to the message ladder: it was read as an unknown enum and dropped, silently, exactly like break ①. Whether `usageLimitExceeded` is itself a unit or a data variant in 0.153.4 could NOT be measured here (no such record survives, and `codex app-server generate-json-schema` is a REJECTED oracle — 7 INET connects incl. `chatgpt.com:443` even logged out, so asking it is a §ban-safety violation), so BOTH shapes are read rather than betting on one.

**The ruling.** *"auto resume 应该是通用的, 只要支持 hook/注入的 harness 都支持, 形式可以不一样 — 有些是发消息, 有些是 start turn 之类的固有指令."* The timer, the loop breaker, the notices, the tri-state gate and restart survival are VibeSpace's own and stay harness-neutral; exactly two facts are harness-specific and both move onto the DESCRIPTOR — the LIMIT SIGNAL (`quota.signalFromStream`, which already existed) and the RESUME VERB (`descriptor.resume {form, deliver}`: `message` / `turn-start` / `prompt` / null). `capsOf(backend).autoResume {signal, resume, supported}` is DERIVED from those two by the registry, and every surface that offers or acts on the feature reads that row.

**The fix, per break.** ① the enum is a closed SET seeded from the retired regex and extended with what the wire sends, compared case- and separator-insensitively (one list, not two), plus a LAST-RESORT parse of the CLI's own sentence for the reset the record omits (rounded UP a minute — late costs a tick, early costs a billed turn into a wall). ② the wrapper forwards `params.turn.error`'s message + typed enum, so ONE classifier reads both the `error` notification and a failed `turn/completed`. ③ a far reset is now a **WATCH**: still no timed continue (the ceiling is intact and costs nothing), but the fresh-window edge can keep the promise — and a quota reading on an ARMED session is JUDGED instead of disarming it (PURE `src/auto-resume-signal.js`). ④ `codexErrorEnum()` reads the enum as a string, as an externally-tagged one-key object, or as an internally-tagged `{type}` — the variant's own payload comes back with the name, because the http variants prove a variant can carry fields and a reset would ride exactly there; `classifyAuthFailure` reads the same field through it, so the two cannot disagree.

**Invariants.**
- **A capability that only one harness implements is a branch waiting to happen.** The two harness-specific halves are descriptor members and the caps row is DERIVED from what the descriptor really implements, so a new harness cannot inherit a control nothing serves, and `supported` cannot be hand-set on the surface that reads it.
- **An enum's SHAPE is the emitter's decision, not the schema's** — measure the bytes, and when the bytes are gone (buffers rotate) read BOTH plausible shapes rather than betting on one; `String(x)` on a wire value that can be an object yields a truthy `"[object Object]"` that no vocabulary matches and no degrade path notices. A vocabulary fix that only fixes the SPELLING re-ships the same silence.
- **An enum you match on is a MEASUREMENT, not a memory.** The retired names had been in the file since the feature shipped and nothing ever asked the corpus whether they occur; the answer was zero, twice over, in two different casings. Census the wire before believing a vocabulary — and keep the retired spelling as the seed of the new set so the diff shows what changed.
- **"Refuse to sit forever" and "watch for free" are different decisions.** Refusing a six-day TIMER was right; refusing to WATCH cost the user 32 hours. A ceiling on a promise about TIME must not silently also cancel the promise about EVIDENCE.
- **A reading is evidence about the WALL, not about the session.** Disarming on one is only harmless where readings cannot reach an idle session; codex proves that is a per-harness accident, not a rule. Judge the reading, and keep the promise when it says the wall is gone.
- **What a reading is ABOUT is three facts, not one.** Lane, bucket, and no sibling bucket spent — each of them a real way to fire a billed turn straight back into the wall (the measured `codex_bengalfox` lane read 0 % through the entire stall).
- **A re-verdict must not downgrade a wait it does not understand.** Two callers re-arm the same session from a verdict with no signals in hand; before they inherited the wall's lane/bucket they replaced a wall-aware wait with a lane-blind one that no reading could ever open — this release's own defect, one function later, caught by its own conformance leg.
- **The negative control is the pre-fix classifier, through the harness's public seam** (`signalFromStream`), so the 32-hour stall is reproduced with everything else — the real engine, the real pool, the real module, the real records — unchanged.

**r2 — THE FIX'S OWN LOOP, three findings from the adversarial verifier, all reproduced against the real modules before anything was changed.**

**① The fresh-window edge was a SUSTAINED burn, not a one-shot.** Round 1's `noteQuotaReading` put no per-arm limit on itself: every reading whose armed bucket read healthy re-entered `fireNow`, whose only ceilings are the loop breaker's — `FIRE_MAX_IMMEDIATE = 3` per **rolling hour** and a **ten-minute** quarantine. A WATCH stands until its far reset passes (six days in the incident), so the shape is a standing burn. Reproduced through the module's own public seam with a clock we own, against the REAL `src/server/auto-resume.js` as committed in round 1 (the incident's world: a watch six days out, the plan lane reading healthy every 30 s, the CLI answering every continue with another limit rejection): **[3,3,3,3] = 12 billed continues over four simulated hours, 492 "continuing now" journal lines for 480 readings**, arm still standing — ~72/day for the life of the wait. The engine's own comment had already claimed the opposite bound ("a healthy `codex` reading fires once, the CLI rejects, and the loop breaker quarantines the identity"): false in both halves, and the reason the loop was invisible. **The premise is reachable here today**: the exhaustion record carries `rateLimits: null` so the engine SYNTHESISES `limitId:'codex'`, and if the true wall is the model lane (`codex_bengalfox`, measured interleaving on this account minute by minute) the plan lane's very next push reads healthy about a wall it does not describe.

**Fix** = the edge is SINGLE-SHOT per WALL, and the shot is spent **at the delivery** (`edgeSpent` on the persisted breaker record, keyed `lane|bucket|scopedName`), cleared only by PROOF OF WORK. Stamping it at the REJECTION REPORT — the first draft, and the reviewer's own wording — is indistinguishable while every caller reports the outcome and is round 1's loop again the moment one does not: measured through the seam (arm → reading → fire → **no outcome reported** → re-arm) it gives [3,3,3,3] against 1. Today's engine does report it (`onWalledTurn` calls `noteFireOutcome(id,false)` first and is the only arm site carrying a wall), so the delivery-time stamp changes no production count as wired — it is there so the bound is a property of the module that SPENDS rather than an emergent property of five call sites plus a classifier, and this feature's own incident is a classifier that matched nothing for eight months. The key is deliberately NOT the reset instant, and both reasons were checked: on this instance's codex anchor stream (1799 readings / 30 days) the sevenDay reset carries **316 distinct values and changes between consecutive readings 414 times**, one wall being spelled two ways (`1789356983` ×417 beside `1789356984` ×3 — the ±wobble that made reading-lag.js compare weekly PHASE); and it would not cover this finding's own premise anyway, since the mis-attributed sibling lane carries a different reset BY CONSTRUCTION. A monthly SPEND CONTROL now opens nothing (`spendControlReached`, asked BEFORE the lane because it is a fact about the ACCOUNT — the codex twin of 2.361.2; unverified on the wire and it says so, which is safe because the rung can only REFUSE), while `credits.hasCredits === false` is the rung we did NOT write: measured, `usage-cache/__global_codex__.json` — the incident's own login — carries `hasCredits:false` while serving turns off plan quota, so reading it as spent would make the edge permanently inert here, i.e. the original incident re-introduced as a guard.

**② The branch's own leg for this was vacuous, which is why it shipped green.** Round 1's leg drove 8 readings and asserted `opened >= 1` (the WALL verdict — true whether or not a turn was billed) and `r.n > 0 || r.last` (true for n = 1 and for n = 999); `w.fired.length`, the only thing that costs money, was never read, and no hour boundary was crossed. Mutation-tested: with `FIRE_MAX_IMMEDIATE = 999`, `FIRE_QUARANTINE_MS = 0` and `FIRE_BACKOFF_MS = [0,0,0]` — the loop breaker's caps removed outright — the suite still reported ALL PASS. It is now an upper bound on billed fires across four simulated hours with three patched-copy controls (round 1's un-guarded edge; round 1's optimistic log; this fix's own draft), the constant is pinned to a literal a human agreed to as well as read back from the module, and **the suite that OWNS the breaker drives the new producer** (test-auto-resume-loop §4g: the real `recordRateLimitEvent`, the real engine, the real pool, its own clock — because the breaker's backoff is a wall-clock rule and 40 readings inside one real second are bounded by it whatever the edge does, which would have made the control read 1 against 1 and prove nothing). Under the same mutation both suites now go red.

**③ The journal announced continues that mostly never happened.** The line was written once per READING, before the attempt — 492 lines for 12 continues (41:1) — burying the exact channel this incident was diagnosed from ("ZERO [auto-resume] lines for that session"), while the branch had already added a per-verdict throttle for the negative side citing that very rule. It now speaks AFTER the attempt and says what happened: `continued now` only for a delivered continue, a breaker refusal once per `REFUSE_LOG_MS`, and a refusal by this rung reported to the caller by NAME (`already-refuted`, with `wallOpen:true` so it can never be printed as "the wall is still up").

**r2 invariants** = **a reading is a CLAIM and the CLI's answer outranks it** (a producer pushing every few seconds must not be able to re-buy a verdict it has already been refuted on); **a money bound belongs to the module that SPENDS, not to a caller's classification** (stamp at the spend, not at the report about it — and say so where the caller's comment claims otherwise); **a comment that credits a mechanism is an assertion — measure it or delete it** (the quarantine is ten minutes; the hourly cap it really rested on resets every hour, forever); **an assert that counts VERDICTS does not bound SPEND** (`open` is true whether or not a turn was billed, and `n > 0` is true for 999); **the suite that owns a budget must DRIVE every new producer that spends it**, not grep for it; and **a fixture with no clock cannot measure a rule made of clocks** — the world has to be able to reach the hour boundary the loop lives on.

Gates: test-auto-resume (200), test-harness-contract (306, the conformance run + the backend-id census), test-quota-source (112 — the shape legs drive the REAL object-form record from the incident buffer as a negative control, with the pre-fix `String(enum)` reader as the mutation), test-codex-p2-wrapper (382), test-auto-resume-loop (200), test-new-member-wake (119, master's own wake suite — its `attemptFire` wiring pin now names the ROUTE rather than the exact argument list, since a reading-driven fire carries its wall).


## AUTO-RESUME FIRED 130 TIMES INTO A WALL (2026-09-07, owner's frozen journal + screenshot; owner decision ut-1c6c15a2db items ①④ — items ② ledger backfill and ③ projection-driven re-reads stay OPEN)

**Symptom.** One conversation was auto-continued **130 times** between 23:32 and 04:03 (a second one 32 times, up to two per SECOND). Every continue was a billed turn the CLI answered with "You've hit your session limit · resets 6am". About 150 of the conversation's last 150 messages (2295–2445 of 2445) were the same three junk artefacts repeating: the CLI's error line, VibeSpace's injected "自动续跑" prompt, and a "账号池已切换到 Member Y，已自动继续这个任务。" card.

**What the evidence said.** Every cycle in the journal is byte-identical apart from timestamps:

```
[auto-resume] <id>: armed for <now+45s> (switched to a usable account)
[pool] per-session switch <pool>/<id>: <observed> (observed; linked <link>) → <link> (re-point, same target)
[auto-resume] <id>: 账号池已切换到 <link> — continued immediately
```

740 of those `(re-point, same target)` lines — the pool "switching" a session to the member it was already linked to. The final arm at 04:03:48, once a real reading finally landed, names every member dead **including the one the engine had spent four and a half hours calling usable**, and ends `— session observed on Member F while linked to Member B`.

**Root cause (two independent defects; both reproduced before either was touched, scripts/test-auto-resume-loop.mjs).**

① **Attribution.** B-2c9b (plan B) made the OTel-observed org override the link for BLOCKING decisions, on the premise that "a hot-switched CLI keeps its old token, so the observation names what it is burning". The owner's post-mortem corrected the premise: `organization.id` on an `api_request` is the identity the CLI cached in its **config dir at SPAWN**, not the token that authorized the request — the credential file itself IS re-read, which is exactly what the re-point's mtime bump exists for. So every rejection was filed against the org the session had STARTED on, the linked member — whose credentials the process actually reads — never received a wall verdict and stayed "healthy" in the cache forever, `quotaVerdictFor` answered "usable via \<link>" on every cycle, the per-session pass decided FROM the (dead) observed member and therefore "switched" to the link it was already on, and the hot-switch path called `fireNow()`.

② **No memory.** Neither fire path remembered that the previous continue onto that exact identity had been rejected seconds earlier, and `fireNow` skipped the pre-fire gate (`beforeFire`) that the timed path runs — so the one component that re-probes and re-verdicts before spending was bypassed by the path that fired 130 times.

**The fix.**

- **A rejection is a fact about the credential SLOT.** (Since round 3 a rejection RECORD reads the TURN-PINNED form `rejectionSlotFor` — the raw resolver below is only for "where would a fire land right now"; see Round 3 ①.) `wallSlotFor(session)` resolves the session's own per-session symlink (or the pool's default link) and VALIDATES it — a real member of this pool (`validateBillingSlot`; named failures `no-slot` / `slot-not-a-member` / `slot-unreadable` — ~~`poolMembers()` already filters by loggedIn, so a separate credentials leg would be an unsatisfiable guard~~ — **REFUTED 2026-09-07**: `parseAuth` answers `loggedIn:true` for a doubly-expired login, so the leg is satisfiable and `validateBillingSlot` now carries it (`slot-wiped`/`slot-expired`/…); the suite still pins that every named reason is reachable). Its `slotOk` verdict travels **on the wall signal**, because it is only true at the moment the rejection ARRIVES: by the time the turn ends the pool has usually re-pointed the link off the account that just refused us (measured — the first version validated at turn end and reported "not this session's credential slot" about the very account that had rejected it).
- **One function no longer answers two questions.** `sessionBillingMember` (link + slot validation) answers every BLOCKING question: the demotion target, the verdict order and `viaId`, the per-session switch's `currentId`, both quota-probe targets, `_wallScope`, and the loop breaker's fire identity. ~~`sessionReadingMember` (observed over link — the old body verbatim) answers only `resolveUsageKey`~~ — **REFUTED AND DELETED the next day (2026-09-07 readings-by-slot)**: a utilization **number** does describe whatever token produced it, but the observation never NAMED that token (it names the identity cached at SPAWN), so the values half was wrong in exactly the way the rejections half had just been fixed. `readingSlotFor` — the same validated, turn-pinned credential slot — now answers for readings too, and `resolveUsageKey` resolves `sessionBillingMember`.
- **The verification ladder gains a top rung.** A wall on the session's own validated slot is ground truth by itself — we know what the symlink points at, which is strictly better evidence than a telemetry attribute. Every other account still needs the 2.368.34 corroboration (≥2 walled turns in 120s, or it IS the observed org), and the journal names which rung authorised it: `(1 walls / credential slot)`, `(1 walls / observed-org)`, `(2 walls / 2-walls)`.
- **A per-SESSION memory.** `noteSessionWall(sid, member)` records "this member rejected THIS conversation" for 10 minutes — written even when the demotion is HELD, because "it rejected me" is true either way — and is unioned with the breaker's `recentFireFailures`. `quotaVerdictFor` cannot answer `usable` through such a member, and `decidePoolSwitch({exclude})` cannot pick one. `exclude` is a NAMED pure input reported as `all-rejected`, never a silently empty candidate list; `pessimism` was the wrong tool because "it just said no to this conversation" is not a percentage, and docking it would let a big enough gap re-select the member on the very next tick.
- **The loop breaker** (auto-resume.js). A fire the session answers with another limit rejection is a FAILED fire; the record `{identityKey, at, count}` is per session and is PERSISTED with the armed waits, because a deploy must not hand the loop a fresh budget. Four named refusals: `fire-pending` (never two continues in flight), `same-identity` (10min quarantine), `backoff` (immediate fires 0 / 60s / 5min), `hourly-cap` (3 immediate fires per session per hour, after which only the TIMED path — the one anchored to a real reset — may fire). Both paths became ONE `attemptFire`, so the immediate path runs the same `beforeFire` gate; `_arFiring` is raised BEFORE the gate is called, because the real gate runs `maybePoolAutoSwitch` → `fireNow` and everything before its first `await` is synchronous re-entry (measured 1992 levels deep with the flag one line late). `onWalledTurn` reports the failure FIRST, before anything re-arms or re-switches — that ordering is the off-switch — and `noteRecovered` clears the breaker BEFORE its armed-record early return, since a fire has already deleted the armed record (round 4: …and only when the caller's evidence is WORK — see the classification table below).
- **One honest line.** When the breaker trips the session is told once per window: "账号池已切换到 X，但它同样被用量上限拒绝… 将在 T 重置后自动继续" or "…且暂时没有可用的成员 — 可以添加成员、把这个会话切到别的账号，或等待配额重置". The success line ("已切换到 X，已自动继续") is likewise once per distinct target per session per window; the incident wrote it ~150 times. A refused TIMED fire stays journal-only — it self-heals inside 10 minutes, so saying it would be noise.

**Invariants.**
- A fire answered by a limit rejection is a FAILED fire, and that fact must survive a restart.
- "Where do I fire" and "who rejected me" must be the SAME fact, or the breaker quarantines an account that never rejected anything.
- A judgement about "which account is this session on" is only valid at the instant the evidence arrives — by the end of the turn our own pool switch has rewritten it. Carry the verdict on the signal.
- A telemetry attribute is not a credential. Values belong to the token that produced them; a REJECTION belongs to the credential slot that was read. We OWN the symlink — that is the stronger evidence.
- Every "no way out" outcome must speak (one operator line, one user line), but an outcome that self-heals in minutes must stay quiet.
- The negative control for a two-defect incident is a MATRIX, not a snapshot: re-create each pre-fix behaviour through a public seam and show that removing either one alone stops the failure (both off ⇒ ≥10 unbounded fires here).

**Round 2 (adversarial verifier; four defects, all reproduced through the real engine before anything was touched).**

- **① The "one honest line" was one line for four different facts (medium).** `logRefusal` computed the named check `chk` and then dropped it: `breakerNotice` hard-coded the exhaustion narrative for EVERY reason. Reproduced at module level and through the real engine + real pool: the first continue goes out onto Account A, the pool re-points onto healthy Account B, the second attempt is refused `fire-pending`, and the conversation is told *"账号池已切换到 Account B，但它同样被用量上限拒绝，且暂时没有可用的成员。已停止反复重试…"* — **B had never been fired at, B and the rest of the pool were healthy, and the session was still ARMED** (it continues ~15 s later on the tick). Worse, that card SPENT the once-per-window budget, so the genuine "nothing can serve you" line was then suppressed for the rest of the hour (measured: the real same-identity refusal that followed said nothing at all). The harsh variant is the one users hit, because it triggers when `resetsAt <= now + 5min` and the near-arm is always `now+45s`. Fix: the text is a PURE function of the refusal reason (`refusalNoticeFor`), and each class has its OWN budget. `same-identity` may say "it refused us too"; the additional clause "and there is nowhere else to go" requires a SECOND fact — the pool's own no-target verdict, handed over by `noteNoPoolTarget` from the `all-rejected`/`no-members`/`stuck` branch of the per-session pass — never an inference from a refusal reason. `hourly-cap` says exactly what it knows (N tries this window, immediate retries paused). `backoff` and `fire-pending` are JOURNAL-ONLY and spend nothing: they are sub-minute pacers on a promise that is still being kept, and no-silent-failures is about a BROKEN promise. A card that says "stopped retrying" seconds before retrying is a lie the user has to un-learn.
- **② The fire was recorded against the account we LEFT (medium).** `attemptFire` resolved the identity BEFORE the pre-fire gate — and the gate is `beforeAutoResumeFire`, which runs `maybePoolAutoSwitch` and can re-point the credential link. Reproduced with a real pool {DeadOne (5h at 1), HealthyOne (5%)}: the tick's gate moved the link, the continue was delivered while linked to HealthyOne, and `_fires.last.key` said DeadOne — so a rejection would quarantine the account we had already left, leave the REAL rejector fireable, journal the wrong name, and dedup the card under the wrong key. This is precisely the invariant round 1 wrote above `identityFor()` ("the fire onto X failed" and "X rejected this session" must name the same X), broken in the one case that comment warns about. Fix: `deliver()` re-resolves the identity after the gate, re-runs `canFire` on it (a gate that moves us onto an identity we already burned this window ABORTS instead of spending), and keys `noteFired`/`announce`/the journal to where the continue actually landed.
- **③ The card claimed the quota had reset when the pool had swapped accounts (low).** The timed path announced "用量上限已重置" unconditionally — and after round 1 that is the DOMINANT recovery path (see ④), so the common case now told the user something false. Fix: the wording comes from what actually unblocked us — `kind === 'now'` (always a pool switch), a near-arm reason (`switched to a usable account` / `account usable again`), or the identity having MOVED during the gate; only with none of the three is "the limit reset" the reason. A drift guard pins the two arm-reason strings against the engine that writes them.
- **④ The suite certified an ordering production does not take (low).** §2's leg "a hot per-session switch still continues the armed session immediately" INJECTED the wall signal, which skips the early `maybePoolAutoSwitch` that BOTH real producers run. Measured on one world, changing only the producer: **injected `noteWallSignal` ⇒ 1 immediate continue; real `recordRateLimitEvent` ⇒ 0; real `markLimitBanner` ⇒ 0.** The link is re-pointed to the healthy member BEFORE `onWalledTurn` arms the session, so the `finally` pass has nothing left to switch and `fireNow` is never reached; the session is armed at +45 s and continued by the TICK — **45 s arm + 15 s grace on a 30 s tick ⇒ ~60–90 s, not "immediately"**. §2 now drives both real producers and pins that latency, keeps the injected leg only as a labelled artificial control, and adds the case the immediate path actually exists for (c1206711: a switch that lands LATER onto an already-armed session — driven through `maybePoolAutoSwitchForPool`, the engine's own seam, with the wall-clock eval gate and dwell belt wound back instead of slept through). §4b's "the cache is untouched" had the same shape — it only held because the signal was injected; a real rejection writes its own reading (`source: rate-limit-event`, utilization 1) through `captureRateLimitEvent` before the ladder runs. It now asserts the LADDER's output (no `wall`-sourced write, the named hold/`not-a-member` verdict) and a companion leg drives the real producer to show both writers.

**Round-2 invariants.**
- A refusal may only claim what its OWN reason knows. Four named reasons are not one message with four spellings, and the clause "there is nowhere else to go" belongs to whoever computes it (the pool), not to whoever noticed a retry was too soon.
- A notice budget is per CLAIM, not per module: one shared stamp lets a frequency notice eat the only line that carries an actionable fact.
- No-silent-failures covers a BROKEN promise. A pacing refusal on a session that is still armed and does continue is journal-only — narrating it is noise that the next event contradicts.
- Any state a GATE can change must be re-read after the gate. Resolving an identity, then running a gate whose whole job is to re-point that identity, then recording the pre-gate value, mis-keys everything downstream — and the re-read must re-run the admission check, not just relabel.
- The text that explains a billed turn must name the fact that caused it. "The limit reset" and "the pool switched" are different events; deriving both from the armed record (plus "did the gate move us") keeps one source for the card and the journal line.
- **A test that INJECTS a signal has chosen an ordering.** When two producers exist, the suite must drive the real ones and state the measured latency; the injected leg survives only as a labelled control, next to the measurement that shows why it differs.

**Round 3 (adversarial verifier; both defects reproduced on the real engine + a real pool before anything was touched).**

- **① The slot was resolved per RECORD, and the first record moves the link (high).** ONE rejection reaches us as SEVERAL records — the CLI's `rate_limit_event`, the assistant limit banner, and a banner inside a `task_notification` (three producers, src/server/stdout/claude-stream-json.js :323/:328/:563; `noteWallSignal`'s own comment already states that a banner and its rejected event are ONE wall). Every producer calls `maybePoolAutoSwitch` the moment it marks the cache, so the FIRST record re-points the link by itself — and round 2 asked `wallSlotFor` again for the SECOND one. Reproduced in both producer orders and in the two-rejected-events shape: `recordRateLimitEvent` keys correctly to LINK and its switch moves the link to SPARE; `markLimitBanner` then resolves the slot to **SPARE**, marks SPARE's cache, and its signal makes `demoteWalledAccount` demote SPARE `(1 walls / credential slot)` and `noteSessionWall(SPARE)`. The healthy member the pool had just rescued us with was thereby marked exhausted **with the branch's own top-rung authority** and excluded from this session for 10 minutes — the exact misattribution the branch exists to remove, now upgraded from "held" (base HELD it: `single wall on SPARE (session not observed there)`) to "authoritative". Harm measured against a FRESH ground-truth reading showing SPARE 95% healthy: verdict `usable:false, blockedUntil:+2h` instead of `usable via SPARE` — a multi-hour wait with a healthy member sitting in the pool, where the pinned code near-arms and continues in ~60 s. (In the 7d variant the healthy member's weekly bucket is stamped exhausted with a reset THREE DAYS out.) Fix: `rejectionSlotFor(session)` — `wallSlotFor` PINNED FOR THE TURN. The first keyed signal of `_turnWallSigs` owns the key and its `slotOk` for the whole turn; both rejection producers call it; `wallSlotFor` stays the fresh reading for `wallKeyFor`/`fireIdentityFor` ("where would a CONTINUE land right now" is a different question) and for the pin's own no-signal fallback. The pin is the TURN, not a time window, because that is what defines it: a pool re-point does not reach a RUNNING CLI (2.361.0 — a process kept billing its original org for 35 minutes across four switches), so every rejection inside one turn came from the credentials the slot named when that turn's first rejection arrived. `noteTurnEnd` clears the signals, so the pin is exactly one turn wide, and a turn whose end record never arrives degrades the way the wall machine already does (the next turn's real work classifies it NORMAL and clears them).
- **② A near-arm guard that could never be true, pinned as if it were the protection (low).** Round 2 refused to near-arm when `v.viaId === demoted.key`. That branch is unreachable: `demoteWalledAccount` calls `noteSessionWall(member)` on EVERY path that resolves a member (held demotions included) and `quotaVerdictFor` forces `usable:false` for that set, so `viaId` can never be the rejector; the only `demoted.key` returned without a session wall is `not-a-member`, whose key by construction matches no member id either. The suite pinned it by source grep, so no test ever executed it — the repo's 恒假守卫 lesson, with the added cost that the pin read stronger than the code was. Fix: the branch now re-reads the SAME store the verdict read (`nearArmVeto(v.viaId, sessionWalledMembers(id))`, PURE and exported), logs `INVARIANT VIOLATED` via `console.warn` when the two disagree, and says in its own comment that it is an ASSERTION and names the mechanism that actually protects. The suite executes the assertion's truth table (walled ⇒ violation, not-walled ⇒ null as the negative control, missing viaId/set ⇒ null, array form identical) AND executes the real protection functionally: a member that walled this session is never the verdict's way out even with its cache reading healthy and the demotion HELD — with "nothing walled ⇒ it IS the way out" as the control.

**Round-3 invariants.**
- **ONE event, SEVERAL records: resolve the mutable fact ONCE per event, not once per record** — especially when handling the first record CHANGES that fact. The `slot` flag already carried "this was true when the rejection arrived"; the KEY needed the same treatment.
- Pin by what DEFINES the scope (the turn — one CLI process, one token) rather than by a time window; a window admits every coincidence that falls inside it and excludes everyone slower.
- A guard that cannot fire must not be pinned as protection. Either delete it, or keep it as a loud invariant assertion that SAYS so and let the suite execute the mechanism that really protects.
- A negative control for a per-record/per-event fix re-creates the un-pinned resolution through the same public seam, and the fix's own legs must go red without it (mutation-tested: pin off ⇒ 19 asserts red; the near-arm wiring reverted ⇒ its pin red; the session wall dropped from the demote path ⇒ 5 red, from the held path ⇒ 6 red).

**Round 4 (adversarial verifier; reproduced on the real engine + a real pool + both real producers before anything was touched).**

- **The breaker's whole memory was cleared by a PASSIVE READING (medium).** `noteRecovered(id, why)` ran `noteFireOutcome(id, true, why)` unconditionally, and `noteFireOutcome(_, true)` **deletes the record**: the failed-fire quarantine, the 3-per-hour immediate counter, both once-per-window notice budgets, and the persisted copy on disk. But `noteRecovered` is not one signal — its loudest caller is the engine's non-rejected `rate_limit_event` (usage-pool-engine, "fresh non-rejected reading"), the record the CLI emits *whenever quota info changes*, which this instance sees roughly **20× per rejection**, plus its codex twin (`rate_limits_updated` with nothing exhausted). A reading says a bucket has room; it says nothing about whether the conversation produced a token. Reproduced end-to-end: a real continue delivered onto Member B, the CLI answers with another `rate_limit_event status:"rejected"`, the breaker records `fails:[{key:SPARE}]` and spends its exhaustion notice — then ONE `status:"allowed"` reading through `recordRateLimitEvent` and the record is gone from memory *and* from `data/auto-resume.json` (`fires: {}`), `canFire(SPARE,'now')` answers `ok:true`, and the identity that had just rejected our continue is immediately fireable again. So neither budget the round-1 spec requires was ever enforced in production — the loop the breaker exists to break, one reading later. Fix: `noteRecovered(id, why, { worked = true } = {})`, and `noteFireOutcome(id, true, why)` runs **only when `worked`**. The DISARM stays unconditional for every caller (a fire onto a session that is no longer waiting is the wasted billed turn this call has always existed to prevent) — only the breaker reads the classification.

**Who may clear the loop breaker — every `noteRecovered` caller, classified.** The table is executable: test-auto-resume-loop §5 DERIVES the call sites (walk `src/`, keep whatever mentions `noteRecovered`, paren-match each call, read its `why` string and whether it passes `worked:false`) and fails on any site missing from the table *and* on any dead row — a hand-written inventory is the tool this codebase has watched fail four times (B-3185 r7).

| call site | `why` | worked | because |
|---|---|---|---|
| usage-pool-engine `noteTurnEnd` | `turn completed normally` | **yes** | the turn ended with real output. This is also how a *successful continue* clears the breaker: its own turn completes and reaches this line |
| ws-handler chat-input | `user sent a prompt` | **yes** | a human took the conversation over — exactly who the budget was protecting |
| usage-pool-engine `recordRateLimitEvent` | `fresh non-rejected reading` | no | a bucket has room; nothing about this conversation. The ~20×-per-rejection event that caused this defect |
| usage-pool-engine `recordCodexQuotaSignal` | `fresh non-limited codex reading` | no | the codex twin of the same reading |
| usage-pool-engine `reset_credit_result` | `codex reset credit consumed` | no | the LIMIT moved on that identity; the conversation still produced nothing. Nothing on this path fires (the disarm above already removed the wait), so the classification only decides whether a live quarantine survives — and it self-expires in 10min, the same floor `tryResetCredit` itself paces on |
| auto-resume `tick` | `disabled` | no | the feature was switched off under a live arm; a toggle off/on must not hand the loop a fresh budget any more than a deploy may |

**Round-4 invariants.**
- **A "the session is fine now" signal is not one signal.** Before letting a call clear state that BOUNDS SPENDING, ask what its cheapest caller actually knows — the passive readings outnumber the rejections ~20:1 here, so the rare caller's semantics were being written by the common one.
- **Disarm and de-quarantine are different decisions with different evidence bars.** Dropping a wait is cheap and self-correcting; forgetting that an identity rejected our last continue re-opens a billed loop. One function may serve both, but only if each half reads its own evidence.
- **The classification must be enforced where it is DECLARED, not trusted.** `worked` defaults to true (so the two proof-of-work callers stay unchanged), which means a new caller that says nothing gets the dangerous answer — acceptable only because the suite derives every call site and fails on an unclassified one before it can reach production.
- Negative control = the pre-fix CALL, replayed through a public seam: the engine reaches auto-resume through `getAutoResume()`, so handing it a wrapper that drops the third argument re-creates round 3 exactly, with both real producers untouched. With one reading per cycle it re-fires **25/25** cycles (≥10 = unbounded); with the classification honoured the same world stops at ≤3 and the quarantine holds. Mutation-tested three ways: the `if (worked)` guard removed ⇒ 13 asserts red (incl. `fires=25`); the claude call site's `{worked:false}` removed ⇒ 12 red (the codex legs stay green, so each call site is separately load-bearing, and the audit pin names the unlisted site); and the guard-removal is also run from a MUTATED COPY of the product source inside the suite so the guard itself has a leg that dies with it.

**Gates.** scripts/test-auto-resume-loop.mjs (186; each round-2/round-3/round-4 fix mutation-tested — re-applying the earlier behaviour turns 1–19 asserts red, including the exact round-1 card text) + scripts/test-auto-resume.mjs (128; §11 was rewritten to RECORD the refuted plan-B rule rather than delete it) + test-pool-auto (67) + test-account-pool (33) + test-account-verdicts (45) + test-otel-truth (43) + test-rate-limit-capture (42) + test-codex-pool (61).

## THE INSTANCE DEFAULT ATE THE CONVERSATION'S OWN VALUE ON EVERY RESUME (B-6b6d, owner ruling 2026-09-07 — option ①)

**What it was.** 2.369.62 fixed the record that STATED a turn's effort but disclosed the reason the wrong value was there in the first place: `src/lib/session-lifecycle.js` resolved `sessionEffort = effort !== undefined ? effort : defaults.effort` (and the identical `sessionModel` line) on EVERY create — including the ones that carry a `resumeId`. So a resume that supplied nothing (the chat resume bar, resume-all, a card with no ⚙ override, `restartConversationInPlace`, a fork) shipped `codex.defaultEffort` on the wire, ws-create's B-21e4 continuity fallback was gated on `if (!sessionSpec.env.CODEX_WEBUI_EFFORT)` — i.e. on the client having sent nothing — and therefore could not fire exactly when it was needed. A conversation the owner had set to `ultra` resumed at `xhigh`, and the wrapper then synthesized a `turn_context` saying so.

**The ruling.** The conversation's OWN value wins on resume/fork/restart. The instance default (`<prefix>.defaultModel` / `.defaultEffort`) is a NEW-session default. An explicit per-session pick (card ⚙, Session Properties, the New Session dialog's pickers) still beats both.

**The shape.** One PURE module, `src/resume-continuity.js` — `resumeSpawnPick({explicit, conversation, instanceDefault, resume, hasSource})` → `{value, origin}` with `origin ∈ chosen | conversation | instance | harness` — and TWO callers:
- **the client** (session-lifecycle's `createSession`) calls it with `hasSource:false`, which is the honest client-side fact: only the server can read a conversation's own records. On a continuation it resolves to `''` and the client sends NOTHING. `permission`/`extraArgs` deliberately keep master's behaviour (the ruling covers model+effort; a permission mode IS recoverable from claude's init record, so changing it is its own product decision ⇒ owner gate).
- **the server** (ws-create, immediately BEFORE `buildSessionArgs`) calls it with the conversation's value read through the harness descriptor's `store.lastTurn<Knob>` hook, and writes the resolved values back onto `data.model`/`data.effort` so every downstream reader (pool chooser's declared model, `_spawnModel`, the model lock's implicit target, `session._effort`) sees the value the session really starts with.

**`hasSource` is not a second declaration.** It is derived from the descriptor hook's PRESENCE, so a harness either ships the reader or it does not — there is no `resumeContinuity` caps boolean to drift out of sync with it. codex ships both (`lastTurnModel`/`lastTurnEffort` → the rollout's last `turn_context`, the B-21e4 readers moved onto the descriptor); **claude declares NEITHER** (round 2 — round 1 shipped a model reader and it is removed, see below); **opencode declares the model** (`serve.facts().sessionModel` — OpenCode's own session record names it, v1 list only so a resume adds no route the sidebar poll does not already use; `''` when the opt-in background service is off, which falls to the instance default and is logged). A generic ACP harness declares neither, and `src/harnesses/acp.js` says why in place rather than leaving it to be re-derived.

**THE ASYMMETRY, stated because it looks like an omission.** On a knob with NO conversation source, a resume sends **nothing** rather than the instance default — nothing claude writes records the effort a turn ran at, so an unconditional fallback there would mean "the instance default applies to every claude resume", which is the defect itself wearing a fallback's clothes. With a source that came up EMPTY (a thread resumed before its first turn) the instance default IS honest, and the ladder's caller logs the decision (`continuityLogLine`) so a resume that quietly took the default is readable afterwards.

**The origin is STATED, never derived.** A conversation's own value and the instance default are frequently the same string, and only the server ever read the conversation's records — so the panel cannot work out where a value came from. On a NEW session the client resolves the instance default itself, and the wire cannot tell that apart from a pick, so it also sends `spawnOriginHint` and the server reconciles through `applyOriginHint` (round 3 ②). The server records `_modelOrigin`/`_effortOrigin` on the session, persists them in session-meta (all three boot-restore paths — and every writer that RE-AUTHORS one persists it too, round 3 ①), and ships them on the `created` reply (the creator never gets an `attached` — 2.368.4), the attach payload, and the active-sessions payload. `spawnValueOrigin(stated, live, picked)` in agent-meta only decides how to SHOW it: a pick saved AFTER the spawn still flips the row to `spawn`, so the origin can never contradict the "(saved: X — applies on the next resume)" note beside it, and a session that predates the field falls back to `responseStyleOrigin`'s comparison rather than claiming a fact we do not have.

**Two twins fixed on the way past.** (a) `outputStyle` was added to the active-sessions payload in 2.369.58 but never to the sidebar MERGE, so Session Properties' "live" response-style value was silently always empty and every session read as `saved`; the B-6b6d rows land in both merge branches WITH it. (b) On the `created` reply the effort now rides along with the origin, because on a resume the client deliberately sends none — without it the chip on a resumed window would stay empty.

**Boundaries, stated rather than discovered later.** ① A REMOTE resume reads the conversation's records out of the local transcript CACHE (both locators already scan `data/remote-jsonl`), so its answer is as fresh as the last fetch — still the conversation's own value, just possibly an older one; the alternative (an unrelated instance default) is strictly worse, and this is the behaviour the codex twin has had since 2.369.32. ② The read costs one directory scan + one bounded tail read per resume, and only when the client sent NO pick — an explicit pick short-circuits it, because the ladder would discard the answer anyway. ③ `permission` and `extraArgs` still take the instance default on a resume: the ruling covers model+effort, and a permission mode IS recoverable (claude's init record), so moving it is its own product decision.

**Invariants.** ① An instance default is a NEW-object default; on a continuation, the object's own recorded value wins, and an explicit per-object choice wins over both. ② A fallback gated on "the caller sent nothing" is dead code for as long as some other layer always sends something — when you add such a gate, name the layer that must stay silent and pin it. ③ Do not declare the same capability twice: derive "can this harness answer?" from the presence of the reader, never from a parallel boolean. ④ Where a value came from is a FACT the resolver holds and no one downstream can reconstruct — state it on the record instead of guessing from the value. ⑤ A knob with no recoverable history must send nothing rather than a default, or the default becomes the answer for every continuation. ⑥ A row that states two facts must not let the ellipsis eat the second one (375×667: the origin is the last thing on the line — measured, then wrapped).

**ROUND 2 (adversarial verifier: 1 major + 4 medium/low, every one reproduced on real data first).**

**① THE CLAUDE MODEL SOURCE IS GONE, NOT REPAIRED (major + medium, one root cause).** Round 1's reader answered a different question from the one the ladder asks. The ladder asks *what should this resume COMMAND*; `message.model` says *which model SERVED that turn*, and it says it in a form that cannot express the conversation's context-window variant. Measured over the whole local corpus — 2650 transcripts, 3.5 GB under `~/.claude/projects` — **zero** `"model":"…"` values carry a `[…]` suffix, while the CLI's own `system/model_refusal_fallback` records prove conversations running `claude-fable-5[1m]` (5 sightings). So `--model claude-fable-5` on the resume of a `claude-fable-5[1m]` conversation silently turns 1M of context into 200k — on a path where master commanded nothing at all, since the client had just stopped sending `claude.defaultModel` there. It was also wrong about the model itself: a classifier reroute is recorded as an ASSISTANT record whose `message.model` is ALREADY the fallback target and which carries a `{type:'fallback',from,to}` content block, and the `system/model_refusal_fallback` record round 1's guard looked for is written **2–10 lines AFTER it** — so the ≤20-line BACKWARD look-back matched **1 of 139** reroutes in the owner's transcript, while **13,694 of 81,707** main-thread assistant records (16.8%, median run 87, max 1647) sit inside such a reroute run, i.e. ~1 in 6 interruption points would have resumed pinned to the fallback (and `_spawnModel`/`familyOfModel` would then have sent the plan-C pool chooser after an Opus-quota account for a Fable session). Round 1's own suite stayed green because its fixture wrote the system record BEFORE the assistant record it describes. **Fixing the guard would have left the variant loss**, and a rung that can never produce a commandable answer must not ship as the capability (the 2.369.66 rule), so the SOURCE goes: `lastClaudeTurnModel` and the claude descriptor's `store.lastTurnModel` are deleted, a claude resume commands NEITHER knob, and the CLI's own session record — which is variant-exact — decides. The asymmetry stops being an odd exception and becomes one sentence: **claude records neither knob in a form a spawn can command.** The reason lives in `src/session-store.js` where the reader used to be, so the next person to want one reads it first.

**② AN EXPLICIT "Auto (model default)" MUST SURVIVE (medium).** The New Session dialog always sends a defined string and its FIRST option is `''` (“Auto (model default)” / “Default”).
