The three outcomes (`VS_GONE`/`VS_OK`/`VS_NOTAGENT`) and the `ps`-based existence test are unchanged (`kill -0` would report another user's process as gone). **Enumerate kill paths as kill paths: the sweep is not the only thing in a product that sends a signal.** The rest of the sweep, with reasons, is short and now written down: the codex argv leg (`*codex*resume*<RID>*` / `CODEX_WEBUI_RESUME_ID=<RID>`) is an OWNERSHIP test, not an identity test — it names the exact conversation, which is self-evidencing, and the shape it catches is deliberately not the CLI binary; every other kill in the tree selects by something WE own (our dtach socket path, our own child pid, a plugin's own install root), never by what a command line says. (t) THE SURVIVING TWIN'S RECORD WAS WRONG — corrected above; a twin you keep must be recorded with the radius it actually has, or the next reviewer accepts a kill-path rule on the strength of a discovery-path justification. It is now pinned as discovery-only: one caller (`isLockClaude`, itself reached from ONE place — the lock probe in the discovery scan), asserted by name, and its sync twin asserted GONE. The honest edge: a lock this rung credits WRONGLY still puts that pid on a card, and the sidebar's Terminate hands that pid to `/api/kill-pid` — but the ROUTE re-asks THE identity and refuses, which is precisely why the gate belongs where the SIGTERM is and not on the label that led there. (u) THE TWO SPELLINGS DISAGREED ON A NEWLINE INSIDE AN argv WORD. argv is a list of NUL-separated words; the JS twin reads that list, but the shell turned NULs into newlines and took the Nth LINE — the FIRST LINE of the Nth word. Verified on live processes, in both directions and both harmful: argv[0] `/usr/bin/claude<LF>/usr/bin/tail` is basename `tail` to JS and `claude` to the old shell (the sweep kills a reader), and `<dir>/dir<LF>name/claude` is the reverse (a real writer survives the sweep, and the double-writer corruption happens). The shell now parks real newlines on `\001` across the line select and restores them after, so "the Nth line" IS "the Nth NUL-record" in both spellings, and the `ps` fallback flattens its blob the way the JS twin's `/\s+/` split always did. **AND THAT WAS ONLY HALF OF IT — the round-4 fix was itself measured, and the value still had to survive `$(…)`, which strips EVERY trailing newline.** So a word (or a path) that ENDS in one still read `…/claude` in the shell and `…/claude<LF>` in JS, and the shell's answer is the PERMISSIVE one, on the paths that kill: verified on live processes for all three captures — argv[0] `…/claude<LF>` (rung 1), argv[0] `…/claude<0x01>` (the park byte itself comes back out as a newline, so it was eaten too — the code comment claiming that residue "can only make a word look LESS like the CLI" was false for a TRAILING one), and `/proc/<pid>/exe` `…/claude<LF>` under a launcher argv[0] (rung 3, which no argv fixture can reach). One helper `vs_cap` now captures all three: a sentinel byte appended INSIDE the subshell protects the tail, then exactly ONE terminator — the one the producer itself printed — comes off. **A fix for "the two spellings read this structure differently" is not done until the value has been carried all the way to the comparison: the transport (`$(…)`) mangles it too, and a partial fix leaves the harmful direction alive.** **When one spelling of a rule can read a structure the other cannot, the difference is not academic — it is a different answer on the input that carries the structure.** **R5 REVIEW ROUND — three defects, and two of them are the same shape: a rung the harness cannot reach, and a name in a comment that no longer exists. (v) THE EXISTENCE PROBE IN FRONT OF THE KILL WAS BUSYBOX-BLIND. r4 fixed WHO `killRemotePid` may SIGTERM and left the question BEFORE it — does this pid exist? — spelled `C=$(ps -p N -o args=)`, with the reason written down that `kill -0` would report another user's live process as gone. busybox `ps` has no `-p` at all (measured here, busybox 1.37.0: `ps: invalid option -- 'p'` plus a usage block on stderr, exit 1, stdout EMPTY), and the capture's whole point is `2>/dev/null` — so on a host whose login shell lives in busybox (Alpine images, embedded boxes, any `ash` rootfs, and remember BOTH ssh rungs hand this text to the REMOTE USER'S shell) EVERY Terminate answered `VS_GONE` and the route returned `{success:true, gone:true}` while NOTHING had been signalled: the user was told the process was already gone, the card flipped, and the CLI kept running and kept writing. **A capability the probe does not have must never read as a FACT about the subject.** The fix is a ladder in which every rung is POSITIVE evidence: `kill -0` (POSIX, a shell builtin everywhere — its SUCCESS is proof; r4's objection was only ever about its FAILURE, where EPERM and ESRCH share one exit status, so a failure is handed to the next rung instead of believed), then `[ -d /proc/N ]` (Linux incl. every busybox host, world-visible for a process we may not signal), then `ps -p N` (the no-/proc rung — BSD/macOS `ps` does have `-p`). And a second, quieter lie went with it: once the pid is known to exist, `vs_is_cli` answering NO has two very different causes — we READ its argv/exe and it is a `tail` (a finding), or we could read NEITHER (no /proc AND a `ps` that cannot answer, i.e. exactly the busybox combination). `vs_known` separates them, so the outcomes are FOUR: `VS_GONE` / `VS_OK` / `VS_NOTAGENT` / `VS_UNKNOWN` (alive, no evidence readable), and `killRemotePid` THROWS with an explanation on the last two instead of manufacturing a success. The enumerated sibling `src/server/sysinfo-wiring.js signalProc` keeps its `ps -p`, with the reason re-derived rather than inherited: there it sits on the FAILURE branch of a kill that was already ATTEMPTED, so it can mislabel EPERM as ESRCH but can never report a kill that did not happen as a success — pinned by asserting the `kill` precedes any `ps -p` in its script. (w) THE `ps` FALLBACK RUNG OF THE r4 FIX HAD NEVER BEEN RUN. `vs_argv`'s `else` branch only executes where there is no /proc, r4 changed it (`tr '\n' ' '` before awk) and justified it with a sentence about the JS twin — but every fixture on a Linux box takes the /proc rung, so the claim shipped as prose. It is now driven with NO edit to the text under test, by two environment substitutions that together spell "this machine has no /proc, and its `ps` is not procps": the `/proc/$1/` root is re-rooted (three literals, asserted — re-rooting only the cmdline read leaves rung 3 answering from `/proc/<pid>/exe`, and the `ps` rung never decides anything), and a stand-in `ps` goes first on PATH. The stand-in is asserted to be NECESSARY, not assumed: measured here, procps `ps -o args=` renders an argv word's embedded newline as a SPACE, so this box's own `ps` cannot produce the multi-line blob the flattening exists for — and the branch only ever runs on a platform whose `ps` this box does not have. The JS side is DRIVEN too, not mirrored: a child node process whose two /proc reads throw runs the SHIPPED `procArgv`/`isCliProcess`. Result: the two spellings return the identical word for argv[0..2] and the identical verdict on three live fixtures, and the verbatim pre-r4 line (no flattening — awk then prints field i of EVERY line) answers the OPPOSITE of the JS twin on BOTH newline fixtures while remaining indistinguishable on a plain one. **A rung whose test can only take the other rung is a rung with no test.** (x) A COMMENT ABOUT IDENTITY NAMED A FUNCTION THAT NO LONGER EXISTS. scripts/test-local-discovery-device.mjs — the page that ARGUES why a second spelling of "is this pid claude?" is allowed to survive — still credited the local rung to `isProcessClaude`, the sync twin r4 DELETED, and still glossed `comm` as "the name of the file that was EXEC'd", which r4 had just measured to be false as an identity claim. On that page, of all pages, a stale name is an invitation to re-add the retired rule. Corrected to `isProcessClaudeAsync` reached from `isLockClaude`'s no-`procStart` fallback (which is why that fixture's lock deliberately carries no numeric `procStart`), and the note now carries a DRIFT GUARD: every identity function it names must be DEFINED in one of the three modules it attributes them to, and the deleted sync twin must be nameable nowhere — not in the note, not in session-store's code — with the verbatim pre-r5 sentence as the negative control. **Documentation that justifies keeping a twin is load-bearing: guard its names the way the code's names are guarded.** **R6 REVIEW ROUND — one defect, and it is r5's own escape hatch: (y) THE ENUMERATED SIBLING WAS NOT SAFE, AND THE REASON THAT SAID SO ONLY DESCRIBED HALF OF IT. r5 fixed `hosts.js killPidShell`, ENUMERATED `src/server/sysinfo-wiring.js signalProc` as the other kill path, and let it keep its `ps -p` on a hand-written reason: 'there it sits on the FAILURE branch of a kill that was already ATTEMPTED, so it can mislabel EPERM as ESRCH but can never report a kill that did not happen as a success' — pinned by asserting the `kill` precedes any `ps -p`. That script has TWO `ps -p` calls and the sentence is true of the SECOND only. The FIRST is the post-signal aliveness check on the SUCCESS branch, and there a busybox-blind probe does not mislabel an outcome, it MANUFACTURES one: measured here (busybox 1.37.0; fixture `trap \"\" TERM; sleep 60`) the pre-r6 script answers `OK-GONE` for a process that is still running, so `signalProc` returns `{ok:true, gone:true}` — the process-manager row flips to gone, the user believes the Terminate worked, and the CLI keeps writing. The failure branch's mislabel is real too and also measured: a live pid this uid may not signal answered `ESRCH` = 'no such process (already gone)' rather than EPERM. **A twin kept alive by a comment is a twin, and the comment is only as good as its author's count of the call sites** — the ordering pin was satisfiable and true and still let the defect through, because it asked about ORDER when the question was CAPABILITY. The fix is therefore not a second, better reason: `vs_alive` moved into src/cli-identity.js beside the identity text (same three transports, same remote-login-shell floor, dependency-free so the daemon still bundles it) and BOTH scripts embed it, so no per-site judgement remains to be got wrong; `signalVerdictScript(pid, sig)` is module-level and EXPORTED so the suite drives the shipped bytes and validates pid/signal AT the builder. And the class gets a STANDING SWEEP rather than a memory: no `ps -p` may serve as an EXISTENCE test anywhere on a kill/signal path (cli-identity, writer-sweep, hosts, sysinfo-wiring, session-store, routes/sessions, the agentd installer, every tracked data/bin twin), every surviving occurrence is listed WITH its reason — all of them ARGV/environ READS whose existence question was already settled by `kill -0`, plus the one no-/proc rung inside `vs_alive` itself — and a DEAD allowlist entry fails the suite too, because an exemption nobody prunes is exactly how this one survived a round of review. Honest edge, pinned: a ZOMBIE reads ALIVE to the ladder, to procps `ps -p` and to the local branch's `process.kill(pid,0)` alike — unchanged, kernel-level, and the reason §17's fixtures are double-forked orphans.** **R7 REVIEW ROUND — one defect, and it is the instrument rather than the rule: THE STANDING SWEEP WAS GATED ON A HAND-WRITTEN LIST.** r6 replaced "a twin kept alive by a comment" with a sweep, and then handed that sweep a literal array of seven files (plus `git ls-files data/bin`). A hand list is the SAME instrument this bug has already defeated three times — r4 found the retired rule alive on a kill path nobody had counted, r5 counted the sibling and let it go, r6 found that r5's reason for letting it go described only the second of that script's two `ps -p` calls — so "no `ps -p` existence test on ANY kill/signal path" was enforced exactly where its author had already looked. Measured: the r6 list missed **47** files that really do send or probe a signal, among them `server.js` (it wires signalProc), `src/jobs.js` (verified handle-kills), `src/server/boot-restore.js` (dtach retire = SIGTERM + unlink), `src/plugins.js` / `src/vnc.js` / `src/port-forward.js` / `src/opencode-serve.js` (each stops a daemon it started), `src/agentd/agentd.js` (device-side session pipes) and `src/ws-handler.js` / `src/ws-create.js` (the kill + teardown cases) — every one a place the next probe could land without reddening a thing, and the sweep would still have printed ALL PASS. The fix is that the file set is DERIVED by grep from the question the rule is about: every non-test file under `src/`, `data/bin/` and `scripts/`, plus the repo-ROOT scripts where `server.js` lives, whose text sends or probes a signal (`kill -TERM/-9/-0/…`, `SIG*`, `pkill`, `process.kill(`, `killRemotePid`, `signalProc`, `vs_alive`); extension-less files enter by SHEBANG, which is also how a GENERATED agent CLI (`vibespace-status` is deliberately untracked) is reached at all — `git ls-files data/bin` could never see it. The derived list is PRINTED by the suite, so the reviewer reads what was swept instead of a comment's count of it, and the grep is deliberately OVER-inclusive (the i18n dictionaries match the word `SIGSTOP`, the adapters match `SIGINT`): a false positive only widens where the rule is enforced, a false negative is the whole defect. Two structural pins keep the instrument honest — the derivation must lose nothing the r6 hand list covered (this reddens if the walk roots are narrowed) and it must still cover the ten named permanent kill paths the hand list missed (this reddens if anyone re-narrows to a hand list). And because a sweep only ever run against a clean tree is a sweep proven to say nothing, the SHIPPED sweep is parameterised by ROOT and run a second time against a temp tree built to contain each shape: the retired `ps -p N >/dev/null 2>&1` probe on a brand-new kill path IS caught; an ALLOWLISTED line copied into another file is still stray (the exemption is per file, it does not travel with the text); a kill path with no `ps -p` is swept and stays green; a `ps -p` in a file that signals nothing is out of scope, stated rather than assumed; the suites are excluded, which is the only reason this file may write the retired shape down as a control; and a shebang tool under `data/bin` is caught. A last leg closes the one hole a source scan structurally cannot see: every exported shell BUILDER (`pidAliveShellFn`, `cliIdentityShellFns`, `fdScanShellFns`, `writerSweepScript` claude+codex, `killPidShell`, `codexOpenRolloutsShell`, `signalVerdictScript`) is swept on the text it EMITS, with the verbatim pre-r6 script as that scanner's negative control.  A rebuilt tree caught a real hole in the first cut of this leg: `data/bin/vibespace-agentd.js` is the esbuild bundle of `src/agentd/**`, so it carries cli-identity's and writer-sweep's `ps -p` lines VERBATIM under a filename no per-file allowlist can ever hold — the sweep went red on a CLEAN tree the moment anyone ran `npm run build` (measured). GENERATED files (gitignored = not part of the shipped source) are therefore a THIRD bucket rather than a skip or an allowlist row, and their rule is stricter and needs no author: every `ps -p` line in them must appear VERBATIM in an AUTHORED file the sweep already walked, so a build step cannot introduce a probe and the reason still lives with the author (controls both ways: a bundle that only COPIES an authored probe passes; the same bundle with one line its sources never contained is caught). **The invariant: a standing sweep is only as good as the set it walks — derive the set from the property, never enumerate it, and print what was walked.** Suite: scripts/test-writer-sweep.mjs (239) — writers swept / readers survived incl. the suite's own process under a SIGTERM trap and two copies of it run from a `.claude/worktrees/` checkout, the rung-3 identity matrix (one binary, three argv[0]s) with r1's rung reverted against the live helper as the control, the awk attribution probe with the sticky-`p` revert as the control, the discovery-twin legs (structural + functional + exit status), the two `(deleted)`-image writers (one per exe rung) with the strip reverted as the control, the cross-login-shell verdict parity (dash/bash/busybox/zsh/ksh) with the UNQUOTED `(deleted)` pattern as the control, the JS⇄shell identity parity matrix and the retired JS rule as ITS control, the `vs_argv` silencing control on a socket, the two newline-in-argv[0] fixtures (one per direction) with the pre-r4 `vs_argv` reader as the control, the three TRAILING-byte fixtures (argv[0] in LF, argv[0] in `\001`, an exe PATH in LF) with the three pre-`vs_cap` `$(…)` captures as the control — reverted in the product, five asserts go red including the 46-pair live parity matrix, §14's KILL-PATH legs — the real `killPidShell` text run against real processes (a `tail -f` on a claude transcript and on a codex rollout REFUSED and alive, a real `claude` and a real `codex` terminated, `VS_GONE`, a non-integer pid rejected at the builder) with the verbatim pre-r4 remote line KILLING its own victim as the control, `killRemotePid` ITSELF with the device link stubbed to run the script locally (a builder referenced as a free identifier throws only when the METHOD runs — the lost-binding class no structural pin can see), plus `/api/kill-pid` driven through the real express router (the reader refused 400 and alive; the npm-shape CLI the comm rule could never see terminated) with the retired comm rule as ITS control — the structural pins (one /proc walk, no per-process ls, no whole-argv test, every `kill -TERM` line carries `SWEPT:`, killRemotePid builds from killPidShell, the surviving comm twin has exactly one caller and no sync twin) proven by reintroducing each retired shape, the boot-restore wiring pin, and a self-checking pin on this very number — plus §15's no-/proc `ps`-rung parity (re-rooted /proc + a stand-in `ps`, the JS twin driven into its own fallback in a child, the pre-r4 line as the control) and §16's busybox kill-path legs (the pre-r5 script answering VS_GONE for a live reader AND a live `claude` under busybox as the control, the shipped script refusing/terminating/reporting-gone correctly there, VS_UNKNOWN reached by re-rooting /proc, the caller's four outcomes, and the enumerated `signalProc` sibling, whose pin r6 replaced with the shared-text one) — plus §17's SIGNAL-verdict legs: the measured busybox blindness of the quiet `ps -p N >/dev/null` form, the verbatim pre-r6 script answering OK-GONE for a LIVE `trap "" TERM` process as the control, the shipped builder answering OK-ALIVE on that same process and OK-GONE on one that really dies (fully reaped, asserted via /proc, which is why the fixtures are double-forked orphans), ESRCH for a pid that was never there, a REAL EPERM leg on a live pid this uid may not signal with the pre-r6 ESRCH as its control (skipped-with-a-reason where the environment cannot produce one), signalProc's own four-verdict mapping through a stubbed device link, the builder's pid/signal validation, and the STANDING SWEEP over every kill/signal-path file with its reasoned allowlist. Revert-verified: putting the pre-r6 probe back in the product turns 5 asserts red (naming both offending lines by file:line), a brand-new `ps -p` existence test elsewhere turns the sweep red on its own, and a STALE allowlist entry turns two red. r7 revert-verified the same way: reverting the derivation to the r6 hand list turns the regression pin red (it names the 47 files that fall out), and each of the six temp-root controls is a live measurement of the shipped sweep rather than a description of it. test-local-discovery-device is 9 (the three new asserts are the note's drift guard); test-sysinfo-op is 29 (its two "probe existence via ps -p" pins became three: no bare `kill -0` split, no bare `ps -p <pid>`, and the shared ladder embedded verbatim).

**ROUND 2 — three defects an adversarial verifier reproduced against that fix, and the deeper one the reproduction exposed.**

1. **The MAJOR (its own scenario).** Round 1 keyed the inherited bubble on its CONTENT so codex's copy would collapse onto it — so two INHERITED items with the SAME text steered into one turn were two bubbles live and ONE after a reload. A steered message deleted, live and reload disagreeing, in the exact case the fix exists for ('continue', 'go on', identical agent-to-agent notices — a 25-item queue makes that ordinary).
2. **Measuring it turned up the pre-existing twin.** Codex's OWN user records were content-keyed too. On the OWNER'S rollout — the session in the report — a reload rendered **68 of its 95 user messages: 27 deleted**; across the local corpus, **133 deleted over 89 rollouts**. Every single collision carried a different `msg_…` id AND a different `create_time` (0 same-id, 0 same-create_time): distinct submissions, minutes apart, not one genuine duplicate among them.
3. **A phantom spinner (minor).** The inherited bubble deliberately skips `_finalizeStreaming` (it lands mid-turn), but it was still a hard `break` in that function's backward scan — so a reply left open when the turn ended stayed `status:'streaming'` forever on every rebuild: a spinner on a dead, read-only conversation. The same mechanism the peer-card exemption on that line was written for.
4. **A claim that leaks deletes (minor).** A twin claim short-circuited by the fingerprint's duplicate check was never retired, and the leaked claim later ate a legitimate codex-only record — the deletion the forward-only rule exists to prevent, arriving through the back door.

**THE ROUND-2 RULE: a user submission's identity is the ID its producer minted, never its text.** Ours is keyed by `userRecordIdentity` (webui_msg_id / client_msg_id / **webui_queue_id** — the cid a previous wrapper minted names the same submission, so one namespace), codex's by `codexRecordIdentity` (`msg_…`, its own namespace). Records with no id on either side (pre-0.15x rollouts: 144/572 in the corpus, all ≤2026-05, no file mixed) keep the content key — but ours and codex's stay in SEPARATE content namespaces, so the duplicate check can only ever collapse ONE producer's own record, which is what makes "a record dropped as a duplicate already had its twin effect applied" true and the leak in (4) structurally impossible. The ours↔codex pair is retired by the CLAIM alone, and now EVERY copy of ours claims (round 1 claimed only the id-keyed ones because a content-keyed copy of ours collided with codex's on the fingerprint; that collision is gone).

**AND THE ASYMMETRY THE CLAIM CANNOT COVER.** A forward claim assumes ours is written first. Two producers of ours are written LAST: the `item/completed` twin for an item the app-server DRAINED (it emits that only after persisting its own record) and the IDLE peer path (`turn/start` commits before `recordPeerMessage` runs — test-peer-msg-card had pinned exactly that order since 2.362.2 and caught the first, provenance-blind attempt at this rule). Such a record marks itself `webui_after_commit` and YIELDS to an unconsumed codex copy of the same content **in the same turn** — it drops ITSELF, never deletes anything — while an early copy never yields, so a same-text neighbour can never swallow one. `webui_queue_via` ('steered'|'drained') stays as forensics; the READER reads the commit-order fact only, because reading the queue label would have made the rule queue-only.

**ROUND-2 INVARIANTS.** (f) **Identity before content**: keying a record by what it SAYS collapses distinct facts — the corpus number (133 + 27 real deletions) is what that costs. (g) **A duplicate is only a duplicate within one producer**: two producers' records must never share a key namespace, or the dedupe silently means two different things. (h) **A message that does not CLOSE a turn's open streams must not HIDE them either** — the exemption is the same one, on both sides of that scan. (i) **Every claim must be retired on every path that drops the record standing for it**, including the duplicate short-circuit; an unretired claim is a deletion waiting for the same text to appear again. (j) **A producer that writes after the commit must SAY so**: the wrapper's three writers of a user record are now a census the suite counts, and a fourth answers "had the app-server committed it yet?" before it ships. (k) A negative control is a PATCHED COPY of the real module (asserted to have matched), never a hand-written imitation of what I believe the old code did.

**Round-2 gates.** scripts/test-queue-steer.mjs ⑨(j) — the two same-text steers (live + rebuild + a control at ONE bubble), two distinct codex submissions of one text (rollout alone + a control that deletes the third), a fork replay still collapsing, the drain twin (+ a control that doubles it when the record is marked early), the leaked-claim scenario, the legacy retirement (+ its control), the stranded stream (+ a control that leaves it streaming), and the producer census. scripts/test-codex-p2-wrapper.mjs ⑥ — the REAL wrapper's own records rebuilt against synthesized codex copies (14 messages × 2 producers → 14 bubbles, in order), and each inherited record naming its producer. scripts/test-peer-msg-card.mjs — both peer orders, with the idle path declaring the commit order the wrapper now writes.

**ROUND 3 — two more the next adversarial verifier reproduced, and the third producer the measurement found.**

5. **The twin that could never collapse: an ATTACHMENT (minor, but it hit every image message).** Round 2's own comment claimed our copy was "BYTE-IDENTICAL to codex's rollout copy … for text and data-URL images". It was true of `userInputToContent` and false of its sibling three hundred lines below: `handleInput` hand-rolled `content: [...attachments.map(input_image), ...(text ? [input_text])]` — **attachments first** — while codex persists what `encodeUserInput` SENT, i.e. the text first. Measured on the whole local corpus (97 plain rollout files, **5489 user records**): **0** begin with an `input_image`, every `input_text` block is exactly `{type,text}`, and **34 of the 40** `input_image` blocks carry a third key `detail` that only codex writes. Two different keys ⇒ no claim made, no claim consumed, **two bubbles for one message** — reproduced, with the text-only control on the identical scaffold at one.
6. **A claim whose twin NEVER ARRIVES (minor) — round-2 finding ④ through a second back door.** Round 2 retired a claim when a *duplicate* codex record was short-circuited by `seen`; it did nothing for a claim whose twin never existed at all. Our copy is written BEFORE the submission reaches the app-server, so there are whole classes of send that never become a user message: every wrapper-served slash command (`/compact`, `/review`, `/model`, `/effort` — answered by the wrapper, `record()` runs before `applySlashCommand`), every send whose RPC throws, and every queued item Stop or the user removes before it runs. Reproduced against the shipped module: ours `continue` in turn A + a codex-only `continue` in turn B ⇒ ONE bubble, **the turn-B message deleted**, at any turn distance. Codex's copy carries no client id (all 389 user records in the 2026-09 corpus have only `internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough`), so the pair genuinely cannot be joined by id — the claim must be **withheld** instead.
7. **THE THIRD PRODUCER, found by measuring (5) end to end.** `CodexAdapter._buildUserPreview` builds the same hand-rolled attachments-first array on the SERVER and ws-handler appends it to `session.buffer`, so a typed message has **two copies of ours** in the buffer, both carrying the same `webui_msg_id`. The preview is written first, so it WINS the fingerprint and **its** content key is the one that claims — a fix that only straightened the wrapper's spelling would have changed nothing in production. Measured: preview + wrapper + codex ⇒ 2 bubbles before, 1 after.

**THE ROUND-3 RULES.** (l) **One message, one spelling, across every producer of our own record.** Our copy is `userInputToContent(encodeUserInput(text, attachments))` in the wrapper and the same order in the server preview; a field only ONE producer writes (codex's `detail`) is normalised out of the twin key, exactly as `TWIN_VOLATILE_FIELDS` does at the record level — inside the content array now. (m) **A submission that never reaches the app-server claims nothing.** Every such case says so out of line — the `webui_user_retracted` event, which names the record by IDENTITY (round 3 also declared the known-in-advance case on the record with `webui_no_commit`; **round 4 deleted that half, see below**) (a data URL can be megabytes, and re-deriving the reader's content key inside the wrapper would be a second copy of that algorithm). The merge maps identity → the content key that claimed under it and withdraws exactly one claim; two different retractions of one record never go negative, an identical one from two sources is folded by the fingerprint, and a retraction naming an id we never emitted is a no-op. **The BUBBLE always stays** — the user really sent that text, `task_failed` is what reports the failure, only the claim goes; and an item the app-server had already DRAINED (`{deleted:false}` = it RAN) is never retracted. (n) **The queued PEER copy finally has an identity**: the app-server cid it was queued under, carried as the same second-class `webui_queue_id` an inherited bubble uses (never `webui_msg_id`, which suppresses the peer card) — so Stop can retract it by name, and two peer messages with the SAME text inside one turn stop collapsing into one, which was round-1's MAJOR still open for the one producer that minted no id.

**ROUND 4 — THE HALF OF (m) THAT COULD NEVER RUN, AND A FILE NO SEARCH COULD READ.** Two findings, both reproduced first.

8. **The write-time declaration was INERT in production (minor, but it silenced its own defect).** Round 3's own finding (7) had already named the third producer: `CodexAdapter._buildUserPreview` writes the SERVER's copy of a typed submission into `session.buffer` (ws-handler) under the SAME `webui_msg_id`, it lands FIRST, and **it is therefore the copy that wins the fingerprint and makes the claim**. That copy carries no marker and cannot: only the wrapper knows which texts are its own slash commands (`SLASH_COMMANDS` is advertised through `wrapper_meta`), and a second regex in the adapter is precisely the drift round 3 wrote its comment block to prevent. So `webui_no_commit` was written onto the copy nobody reads — measured on the production scaffold (preview + wrapper + a later codex-only `/compact`): **1 bubble, the turn-9 message deleted**, exactly the defect (m) was written against. The r3 regression leg was green only because its scaffold omitted the preview — the very producer the same round had identified. **The fix uses the mechanism that already survives the preview**: the wrapper retracts by ID (`retractUserRecord(msg.msgId, 'served by the wrapper as a slash command')`), and the id is a fact BOTH copies carry. It is withdrawn **before** `applySlashCommand` runs, not after: `/compact` takes 1–2 minutes and a wrapper killed inside that window would otherwise leave a claim standing over a message it never sent. One gate now decides both (`isWrapperSlashCommand`), so the two cannot disagree. The marker is gone from every producer; it survives on `WEBUI_USER_MARKERS` as a **compat strip only** (wrappers outlive an update inside dtach — 2.361.1 — and a record from an r3-era wrapper must still key like the same message, or those buffers double every typed message on reload).
9. **Two raw NUL bytes made a module invisible to search (minor).** `src/codex-session-store.js` used `\x00` — the actual byte — as the separator inside the per-turn Map key, and echoed it in the comment above. `file(1)` reported "data"; `grep -n USER_RETRACTION_EVENT` **on the very file that defines that constant** printed nothing and exited 1; ripgrep answered "binary file matches" with no line. Every search-driven read of that module — a review, an incident, a twin sweep — skipped it silently. Fixed by spelling it `\u0000` (byte-identical at runtime, verified), and the same class was found in `src/incident.js` (the `startsWith` guard against transcript-service's `DEVICE_HANDLE` sentinel, on master since B-8ebb; the raw byte made that file binary too and the guard read like a typo, so it is now spelled as an escape WITH the comment naming the convention — behaviour untouched).

**THE ROUND-4 RULES.** (o) **A declaration is only worth what the reader can see.** When two producers write the same fact, a marker on one of them is not a rule — it is a coin flip decided by write order. State it about the SUBMISSION (an id both copies carry), not about one copy of it. (p) **A guard that must be withdrawn later should be withdrawn at the moment of decision**, not after the long operation it guards — a process killed in between leaves the wrong state permanent. (q) **A regression leg must be built from the producers PRODUCTION has**, not from the ones the fix happens to touch: the r3 leg passed on a scaffold missing the producer its own commit message named. (r) **Source files are text.** A control byte in source removes the whole file from every text tool the team uses; a separator that cannot collide with the data is a fine idea, spelled as an escape.

**Round-4 gates.** scripts/test-codex-p2-wrapper.mjs ⑦ — the REAL wrapper now retracts the slash command (asserted BEFORE `compact_started` in the event stream, and its record asserted to declare nothing), the retraction census is SIX, and the rebuild scaffold carries **one server preview per chat-input, built by the real `CodexAdapter._buildUserPreview` from the same frame the client sent** — 12 bubbles, with two controls: strip all retractions (5 messages deleted) and strip ONLY the slash command's (the turn-9 `/compact` deleted, 11 of 12). scripts/test-queue-steer.mjs ⑨(j9) — the production scaffold end to end, plus **round 3's own reader rebuilt by patch**: green on r3's scaffold (wrapper record only, 2 bubbles) and red on the production one (preview first, 1 bubble) — the finding pinned as a control; a census asserting no producer writes the marker; the legacy marker still stripped from the fingerprint, the twin content key and the normalizer's record key. scripts/test-architecture.mjs #42 — no file under `src/`, `data/bin/` or `scripts/` carries a NUL byte (binary fixtures excluded by extension), with a temp-directory control that plants one and confirms neither the `\u0000` escape nor a `.zst` fixture is a false positive; verified RED against the pre-fix bytes (**that WALK is superseded by round 5 below** — it read the working tree, which is where the product installs its own 64 MB `rclone`).

**ROUND 5 — THE CENSUS THAT READ THE WORKING TREE INSTEAD OF THE SOURCE.** One finding, MAJOR, reproduced first.

10. **The NUL census failed `npm run build` on every instance that had ever used a storage mount (major).** Round 4's #42 walked `src/`, `data/bin/` and `scripts/` with `readdirSync` — but **`data/bin` is where the product installs its own runtime artifacts**: `installRclone()` writes a 64 MB arch-specific `rclone` there (gitignored since 2.368.9, boot self-heals it when missing), next to `rclone-dl.zip` and the boot-generated `vibespace-status` / `vibespace-agentd.js`. Byte 7 of that download is a NUL, so the assert read `✗ … (data/bin/rclone (byte 7, line 1))` and the build exited 1 — reproduced by copying the production instance's own 63,668,408-byte `rclone` into a clean worktree. The blast radius is what makes it major: `npm run build` runs the suite AFTER esbuild, and that build is the pre-push **release gate** and the in-app **"Update VibeSpace…"** step (`scripts/update.sh`), so an affected instance could neither push nor self-update — the service simply never restarted. The extension blocklist could not save it (the artifact has no extension, and the *next* 64 MB download would evade a new entry too), and neither could a path exclusion: the census must still read the tracked CLIs that live in that very directory. **The fix changes what "source" means**: the file list comes from `git ls-files -z -- src data/bin scripts` (432 files; ~3 ms to list, ~25 ms to read the 9.3 MB — where the walk it replaced read that 64 MB binary in full just to look at byte 7), so every gitignored or otherwise untracked runtime artifact is out of scope BY CONSTRUCTION. Tracked-but-deleted files are skipped; the binary-fixture extension exclusion stays for tracked `.zst`/`.png`/… fixtures. When git cannot answer for the tree — no git binary on PATH, or no metadata at all (tarball / `git-archive` / `npm pack`) — the source list is unknowable and the census **SKIPS with a reason** instead of failing: verified both ways (a `git archive` export of this branch, and the suite run under `env -i PATH=/tmp/empty-path-dir`, both green with the 64 MB binary present).

**THE ROUND-5 RULES.** (s) **A census over "the code" must read the SOURCE LIST, not the working tree** — the working tree also holds what the product itself writes there, and a gate that a normal user action turns red is a gate that gets bypassed. `git ls-files` is the structural answer; an extension/name blocklist is a guess that the next artifact invalidates. (t) **A check that cannot establish its own scope must SKIP with a reason, never fail** — failing a build over files it was never meant to read is a false positive with the blast radius of the release gate. (u) **An assertion that can pass vacuously is not an assertion**: the census now also pins that its listing really covers `src/` + `scripts/` + `data/bin/` (naming the incident's own module, this suite, and a tracked extension-less `data/bin` CLI), so a mis-scoped or empty listing is loud rather than green.

**Round-5 gates.** scripts/test-architecture.mjs #42, rebuilt: the census itself (432 tracked files) + the **scope pin** (a vacuous listing cannot pass) + three controls in a throwaway `git init`/`git add` repo — (i) a tree with **no git metadata SKIPS**, (ii) a **tracked** NUL is found while the `\u0000` escape and a `.zst` fixture are not, (iii) **planted-but-untracked is invisible**: an extension-less gitignored `data/bin/rclone` and an untracked `src/untracked.js` full of NULs never reach the census — *that is the point of the round*, so it is asserted rather than assumed. Verified RED for a real regression (a NUL file `git add`ed into `src/` fails the suite; the identical file unstaged passes), and `npm run build` verified GREEN end to end with the 64 MB binary still planted under `data/bin`. NOTE: `origin/master`'s `scripts/test-writer-sweep.mjs` carries a raw NUL at line 2016 (confirmed by reading `origin/master:` — byte 157114), so the census will be red on the integration target until that literal is spelled as an escape; that file is not on this branch.

**ROUND 6 — THE CENSUS'S OWN GIT RAN IN SOMEONE ELSE'S ENVIRONMENT.** Two findings, both reproduced in throwaway `/tmp` repos before a line was changed.

11. **Round 5's controls ran `git init` / `git add -f` with the inherited process environment, so they could rewrite a REAL repository (major).** Git's entire repository-location layer lives in environment variables — `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_WORK_TREE`, `GIT_INDEX_FILE`, `GIT_COMMON_DIR`, `GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY`, `GIT_ALTERNATE_OBJECT_DIRECTORIES`, `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES`, `GIT_PREFIX`, `GIT_NAMESPACE`, and the config channels `GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS` / `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`+`GIT_CONFIG_KEY_n`/`VALUE_n` (which reach the same place indirectly, via `core.bare` / `core.worktree` / `safe.directory`). A `git -C <tmpdir> …` obeys them all. And this suite does not choose its own process: it runs inside `npm run build`, which runs inside the pre-push **release gate** and the in-app **"Update VibeSpace…"** — i.e. inside hook processes that export exactly those names (measured on git 2.51: a `pre-commit` hook is handed `GIT_INDEX_FILE` and `GIT_PREFIX`; `GIT_DIR` is normal for any tool driving a worktree). Three reproductions: **(a)** `GIT_DIR` at a linked worktree's gitdir ⇒ the control's `git init` REINITIALISED that repository and flipped `core.bare` `false`→`true` **in the shared config**, after which `git status` in the main checkout dies `fatal: this operation must be run in a work tree` — which breaks `scripts/update.sh`'s `git pull --ff-only`, `ci.mjs`'s green marker, and (finding 12) makes the census itself SKIP green forever; *this happened to the production checkout and was repaired by hand*. **(b)** `GIT_INDEX_FILE` at another repo's index ⇒ the REPO census read the FOREIGN listing (432 files → 1), so the suite went **RED describing a tree that is not ours**, while `git add -f` staged the raw-NUL fixture into that repository and replaced its `.gitignore` index entry. **(c)** plain repo + `GIT_DIR` ⇒ the fixture is staged there too. **The fix is ONE sanitized environment**, built above `trackedSource` and passed to every git the block spawns, read side and write side: 25 redirecting names deleted (plus the numbered `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_n`/`VALUE_n` pairs), while `PATH`, `GIT_EXEC_PATH` and the strictly-more-isolating `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM` / `GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM` are deliberately kept (they only *remove* ambient system files). The write side additionally names its target on the command line (positional directory for `init`, `--git-dir`/`--work-tree` for `add`/`config`, `cwd` = the tmp repo) — but that is belt-and-braces only, because **it is not sufficient**: measured, `git --git-dir=A --work-tree=A add` under `GIT_INDEX_FILE=B/.git/index` still writes B's index. The environment is the load-bearing layer.

12. **The SKIP branch counted as a passing assert and asserted a cause it never verified (minor).** It printed "no readable git index (export/tarball)" without checking anything of the sort. A checkout whose `core.bare` was flipped TRUE — exactly what finding 11(a) leaves behind — prints the *same green line*, even though `git ls-files` there answers perfectly; only `rev-parse --show-toplevel` dies, because bare means "no work tree". So the accident both disabled the census and told everyone the tree was a tarball. Now `--show-toplevel` failing falls back to `git -C <base> rev-parse --git-dir`, with ownership = `<base>/.git` is itself a repository entry — so a tmpdir that merely *sits inside* another repo still cannot borrow that repo's index, bare or not — and every skip carries git's own failing command plus its trimmed stderr. Re-verified that the census stays SKIP-with-reason (never red) when git is absent from `PATH`: `git -C <base> rev-parse --show-toplevel: spawn failed: ENOENT; --git-dir: spawn failed: ENOENT`.

**THE ROUND-6 RULES.** (v) **A test that has to run inside someone else's process must NAME the environment it runs its own tools in** — inheriting it hands the caller the choice of which repository/index/config your tool writes, and the release gate is exactly such a caller. (w) **An explicit target on the command line is not isolation**: `--git-dir`/`--work-tree` do not override `GIT_INDEX_FILE`; sanitize the environment *and* name the target, and know which of the two is actually protecting you. (x) **A SKIP must quote the failure, not guess the cause** — a green line that invents its own reason disguises "this gate was switched off" as "there was nothing here to check", and it survives every later reading of the log.

**Round-6 gates.** scripts/test-architecture.mjs #42, controls rebuilt: the **sanitizer pin** (all 25 redirectors plus `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_0`/`VALUE_0` gone from a deliberately hostile environment, `PATH` and `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM` kept); the **finding's own negative control** — two decoy repos, one hostile `GIT_DIR`+`GIT_WORK_TREE`+`GIT_INDEX_FILE`+`GIT_CONFIG_*`: through the sanitizer the decoy's `config`/`index`/`HEAD` are **byte-identical** afterwards *and* the controls are asserted to have really done their work in the throwaway repo (so the assert is not vacuous), while the same write run **raw** is asserted to reach the other decoy (so "unchanged" is the sanitizer, not a control that never ran); the **`core.bare=true` control** (`--show-toplevel` asserted to fail there, and the census asserted to RUN and find the tracked NUL anyway); the **ownership pin** that the `--git-dir` fallback did not weaken (a directory inside another repo borrows nothing, bare or not); and every SKIP asserted to quote `rev-parse`. Verified end to end: `npm run build` green, and green again under `GIT_INDEX_FILE=<throwaway>/.git/index` and under `GIT_DIR=<throwaway worktree gitdir>` with the throwaway repos' whole `.git` trees (64 files) byte-identical before and after and the REPO census still reading **our** 432 files; SKIP-with-reason confirmed with git absent from `PATH`. test-queue-steer 265 + test-codex-p2-wrapper 250 unchanged.

**ROUND 7 — THE NEGATIVE CONTROL WAS THE LAST THING STILL RUNNING IN SOMEONE ELSE'S ENVIRONMENT.** Two findings, both reproduced in throwaway `/tmp` repos first.

13. **Round 6 sanitized every git except the one whose whole job is to be unsanitized (major).** The RAW leg — the negative control that proves the sanitizer matters — built its `git add -f` environment as `{ ...process.env, GIT_DIR, GIT_WORK_TREE, GIT_INDEX_FILE }`: **three** of the 25 names `GIT_REDIRECTORS` enumerates pinned, the other **22** still ambient. A control may differ from the guarded run in exactly the ONE way it is testing; this one differed in 23. Measured on git 2.51, each in its own throwaway repo: with `GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY` — or `GIT_COMMON_DIR` — exported at a **third** repository, that write puts its blobs there (`objects/e1/5da8…`, `objects/fa/7947…`) while `npm run build` stays **green**; and when the same name points at a nonexistent directory, a `0500` directory, or a plain non-repo, `npm run build` goes **RED** on the line *"the guarded assert above is the sanitizer working, not the controls failing to run"* — a message that accuses the sanitizer of a failure caused by a variable no line in the file ever names (`fatal: not a git repository: '<exposed>/.git'` / `insufficient permission for adding an object to repository database`). Round 6's `repoStamp` could not have caught the silent half either: it read `config`/`index`/`HEAD` only, and the object channel touches none of the three. Fix: the raw leg's base is `GIT_ENV` plus exactly the three decoy redirectors it is testing, and `repoStamp` hashes **every file under `.git`** (recursive readdir + sha256 per file).

14. **The census sentence overclaimed its scope (minor).** "no source file carries a NUL byte" was measured over `git ls-files -- src data/bin scripts` — **432 of the repository's 556 tracked files**. The 124 outside included `server.js` (the bootstrap this same suite ratchets to ≤2100 lines), `install.sh`, `run.sh`, `editor-helper.sh`, `deploy/docker/*.sh`, `docs/screenshot-helper.js` and `docs/examples/hello-plugin/server.js` — every one a file a human greps, none of them protected. The roots were never the load-bearing part (**"tracked"** is what puts the 64 MB rclone out of scope), so the pathspec is gone: the census reads the whole index (measured 556 files; 24 skipped by extension — 4 `.png` + 20 `.woff2`, the only genuinely binary tracked files — and **0** offenders), and the scope pin now names `server.js` and `install.sh` beside the three root witnesses. A new binary fixture ⇒ extend `BINARY_EXT` by extension with a comment naming the file; never a directory exclusion (the tracked CLIs in `data/bin` must stay in scope).

**THE ROUND-7 RULES.** (y) **A negative control is a control**: it may differ from the guarded run in exactly the ONE named way it is testing — `{ ...process.env, <3 names> }` is not "the raw environment", it is 22 uncontrolled variables wearing a label, and the harm runs both ways (a silent write into a stranger's repository, or a RED release gate whose message blames the wrong thing). (z) **A tamper-detector must cover the channel the tamper uses**: `config`/`index`/`HEAD` cannot see an object write, so the stamp guarding against `GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY` has to hash the object store too — hash the whole artifact, not the three files you happened to think of. (aa) **A scoped measurement must state its scope in the sentence that reports it**, and when the scope turns out to be an unexamined sample (three directories out of a whole index), widen it rather than reword it.

**Round-7 gates.** scripts/test-architecture.mjs #42, controls rebuilt again: the census is the **whole index** (556 files) and the **scope pin** names `server.js` + `install.sh` beside the three root witnesses; `repoStamp` hashes every `.git` file; the raw leg starts from `GIT_ENV`. Two new asserts, two halves of one control: `GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY` **and** `GIT_COMMON_DIR` are exported into this process at an `ambient-third` repository for the **whole** census block and it must be byte-identical at the end (with the environment asserted to still be hostile, so the assert cannot pass vacuously), while a **sacrificial fourth repo** proves the PRE-FIX base still reaches one — if that leg ever goes green-by-nothing, the ambient environment is not hostile and the first assert means nothing. Verified: `npm run build` green in five legs — plain, and under ambient `GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY` / `GIT_COMMON_DIR` / `GIT_INDEX_FILE` / `GIT_DIR`+`GIT_WORK_TREE` pointed at throwaway repos — with every decoy's whole `.git` tree (21 files) byte-identical before and after and the REPO census still reading **our** 556 files; SKIP-with-reason re-confirmed with git absent from `PATH` (71 asserts, exit 0) and on a `.git`-less export copy. The fix's own negative controls are **patched copies of the real suite** with the anchor hit asserted: reverting the raw base alone turns the whole-block assert RED, and additionally narrowing `repoStamp` back to `config`/`index`/`HEAD` turns the round-7 negative control RED (the leak becomes invisible to both detectors at once). test-architecture 80; test-queue-steer 265 + test-codex-p2-wrapper 250 unchanged.


**Round-3 gates.** scripts/test-codex-p2-wrapper.mjs ⑦ — the REAL wrapper: a refused `turn/start`, a refused `thread/queue/add`, an explicit remove and a Stop sweep each producing exactly one retraction (and none for the message that landed nor for the slash command, which declares itself on the record); the image message's content compared to the `turn/start` params it was sent as; **the server preview and the wrapper's own record compared BYTE FOR BYTE through one client frame** (+ a control where the old preview spelling splits the pair); and a rebuild of the real records against a rollout that also carries the same texts later — 12 bubbles, with a control that strips the declarations and **deletes five real messages**. scripts/test-queue-steer.mjs ⑨(j8)-(j10) — the image twin (+ a code control with the block normalisation patched out, + a data control in the old order, + two different images staying two), the no-commit and retraction scenarios (each with a patched-module control that reproduces the deletion), the not-a-blanket-off-switch cases, `userTwinKeys`/`retractionIdOf` truth tables, the marker stripped from both keys, the peer-collision pair (+ its control), and wiring pins on all three producers.

## DESKTOP-RESUME PAGED A PINNED WINDOW INTO HISTORY (inc-mtq5bpjt-0o0n, after 2.369.42, owner "切换桌面后，新桌面的窗口内容跳到历史消息了")

Three chat windows on the target desktop — all PINNED at the live tail when the desktop was hidden — came back at `scrollTop 0` in old history with the tail trimmed away. The capture is unambiguous about intent: `wheelAgo` 1.8M–4.7M ms on every window, no touch/pointer, no re-attach. Per window the shape was identical: the resume re-measure bounced scrollTop (`1967→0→1976→3297→1950` in 240 ms, `pin=1` throughout), then ~2.9 s later `trimBottom removed 48`, `extendTop:done … anchored:false st 0`, `scroll 1950→0 pin 0`.
  (round-5 verifier note) The gutter hit-test is the MINIMAP-OFF path only: `.chat-minimap-active` sets scrollbar-width:none, so on every conversation with ≥3 turns the native gutter is 0 px and a reader positions via the minimap, whose callback stamps `_noteUserNav('minimap')` — that is the production carrier of "a positioning act during the resume settle"; the gutter branch covers short conversations and minimap-off windows.

The mechanism was NOT a missing suspend: 2.369.1 suspends a desktop-hidden ChatView and 2.369.3 gives it `content-visibility:hidden`, and both held. The hole was the **resume TRANSITION** plus the one upward-paging entry point that the whole 2.301→2.339 gate campaign never touched. The scroll handler's branch needs `!pinned && userRecentUp`; the wheel branch needs a wheel; neither fired. The gap sentinel's `IntersectionObserver` (`_observeHistoryGap`, `rootMargin: '300px 0px 0px 0px'`) did: it is a PURELY GEOMETRIC trigger, and `_loadEarlierGap`'s tail-mode branch — `if (!this._teleported && this._windowStart > 0) { await this._extendTop(); return; }` — had **no intent gate, no pin gate, no settle gate and no suspend gate at all**. `_showWin` cleared content-visibility, the subtree re-measured, scrollTop read 0 while still pinned, the sentinel entered the 300px margin, and 50 messages were paged in with zero user intent. `_extendTop`'s completion then did the damage: `trimBottom` dropped the live tail (`windowEnd < total`), the element anchor — measured under transitional geometry — failed (`anchored:false`) and the delta fallback clamped scrollTop to 0. `setSuspended(false)`'s rAF re-tail had already run, BEFORE the fetch resolved, so it could not undo any of it. Only sessions >34 MB are affected, because the sentinel only exists when the server reports a gap (all three transcripts were 68–868 MB).

FIX, five parts, all structural:
1. **ONE gate predicate, `ChatView._autoPagingBlocked()`**, returning a REASON (`disposed / suspended / resume-settle / pinned / settling / no-input / input-resize`) — exactly the laws the scroll handler already obeyed, in one place instead of re-invented per entry point. `_loadEarlierGap` applies it for every AUTOMATIC caller (the observer, and the scroll/wheel-driven `_maybeSeekEarlier`) BEFORE the tail-mode branch, and traces `gapSkip {why, via}` so the tracer shows the refusal. An explicit retry CLICK passes `{ auto: false }` and bypasses it — a click is intent.
2. **A resume settle**, `_resumeSettleUntil` (1200 ms, sized from the capture's +366…+602 ms bounce), armed by `setSuspended(false)`: the scroll handler no-ops (BEFORE it touches the pin — transitional geometry must not unpin either), the observer path no-ops, the loadHistory auto-fill DEFERS (bounded re-arms) instead of deciding, and the pinned re-tail is re-asserted once when the settle expires. Real user input (wheel/touch/pointer/key) clears the window immediately — the settle suppresses input-LESS displacement only, never a reader.
3. **INVARIANT: a pinned view never loses its tail.** `_extendTop` does not `trimBottom` while `_pinned` (that trim is what converts an accidental page-up into permanent damage) and re-asserts the bottom after the prepend; both are traced (`trimSkipPinned` / `pinnedRetail`). The DOM stays bounded because the live-append path trims the TOP while pinned and the next genuine (unpinned) page-up trims normally.
4. **The settle covers the re-tail, and the re-tail asserts off a SNAPSHOT** (the verifier's minor on this fix, reproduced before fixing). As first written the settle expired at `RESUME_SETTLE_MS` while the pinned re-tail ran 40 ms later, and the re-tail refused once the live `_pinned` flag was gone — so an input-LESS displacement landing in that 40 ms gap (repro: `scrollTop = 0` injected at resume+1210 ms) reached the scroll handler, unpinned the window, and the re-tail then declined to bring it back: `fromBottom 1536`, permanently. Two changes close it: `_resumeSettleUntil = now + RESUME_SETTLE_MS + RESUME_RETAIL_SLACK_MS` (the window now ENDS at the re-tail, so nothing can decide in between), and `setSuspended(true)` snapshots the pin into `_pinnedAtSuspend` — the last honest reading, taken before the geometry starts lying — which the re-tail asserts on (`!_pinned && !_pinnedAtSuspend`) and then clears. A window that was pinned when it was hidden returns to the tail regardless of any transitional unpin on the way back, while real input runs `_endResumeSettle()` (settle AND snapshot), so a reader who scrolls away during the settle is never yanked to the bottom.
5. Every pre-existing gate stays byte-identical on the non-resume paths (2.307/2.308/2.338/2.339 are pinned by test-chat-trim-guard).

**INVARIANTS: (a) every AUTOMATIC paging trigger — including observer callbacks, which are pure geometry and fire with no event to inspect — goes through the same intent gate; an entry point that pages without asking "was this the user?" is the bug, whatever the mechanism displaced scrollTop. (b) When a state has a "hidden" guard, the TRANSITION out of it is a separate, unguarded state — guard the re-measure window explicitly, and let real input, not a timer alone, end it. (c) A pinned view is AT the live tail by definition: nothing automatic may page it up, and nothing may trim its tail. (d) The guard belongs on the path that is still alive in the failure state — here `_loadEarlierGap` itself, not its call sites. (e) A settle window and the repair it protects must not have a gap between them, and the repair asserts on state captured BEFORE the corrupting transition (a snapshot), never on the live flag the transition is free to corrupt.** **ROUND 2 (two more, both reproduced in headless chrome on the same 36 MB window): (f) A repair that runs on a TIMER must ask whether the user has since acted — only the four message-list listeners ended the settle, so a reader who navigated through a surface that is NOT the list (the minimap lives on the container, the floating run bar on `this._container`, `jumpToIndex` and a search reveal touch neither) was YANKED back to the live tail 2.2 s after their jump; every off-list positioning surface now stamps `_noteUserNav` and the repair compares nav-vs-resume (the chat-view-seek `userScrolled` idiom generalised: `_lastNavAt`, `_lastUserScrollAt`, `_lastJumpAt`, `_search._lastRevealAt`). (g) A time-boxed guard is a CLIFF, and the failure walks right off it — an input-less displacement at resume+1400 ms unpinned and stranded the window 3/3 (the +1240/+1280 probes had only survived because `_forceScrollToBottom`'s 10-frame chain happened to cover them). The fix is the same shape as the paging gates: gate the STATE CHANGE (the unpin) on positive evidence for the whole horizon, not just the transition on a timer — plus a bounded re-assert series. Corollary (h): a no-op guard must sit below the pure READOUTS it does not own — the settle `return` above `_updateRunBar` froze the floating run bar for 1.24 s of every desktop switch.** **ROUND 3 (reproduced with TRUSTED CDP input — `Input.dispatchMouseEvent`, not a synthetic `dispatchEvent`): (i) A CLICK IS NOT A POSITIONING ACT. Rounds 1–2 gave every message-list listener the same effect — `_endResumeSettle()`, which zeroes the settle window AND drops `_pinnedAtSuspend` AND clears the bounded re-tail series — so a plain left-click or tap anywhere in the message list during the 1.24 s settle disarmed the entire repair, and the resume's OWN input-less displacement, arriving a beat later behind the click, reproduced the incident (measured: click at +300 ms, `scrollTop = 0` at +1400 ms → unpinned, `fromBottom 1883`, stranded). The effects are now split by GRADE. **INPUT** (`_noteUserInput`: a pointerdown, a non-navigation key) stamps `_lastUserScrollAt` — the evidence the paging gates' `no-input` rule wants — and may end the settle WINDOW, nothing more. **POSITIONING** (`_notePositioning`: wheel, touchmove, the navigation keys PageUp/PageDown/Home/End/Arrow*/Space, plus `_noteUserNav`'s six off-list surfaces) is the only grade that drops the pin snapshot and cancels the re-tail series, and it is the only stamp `_navigatedSince` reads (`_lastPositionAt`, never `_lastUserScrollAt`). THE SCROLLBAR DRAG is the one positioning act with no event of its own, so it is recognised by a SIGNATURE (`_pointerDragScroll`, stamped in the scroll handler ABOVE the settle return so the drag can end the very settle it starts inside) — round 3 wrote that signature in TIME (a scroll within 400 ms of a pointerdown that moved the view), which round 4 below replaces with the PLACE the press landed. Generalised: **a guard's exit condition is the act that makes the guard WRONG, not any evidence that a human is present** — the resume repair is wrong only once the reader has MOVED the view, so only moving may cancel it. KNOWN RESIDUAL (round-3 MINOR, backlog B-8e5e, deliberately not fixed here): a real wheel-up at resume+1400 ms pages as it should but is re-PINNED about 1/3 of the time, because under collapsed geometry the scroll handler makes no boundary decision at all (`collapsedGeomSkip`) and `_extendTop`'s own pinned-tail invariant re-asserts the bottom — that final pin state belongs to the 2.301.0 guard, not to this fix, and asserting it here would pin someone else's mechanism.** **ROUND 4 (the round-3 SIGNATURE's own two MAJORs, both reproduced with trusted CDP input): (j) A SIGNATURE MADE OF TIME CANNOT TELL THE READER FROM THE ARTIFACT. "A scroll within 400 ms of a pointerdown that moved the view" is satisfied by the very displacement this fix exists to suppress — the incident's re-measure bounces run +366…+602 ms after the switch — so a plain CONTENT click landing anywhere near one was promoted to `scrollbar-drag`, ran `_endResumeSettle()` (window + snapshot + series) and stranded the window BEHIND the click: round 3's own repair, undone by round 3's own signature (its leg passed only because it put the click at +300 ms and the displacement at +1400 ms, outside the window; at +450 ms the fixed build ends pinned at the tail and the restored time-window control traces `userPos/scrollbar-drag → unpin → extendTop:done`, `fromBottom 1883`). (k) …and the same window LOST real readers: a drag whose first move comes more than 400 ms after the press — press, look, then drag, a scrollbar reader's normal tempo — was never positioning at all, so the bounded re-tail series pulled them back to the live tail: a NEW harm versus master, measured (`holdDrag` ends at `fromBottom 1440`; with the hit-test neutered the same drag ends `fromBottom 0`, re-pinned). THE FIX — key the drag on WHERE THE PRESS LANDED, not on when a scroll follows: `_pointerOnScrollbar(e)` hit-tests the press against the list's scrollbar GUTTER (`offsetWidth - clientWidth` minus the borders — on the right in LTR, on the LEFT in RTL, plus the bottom strip for a horizontal scrollbar; coordinates converted viewport→LAYOUT px first, because the uiScale body `zoom` scales `getBoundingClientRect`/`clientX` but not `clientWidth` — the 2.369.5 VNC-pointer class), the pointerdown stores the verdict in `_pointerDownOnScrollbar`, WINDOW-scoped `pointerup`/`pointercancel` listeners bound to `winInfo._listenerCtl.signal` clear it (a drag routinely releases outside the list; `dispose()` also removes them for views whose window outlives them), and `_pointerDragScroll` is simply "that flag is set AND the view moved more than `POINTER_DRAG_PX`". `POINTER_DRAG_MS` is deleted. A click in the content area therefore never becomes positioning however close in time a displacement lands, and a press-and-hold-then-drag is positioning for the whole press. With the semantic minimap on, the native scrollbar has zero width (`scrollbar-width: none`) so nothing can land in the gutter — correct, because there the reader drags the MINIMAP, which is already `_noteUserNav('minimap')`. Generalised: **identify an act by what DEFINES it (a scrollbar drag is a press on the scrollbar), never by a temporal correlation with its effects — a time window admits every artifact that happens to land inside it and excludes every human slower than it.** Pins: scripts/test-chat-trim-guard.mjs (112, incl. a DOM-free unit over the extracted decision table, a FUNCTIONAL one that runs `setSuspended` hide→show against fake geometry (a transitional unpin still re-tails, a real reader is left alone), and round 4's units over the shipped `_pointerOnScrollbar` — content click false / gutter press true / no-scrollbar list false / RTL gutter on the left / border ≠ scrollbar / a uiScale-zoomed rect that the un-converted comparison would call a gutter press) + scripts/test-desktop-resume-paging.mjs (35) — a headless-chrome A/B on a real >34 MB transcript across a real desktop switch, whose NEGATIVE CONTROL rebuilds the bundle with the gates patched out and must show `extendTop:done` on the resume, through an input-less `scrollTop→0` sentinel probe, and must strand the window on the resume+1210 ms gap injection that the fixed build ends pinned at the tail; round 2 adds a nav-during-settle leg (a jump at +400 ms must STAY where the reader put it), a 1240/1400/1900/2400 ms input-less displacement sweep that must all end pinned at the tail, a real wheel-up at +1400 ms that must still page, and a PER-MECHANISM negative control for each — neutered on the instance in the same run, because the source-level control rebuilds with `RESUME_SETTLE_MS = 0` and cannot host a timing-dependent repro. Round 3 adds three TRUSTED-input legs at resume+300 ms on the same window — a real left-click at the list centre (the +1400 ms displacement behind it must still end pinned at the tail; control: give the click the old full `_endResumeSettle()` and it strands again), a real `mouseWheel` up (it must unpin IMMEDIATELY and page, and `unpinSkipResume` must never fire against a reader), and a real native-scrollbar drag (`mousePressed` on the 15 px scrollbar + `mouseMoved`, with `.chat-minimap-active` removed first because the semantic minimap hides the native scrollbar — it must trace `userPos/scrollbar-drag` and leave the reader where they dragged; control: neuter `_pointerDragScroll` and the same drag is pulled back to the tail, st 0 → 624). Round 4 adds two more on the same window, each with its own instance-level control: a click at +300 ms with the displacement at **+450 ms** (fixed: pinned at the tail; control: restore the pre-fix time-window signature on the instance — it strands the window), and a gutter press HELD 900 ms before the first move (fixed: `userPos/scrollbar-drag`, the reader stays at `fromBottom 1440`; control: make `_pointerOnScrollbar` answer false — the same drag is yanked back to `fromBottom 0`), plus trusted-input asserts that a content press is not a gutter press and that the flag is cleared on release. **ROUND 5 (B-9702, the residue the round-2 leg left behind — and the first round MEASURED before it was explained): (l) OUR OWN SCROLL CHAIN IS AN AUTOMATIC WRITER TOO.** Round 2 taught the repair to cancel its re-tail TIMERS the moment the reader positions the view, and left `_forceScrollToBottom`'s 10-frame chain running — the other automatic writer, and the very one round 2's own note blamed for the +1240/+1280 probes surviving by accident. Measured, not assumed: the round-2 leg "a REAL wheel-up at resume+1400 ms still pages" was run 20× on that build and the reader was dragged back to the live tail **5 times (25 %)**, with one trace shape every time — `userPos/wheel` at +1399 ms, then `repin st:1760 sh:2468 ch:708 structAge:1404 fsb:-1 posAgo:3` at +1402 ms, then `trimSkipPinned` → `pinnedRetail` → `extendTop:done`. Read: the +1240 ms rung's chain wrote `scrollTop = scrollHeight` **3 ms AFTER the reader's own scroll**, the pin re-engaged off OUR write, and the pinned-tail invariant (part 3 above) then faithfully re-asserted the bottom — the reader's page-up silently undone. The backlog's hypothesis, "the collapsed-geometry window re-pins", was REFUTED by the same capture: `sh - ch` was 2.5 viewports and `collapsedGeomSkip` never fired there. **FIX: (l) the chain carries an EPOCH and `_cancelForcedScroll(via)` bumps it** from `_notePositioning` / `_noteUserNav` (never from `_noteUserInput` — a click still moves nothing), so every frame already queued finds itself orphaned instead of writing once more; a later `_forceScrollToBottom` starts a NEW epoch, so a cancel can never kill auto-follow (its own unit control). **(m) A MUTE MUST OUTLIVE THE WRITE IT MUTES**: `_programmaticScroll` was cleared in the same frame as the chain's final write, and a scroll event is delivered AFTER the callback that wrote it — which is exactly why the capture reads `fsb:-1` at the re-pin, i.e. the guard whose whole job is to reject our own displacement had already stood down for it. The release now runs one frame later, guarded on `_fsbActive` so a chain restarted in that gap keeps its mute. Same 20-run measurement after the fix: **0/20**, `repin` never appears, and 9 of 20 legs show `fsbCancel … frames:9` — the chain caught on its last frame, which is precisely how narrow the race was. **(n) An assert that declines to name the USER-VISIBLE outcome is where a residue hides**: the round-2 leg checked only "something paged" and "the unpin gate didn't fire", explicitly reasoning that the final pin state "is not this gate's to decide" — so it passed on every run where the reader lost their page-up. It now asserts the reader's outcome on a settled snapshot (unpinned, away from the tail, `windowStart` moved up, no `repin`), plus a DETERMINISTIC form of the race — a chain armed by hand immediately before the wheel — whose per-mechanism control neuters `_cancelForcedScroll` on the instance and must drag the reader back to `fromBottom <= 8`. Generalised: **every automatic writer of scrollTop must lose to a positioning act (a timer was only one of them), and a guard against our own writes must outlive the last of those writes.**

## A MEMO KEY THAT NAMED NEITHER THE PRICES NOR THE LEDGER (2.369.43, review batch — the 2.369.36 interval memo's two holes)
The inc-mtox23xw fix memoized `costBetweenMulti` on `ids|from|to|events-at-or-before-to`. Both remaining holes were reproduced before fixing. ① PRICING: the key names the LEDGER but not the PRICE TABLE, and `_cost` reads `this._pricing` live — so after a pricing edit (a tier rate or a per-account discount, POST /api/usage-stats/pricing or a config import through persistence.js) every already-memoized historical interval kept returning pre-edit dollars forever, poisoning the estimator's learned rates and every anchor's `costSince` while the Usage window (which does not go through the memo) showed the new numbers. Repro: a 10× opus tier edit and a 50% account discount each left the total frozen at $3.50 against a truth of $35.00 / $17.50. FIX: `UsageHistory.pricingToken()` = a sha1 of {tiers, accounts}, recomputed only when `_pricing` is REPLACED (the same object-identity check `_tier` uses), appended to the key; a ledger object that cannot version its prices is NOT memoized at all. ② EVICTION: `learnRates` walks one identity's anchor pairs in the SAME order on every recompute — a cyclic reference string, the one access pattern where FIFO (and LRU) go off a CLIFF instead of degrading. Measured on the shipped code: a 20000-key working set hits 100%, 21000 hits **0.0%** — a 5% overshoot destroys 100% of the memo and the per-pair ledger walk of inc-mtox23xw comes straight back. Real anchor files are append-only forever (production: 5124 qualifying pairs over 28 days across 8 identities, ~180 new/day, biggest identity 1126), so the 20000 cap is reached in months, not never. FIX: a RANDOM victim (O(1) via a parallel key array) — the same input now holds 53% at 1.3× the cap. Also: the memo moved from a module global to a WeakMap per UsageHistory, so two ledgers can never read each other's costs through a key that names neither. **INVARIANTS: a memo over computed values keys on every INPUT of the computation, not just the data it walks (a rate table, a discount, a rounding rule are inputs); if an input cannot be versioned, do not memoize; an eviction policy is chosen against the ACCESS PATTERN — recency policies are worst-case for repeated full scans, and "cap exceeded" must degrade, not cliff; a cache keyed by values that name no instance belongs to the instance (WeakMap), never to the module.** Pins: scripts/test-usage-ledger-perf.mjs (21) — price-edit/discount/clear parity vs brute force, hit-path parity, cross-ledger isolation, and a hit-rate floor at 1.3× cap (both negative-controlled: dropping the token reddens 4, true FIFO reddens the hit-rate assert at exactly 0.0%).

## THE SHORT-VIEW RESCUE THAT COULD NEVER FIRE (2.369.43, review batch — dead since 2.369.36)
`loadHistory`'s post-attach auto-fill exists because a view whose content does not fill the viewport has NO scrollable range: the scroll handler never runs, and on touch there is no wheel-up path either, so a window sits on two run headers with thousands of unreachable messages above. The 2.369.36 hardening (a re-attach rebuild read sh<=ch on a TALL pinned window while content-visibility heights were unresolved, fired `_extendTop`, and a late slab + trimBottom cost the live tail) added `rendered < 30` to the gate. That predicate is UNSATISFIABLE: every attach path ships `tail(50)` (ws-handler's `_normalizer.tail(50)`, `transcripts.page`), `_windowStart > 0` holds EXACTLY when those 50 arrived, and the semantic fold HIDES members (`.chat-run-collapsed`, display:none) instead of removing them — so `rendered` is ~50 whenever the gate is armed. Measured over 33 real production transcripts with total>50: 50 of 50 tail messages render a `.chat-msg` child, min 50, none below 30. The rescue was dead code for everyone, and the fold-dominated case (inc-mtajy6wr's cousin: 50 cards collapsing to ~240px in a 700px viewport) had no way out on touch. RE-DERIVED from the intent instead of the symptom: the indeterminate geometry is TRANSIENT — the same premise `collapsedGeomSkip` runs on ("heights resolve within ~1.5s") — so CORROBORATE the reading: `_shortViewNeedsFill` is a named predicate over the injected list geometry, and `_scheduleAttachFill` measures at 700ms and again 900ms later, extending only if both readings agree AND `_lastStructuralAt` did not move in between (any paging/trim/desktop-resume hands the decision back to the machinery that owns it). The card count survives only as the HARM bound (`rendered + 50 <= 150`): the rescue adds ONE page and never at a size where a trim could eat the live tail. Since inc-mtq5bpjt-0o0n the whole schedule also DEFERS past the desktop-resume settle (bounded re-arms, and the second reading re-checks it) — a resume is the same class of artifact, and the resume path owns the tail while it is running. **INVARIANTS: a guard added under incident pressure must be shown to be SATISFIABLE on the path it guards — a condition that is always false is indistinguishable from deleting the feature, and nothing reports it; when the honest signal is transient noise, corroborate it across the settle window rather than proxying it with an unrelated quantity; DOM-free client predicates get functional tests (chat-view.js imports in plain node) — the pre-fix gate looked plausible in review and was arithmetically impossible in production.** Pins: scripts/test-chat-trim-guard.mjs (61) — the fold-dominated case fires, the tall/no-history/suspended/disposed/teleported/over-cap cases do not, and the two-reading corroboration is driven end to end (artifact ⇒ no extend, genuine ⇒ exactly one).
## CODEX .ZST / DISCOVERY / REMOTE-FETCH BATCH (2.369.x, review findings — four ways a codex thread went missing or came back wrong)

**① A RATIO GUESS DECIDED WHETHER A THREAD EXISTED.** `readHeadText` (discovery-facts) read a bounded COMPRESSED prefix and one-shot decompressed it under `maxOutputLength: max(plainBytes*4, 1MiB)`; anything compressing better than ~4× threw EZSTBIG, the `catch { return ''; }` turned that into an EMPTY head, `extractCodexThreadMeta` produced `threadId: ''`, and the thread VANISHED from /api/sessions — then the empty meta was cached by mtime, so for a stopped thread it stayed gone until a restart. Measured on the dev box: compressing the 40 real `~/.codex` rollouts at the DEFAULT zstd level, 7 reproduced it (ratios 4.0–6.0, e.g. plain 1,115,325 → comp 195,087). Fix: `zstdDecompressHead` BISECTS over compressed prefix sizes under a fixed output cap (a truncated prefix decompresses to the plain text it covers — verified on this runtime) until ≥ plainBytes of plain text exist; an unreadable head now THROWS a coded error (`EZSTUNSUPPORTED`/`EZSTHEAD`; zero plain bytes out of non-empty compressed input is a failure, not emptiness) so the existing `extractFailed` rule refuses to cache it, and `_noteExtractFailure` logs the reason verbatim once per file per 10 min. **Invariants: a bounded reader must be bounded by CONSTRUCTION (bisect/stream), never by a guessed compression ratio; "unreadable" and "empty" are different answers and only the second may be cached; a degrade path that hides a thread must say so in the log.**

**② A CACHE GUARD MEASURED THE WRONG CLOCK.** The codex rollout walk's per-directory listing cache (`codex-session-store._listDirCached`) skipped the readdir when the dir mtime was unchanged AND `now - mtime > 2s` — the coarse-timestamp guard evaluated at LOOKUP time. Settledness is a property of the CAPTURE: a listing taken while the mtime tick was still open can miss a sibling created in the same second, and that sibling never bumps the mtime again, so once the clock passed mtime+2s the stale listing was trusted forever (the poll is 5s — the window is always over by the next lookup) and the second rollout never appeared in the session list. Fix: record `settled` at fill time; an unsettled capture is re-read on the next lookup until it is taken settled (the readdir-per-poll win is untouched for settled captures). **Invariant: a freshness/settle predicate is evaluated and STORED when the data is captured — evaluating it at read time answers a question about the reader, not about the data.**

**③ "PLAIN WINS" WAS A COMMENT, NOT A RULE.** `interpretDiscoveryLines` de-duplicated a thread's `.jsonl` and `.jsonl.zst` twins by keeping the FIRST entry, commented "plain wins — first in mtime order". Both producers sort newest-first (hosts.js `sort -rn`, agentd.js `b.mtimeMs - a.mtimeMs`) and compression happens AFTER the last write, so the compressed twin was always first — and the ssh scanner can only read a compressed head where the host has `zstd(1)`, so the remote card silently lost the cwd/name its plain twin's HC/NC lines carried. Fix: group by thread id, prefer the plain twin's facts EXPLICITLY, MERGE what each twin has (either fills a gap), mtime = the newer of the pair, CO on either twin marks the one thread running. **Invariant: when two files describe one entity, the preference is expressed in the predicate, never inherited from an upstream sort; a dedup that drops facts must merge them instead.**

**④ ONE CACHE SLOT, MANY REMOTE FILES.** `hosts._fetchRemoteByFind` keys `data/remote-jsonl/<host>/codex/<tid>.jsonl` by conversation id, but codex's `remoteFind` predicate matches the plain rollout OR its `.zst` twin, so `find … | head -1` could resolve a DIFFERENT file across polls (the host compresses a finished rollout).
